# Daily Summary: 2025-03-13

### Reasoning and Sampling-Augmented MCQ Difficulty Prediction via LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08551v1)
- **Authors**: Wanyong Feng, Peter Tran, Stephen Sireci, Andrew Lan
- **Abstract**: The difficulty of multiple-choice questions (MCQs) is a crucial factor for educational assessments. Predicting MCQ difficulty is challenging since it requires understanding both the complexity of reaching the correct option and the plausibility of distractors, i.e., incorrect options. In this paper, we propose a novel, two-stage method to predict the difficulty of MCQs. First, to better estimate the complexity of each MCQ, we use large language models (LLMs) to augment the reasoning steps required to reach each option. We use not just the MCQ itself but also these reasoning steps as input to predict the difficulty. Second, to capture the plausibility of distractors, we sample knowledge levels from a distribution to account for variation among students responding to the MCQ. This setup, inspired by item response theory (IRT), enable us to estimate the likelihood of students selecting each (both correct and incorrect) option. We align these predictions with their ground truth values, using a Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence-based regularization objective, and use estimated likelihoods to predict MCQ difficulty. We evaluate our method on two real-world \emph{math} MCQ and response datasets with ground truth difficulty values estimated using IRT. Experimental results show that our method outperforms all baselines, up to a 28.3\% reduction in mean squared error and a 34.6\% improvement in the coefficient of determination. We also qualitatively discuss how our novel method results in higher accuracy in predicting MCQ difficulty.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper "Reasoning and Sampling-Augmented MCQ Difficulty Prediction via LLMs" addresses the challenge of predicting the difficulty of multiple-choice questions (MCQs), which is essential for educational assessments. The authors propose a novel two-stage method to enhance difficulty prediction. The first stage leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate reasoning steps for each MCQ to better estimate question complexity. The second stage incorporates sampling techniques to model the varying knowledge levels of students, inspired by item response theory (IRT), to evaluate the plausibility of both correct and distractor options. Predictions are refined using a Kullback-Leibler divergence-based regularization objective. The method demonstrates superior performance over existing approaches on two math MCQ datasets, achieving significant reductions in mean squared error and improvements in prediction accuracy, thus highlighting its effectiveness in predicting MCQ difficulty. ### Critical Evaluation: **Novelty**: The paper introduces a two-stage methodology that combines reasoning augmentation with a sampling approach to assess both question complexity and distractor plausibility. The integration of reasoning steps generated by LLMs is a fresh angle that taps into advancements in AI-driven educational assessment, which is relatively unexplored in the context of MCQ difficulty prediction. **Significance**: The implications of improving MCQ difficulty assessments are substantial for educational stakeholders, as accurate difficulty prediction can lead to tailored assessments and better learning outcomes. The approach developed could inspire subsequent research on adaptive learning systems and leave a mark on how educators design and evaluate assessments. **Strengths**:  - The use of large language models (LLMs) and item response theory (IRT) reflects a sophisticated blend of recent advancements in AI with established psychological models in education. - The empirical results demonstrate a notable improvement over baseline models, supporting the efficacy of the methodology. - The authors provide a quantitative evaluation that strengthens the paper's contributions. **Weaknesses**: - The experimental evaluation is limited to math MCQs; applying the method to a broader range of subjects could determine its generalizability. - The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of potential biases that might arise from using LLMs for reasoning step generation. - There’s relatively little exploration of the implications of the findings or potential constraints in real-world educational settings. Overall, while the methodology shows promise and marks a significant step for MCQ difficulty analysis, its impact could be enhanced by further validation across diverse contexts and more thorough explorations of its broader implications. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper's notable innovation and potential effect on educational assessment practices, balanced against some limitations regarding scope and depth in its analysis of broader implications.
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### DeepReview: Improving LLM-based Paper Review with Human-like Deep Thinking Process
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08569v1)
- **Authors**: Minjun Zhu, Yixuan Weng, Linyi Yang, Yue Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly utilized in scientific research assessment, particularly in automated paper review. However, existing LLM-based review systems face significant challenges, including limited domain expertise, hallucinated reasoning, and a lack of structured evaluation. To address these limitations, we introduce DeepReview, a multi-stage framework designed to emulate expert reviewers by incorporating structured analysis, literature retrieval, and evidence-based argumentation. Using DeepReview-13K, a curated dataset with structured annotations, we train DeepReviewer-14B, which outperforms CycleReviewer-70B with fewer tokens. In its best mode, DeepReviewer-14B achieves win rates of 88.21\% and 80.20\% against GPT-o1 and DeepSeek-R1 in evaluations. Our work sets a new benchmark for LLM-based paper review, with all resources publicly available. The code, model, dataset and demo have be released in http://ai-researcher.net.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "DeepReview: Improving LLM-based Paper Review with Human-like Deep Thinking Process" presents a novel framework called DeepReview designed to enhance automated paper reviews using Large Language Models (LLMs). The framework addresses common issues faced by existing LLM review systems, such as limited domain expertise, hallucinated reasoning, and the absence of structured evaluation. DeepReview operates in multiple stages and incorporates components such as structured analysis, literature retrieval, and evidence-based argumentation. The authors utilize a dataset known as DeepReview-13K for training their model, DeepReviewer-14B, which demonstrates superior performance compared to CycleReviewer-70B, requiring fewer tokens to achieve higher effectiveness. DeepReviewer-14B notably achieves impressive win rates of 88.21% and 80.20% against competitive models, setting new benchmarks in this field. Importantly, the authors have made all resources related to their work publicly accessible, including code, model, dataset, and demo. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation **Novelty**: The paper presents a multi-stage framework that improves upon existing LLM review systems through a structured approach, which appears to be novel compared to the often more linear methodologies of previous models. The incorporation of literature retrieval and an evidence-based argumentation framework is a significant advancement in the capability of LLMs to perform academic reviews. **Significance**: The impact of this research is substantial, as effective automated review processes could revolutionize academic publishing and peer review systems. By demonstrating significant improvements over previous models and providing benchmarks, the authors contribute to the ongoing quest for more reliable and efficient scientific evaluation methods through AI. **Strengths**: 1. **Robust Dataset**: The creation of DeepReview-13K with structured annotations supports reproducibility and further research. 2. **Empirical Validation**: The reported win rates against earlier models provide a solid empirical basis for the claims of effectiveness and superiority of DeepReviewer-14B. 3. **Open-Source Commitment**: The authors' decision to release resources publicly promotes transparency and enables other researchers to build upon their work. **Weaknesses**: 1. **Limited Domain Expertise**: While the paper addresses this issue, the extent to which DeepReviewer-14B can handle highly specialized topics is not fully explored and may still be a concern for practical applications. 2. **Token Efficiency**: Although the model requires fewer tokens, the implications of this on overall interpretability and depth of review may need further analysis, particularly for complex papers. **Potential Influence**: The constructs and findings from this paper could influence both the development of future LLMs for academic reviews and the standards applied in automated paper assessments. It pushes the envelope of what LLMs can achieve in terms of emulating expert reviewers. Given the considerable advancements presented, the rigor of the empirical results, and the potential for broad impact in both AI and academic processes, I believe the paper has substantial significance in the field. **Score: 8**  This score reflects strong contributions to the field, while acknowledging that reservations remain regarding the model’s application in highly specialized contexts and its ability to handle the complexities of nuanced academic discourse without further augmentation.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Modular Customization of Diffusion Models via Blockwise-Parameterized Low-Rank Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08575v1)
- **Authors**: Mingkang Zhu, Xi Chen, Zhongdao Wang, Bei Yu, Hengshuang Zhao, Jiaya Jia
- **Abstract**: Recent diffusion model customization has shown impressive results in incorporating subject or style concepts with a handful of images. However, the modular composition of multiple concepts into a customized model, aimed to efficiently merge decentralized-trained concepts without influencing their identities, remains unresolved. Modular customization is essential for applications like concept stylization and multi-concept customization using concepts trained by different users. Existing post-training methods are only confined to a fixed set of concepts, and any different combinations require a new round of retraining. In contrast, instant merging methods often cause identity loss and interference of individual merged concepts and are usually limited to a small number of concepts. To address these issues, we propose BlockLoRA, an instant merging method designed to efficiently combine multiple concepts while accurately preserving individual concepts' identity. With a careful analysis of the underlying reason for interference, we develop the Randomized Output Erasure technique to minimize the interference of different customized models. Additionally, Blockwise LoRA Parameterization is proposed to reduce the identity loss during instant model merging. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of BlockLoRA, which can instantly merge 15 concepts of people, subjects, scenes, and styles with high fidelity.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces BlockLoRA, a novel method for the modular customization of diffusion models that allows for the efficient merging of multiple concepts while preserving their individual identities. Traditional approaches either require retraining for new combinations of concepts or risk identity loss when merging. BlockLoRA addresses these limitations through two key innovations: the Randomized Output Erasure technique, which mitigates the interference between merged concepts, and Blockwise LoRA Parameterization, which reduces identity loss during the merging process. The method has been validated through extensive experiments, demonstrating its effectiveness in merging up to 15 distinct concepts—including people, subjects, scenes, and styles—while maintaining high fidelity. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of this paper lies in its approach to modular customization of diffusion models, which has significant practical implications for personalization and stylization tasks in machine learning. The use of BlockLoRA to seamlessly combine multiple concepts while mitigating the downsides of traditional methods marks a meaningful advancement in the field. The introduction of the Randomized Output Erasure technique provides an innovative solution to the challenge of interference—an issue that has not been sufficiently addressed in prior work.  However, the paper does have certain weaknesses. While the experimental results are promising, it would be beneficial for the authors to provide a more comprehensive analysis of the limitations or edge cases where BlockLoRA may fail. Moreover, while combining 15 concepts is an impressive feat, the scalability of the technique or its performance in less-controlled environments could be discussed further.  In terms of significance within the field, BlockLoRA offers a more flexible and less intrusive mechanism for concept merging compared to existing methods, which can inspire new research directions and applications in the creative domains of AI, such as virtual art or personalized media generation.  Considering these strengths and weaknesses, I assign this paper a score of **8**. This reflects the groundbreaking nature of the proposed methodologies and their potential utility, tempered by the need for additional validation in diverse scenarios and clearer limitations. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### RAG-Adapter: A Plug-and-Play RAG-enhanced Framework for Long Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08576v1)
- **Authors**: Xichen Tan, Yunfan Ye, Yuanjing Luo, Qian Wan, Fang Liu, Zhiping Cai
- **Abstract**: Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) capable of video understanding are advancing rapidly. To effectively assess their video comprehension capabilities, long video understanding benchmarks, such as Video-MME and MLVU, are proposed. However, these benchmarks directly use uniform frame sampling for testing, which results in significant information loss and affects the accuracy of the evaluations in reflecting the true abilities of MLLMs. To address this, we propose RAG-Adapter, a plug-and-play framework that reduces information loss during testing by sampling frames most relevant to the given question. Additionally, we introduce a Grouped-supervised Contrastive Learning (GCL) method to further enhance sampling effectiveness of RAG-Adapter through fine-tuning on our constructed MMAT dataset. Finally, we test numerous baseline MLLMs on various video understanding benchmarks, finding that RAG-Adapter sampling consistently outperforms uniform sampling (e.g., Accuracy of GPT-4o increases by 9.3 percent on Video-MME), providing a more accurate testing method for long video benchmarks.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper presents RAG-Adapter, a novel framework aimed at enhancing the video understanding capabilities of Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Recognizing the limitations of existing long video understanding benchmarks—such as Video-MME and MLVU—that rely on uniform frame sampling, the authors introduce a new sampling method that targets frames most relevant to specific questions to reduce information loss. Additionally, a Grouped-supervised Contrastive Learning (GCL) technique is proposed to augment frame sampling effectiveness through fine-tuning with a new dataset, MMAT. The RAG-Adapter is evaluated against various MLLM baselines and demonstrates superior performance over uniform sampling methods, significantly enhancing accuracy on the tested benchmarks. ### Evaluation: **Novelty:** The concept of RAG-Adapter is commendable as it addresses an identified gap in the testing methodology of video understanding benchmarks. By shifting from uniform frame sampling to a relevance-based approach, the framework proposes a significant improvement in evaluation accuracy, which is critical for MLLMs tasked with long video comprehension. **Significance:** The paper positions itself as a substantial contribution to the field of MLLMs and multimedia understanding. The introduction of the MMAT dataset, combined with the GCL technique, potentially opens new avenues for research in fine-tuning and sampling strategies for improved performance in video understanding tasks. **Strengths:** - The paper identifies and addresses a core issue in existing methodologies with clear empirical validations. - The introduction of fine-tuning strategies, particularly using GCL, shows potential for broader applicability in enhancing performance beyond video understanding to other multimodal tasks. - Clear evaluations of baseline models demonstrate the practical effectiveness of RAG-Adapter. **Weaknesses:** - The paper may lack extensive discussion on the limitations of the proposed framework and scenarios where it might underperform. - Comparative analysis with other recent techniques in video understanding could have strengthened the argument for the distinct advantages of RAG-Adapter. - The complexities of implementing the proposed method in diverse scenarios beyond the tested benchmarks are not sufficiently explored. **Conclusion:** RAG-Adapter significantly advances the testing and evaluation processes of MLLMs in video comprehension, potentially leading to improved benchmarks and methodologies in the field. However, its broad applicability and generalization across various contexts remain to be fully investigated. **Score:** 8
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### HierarQ: Task-Aware Hierarchical Q-Former for Enhanced Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08585v1)
- **Authors**: Shehreen Azad, Vibhav Vineet, Yogesh Singh Rawat
- **Abstract**: Despite advancements in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), current approaches struggle in medium-to-long video understanding due to frame and context length limitations. As a result, these models often depend on frame sampling, which risks missing key information over time and lacks task-specific relevance. To address these challenges, we introduce HierarQ, a task-aware hierarchical Q-Former based framework that sequentially processes frames to bypass the need for frame sampling, while avoiding LLM's context length limitations. We introduce a lightweight two-stream language-guided feature modulator to incorporate task awareness in video understanding, with the entity stream capturing frame-level object information within a short context and the scene stream identifying their broader interactions over longer period of time. Each stream is supported by dedicated memory banks which enables our proposed Hierachical Querying transformer (HierarQ) to effectively capture short and long-term context. Extensive evaluations on 10 video benchmarks across video understanding, question answering, and captioning tasks demonstrate HierarQ's state-of-the-art performance across most datasets, proving its robustness and efficiency for comprehensive video analysis.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents HierarQ, a novel framework designed to improve medium-to-long video understanding in the context of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Current techniques often rely on frame sampling, which risks losing crucial temporal information. HierarQ introduces a task-aware hierarchical Q-Former that processes video frames sequentially, circumventing the limitations of frame sampling and the short context capacity of existing MLLMs. The system employs a two-stream approach: one stream focuses on frame-level object information (entity stream) while the other captures scene interactions over longer durations (scene stream). Each stream is supported by memory banks that enhance the ability to understand both short and long-term video context. The framework is evaluated across 10 video benchmarks, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance in tasks such as video understanding, question answering, and captioning, which underscores its robustness and efficiency. **Critical Evaluation:** The novel contribution of HierarQ lies primarily in its approach to enhancing video understanding by integrating task-specific awareness and leveraging hierarchical querying mechanisms. By addressing a critical bottleneck in existing MLLMs—namely, the inability to effectively handle temporal information over longer video durations—HierarQ proposes a significant improvement. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Framework:** The introduction of a two-stream architecture that separately addresses entity and scene-level interactions represents a fresh perspective in handling multimodal videos. 2. **Memory Banks Utilization:** Employing memory banks enhances the model's capacity for retaining context, which is pivotal in video comprehension tasks. 3. **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The performance across 10 different benchmarks suggests a thorough validation and potentially broad applicability of the framework across various contexts. 4. **Addressing Limitations:** Directly tackling the frame sampling inadequacies presents a meaningful advancement in the state of video analysis technology. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Implementation Complexity:** The hierarchical structure and memory bank system may introduce complexity in terms of model training and deployment, which could limit practical use cases. 2. **Dependence on Task Awareness:** While task specificity is beneficial, the model's high performance may depend heavily on the correct identification of relevant tasks, which might not always be straightforward in real-world applications. 3. **Comparative Performance Analysis:** While the results are impressive, a deeper exploration of performance compared to existing state-of-the-art models in specific scenarios would further substantiate the claims of superiority. 4. **Limited Novelty in Conceptual Base:** While the execution is innovative, the underlying concept of enhancing context awareness in video understanding is not entirely new, as several existing models also work upon similar principles. Given the paper's solid technical contributions, its attention to overcoming limitations in current methods, and its significant experimental validation yet tempered by concerns over complexity and task specificity, I assign this work a score of **8**. This score reflects a strong contribution to the field, acknowledging both its innovative aspects and the practical challenges it may present for implementation. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### NSF-SciFy: Mining the NSF Awards Database for Scientific Claims
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08600v1)
- **Authors**: Delip Rao, Weiqiu You, Eric Wong, Chris Callison-Burch
- **Abstract**: We present NSF-SciFy, a large-scale dataset for scientific claim extraction derived from the National Science Foundation (NSF) awards database, comprising over 400K grant abstracts spanning five decades. While previous datasets relied on published literature, we leverage grant abstracts which offer a unique advantage: they capture claims at an earlier stage in the research lifecycle before publication takes effect. We also introduce a new task to distinguish between existing scientific claims and aspirational research intentions in proposals.Using zero-shot prompting with frontier large language models, we jointly extract 114K scientific claims and 145K investigation proposals from 16K grant abstracts in the materials science domain to create a focused subset called NSF-SciFy-MatSci. We use this dataset to evaluate 3 three key tasks: (1) technical to non-technical abstract generation, where models achieve high BERTScore (0.85+ F1); (2) scientific claim extraction, where fine-tuned models outperform base models by 100% relative improvement; and (3) investigation proposal extraction, showing 90%+ improvement with fine-tuning. We introduce novel LLM-based evaluation metrics for robust assessment of claim/proposal extraction quality. As the largest scientific claim dataset to date -- with an estimated 2.8 million claims across all STEM disciplines funded by the NSF -- NSF-SciFy enables new opportunities for claim verification and meta-scientific research. We publicly release all datasets, trained models, and evaluation code to facilitate further research.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper introduces NSF-SciFy, a comprehensive dataset for extracting scientific claims from the National Science Foundation (NSF) awards database, which includes over 400,000 grant abstracts accumulated over five decades. This dataset uniquely captures scientific claims at an early stage in the research lifecycle, before they undergo the publication process. The authors present a new task that distinguishes between established scientific claims and aspirational research intentions found in the proposals. Utilizing zero-shot prompting with advanced large language models (LLMs), the authors successfully extract 114,000 scientific claims and 145,000 investigation proposals from 16,000 grant abstracts specifically focused on materials science, leading to the creation of a subset termed NSF-SciFy-MatSci. They evaluate the performance of models on three tasks: generating non-technical abstracts, extracting scientific claims, and extracting investigation proposals. The results indicate significant improvements in performance with fine-tuned models, achieving high metrics including BERTScore (0.85+ F1) and over 90% improvement in extraction tasks. Furthermore, the authors propose novel evaluation metrics for assessing the quality of claim and proposal extraction. The dataset is noted as the largest of its kind across all STEM disciplines funded by NSF, enabling advancements in claim verification and meta-scientific research. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation **Novelty and Significance:** The paper presents several novel contributions to the field of information extraction and scientific research evaluation. Firstly, the methodology of using NSF grant abstracts for extracting claims is innovative, as most datasets are derived from published literature, which may not reflect the early intentions and hypotheses present in grant proposals. This shift opens new avenues for understanding scientific research from its inception.  Additionally, the introduction of a new task to distinguish between scientific claims and aspirational intentions adds another layer of complexity that could enrich natural language processing (NLP) studies in scientific contexts. The dataset's size and the breadth of claims across multiple STEM disciplines also significantly contribute to its utility. **Strengths:** 1. **Scale and comprehensiveness**: With over 400,000 abstracts and 2.8 million claims expected, NSF-SciFy is positioned as a robust resource for further research. 2. **Early-stage scientific claims**: The focus on early research lifecycle claims provides critical insights that are not accessible through published literature alone. 3. **Evaluation metrics**: The development of novel metrics for evaluating extraction quality addresses a common gap in similar past efforts. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Generalizability**: While the dataset is significant, its focus on NSF grants may limit the generalizability of findings to other funding agencies or disciplines. 2. **Dependence on LLMs**: The reliance on LLMs for extraction means that the performance may heavily depend on model biases and limitations, which should be carefully considered in future applications. 3. **Potential for oversimplification**: The binary distinction between claims and intentions may not capture the nuanced continuum that scientific proposals often embody. **Potential Influence on the Field:** The NSF-SciFy dataset has the potential to reshape how scientific claims are studied, providing a basis for exploring the evolution of research ideas and the efficacy of funding mechanisms. It can facilitate a deeper understanding of scientific discourse and contribute to enhancing methodologies for meta-scientific analysis. ### Score: 8 **Rationale for Score:** The paper is awarded a score of 8 due to its substantial novelty in the extraction of early-stage scientific claims, the robust dataset created, and the implications for improving research methodologies in science evaluation. While there are limitations regarding generalizability and reliance on LLMs, the contributions made to the field of NLP and meta-science are significant and valuable. The potential for future research and development stemming from this dataset further bolsters its impact within the academic community.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### EMMOE: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Embodied Mobile Manipulation in Open Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08604v1)
- **Authors**: Dongping Li, Tielong Cai, Tianci Tang, Wenhao Chai, Katherine Rose Driggs-Campbell, Gaoang Wang
- **Abstract**: Developing autonomous home robots controlled by natural language has long been a pursuit of human. While advancements in large language models (LLMs) and embodied intelligence make this goal closer, several challenges persist: the lack of a unified benchmark for more complex robot tasks, limited evaluation methods and metrics, data incompatibility between LLMs and mobile manipulation trajectories. To address these issues, we introduce Embodied Mobile Manipulation in Open Environments (EMMOE), which requires agents to interpret user instructions and execute long-horizon everyday tasks in continuous space. EMMOE seamlessly integrates high-level and low-level embodied tasks into a unified framework, along with three new metrics for more diverse assessment. Additionally, we collect EMMOE-100, which features in various task attributes, detailed process annotations, re-plans after failures, and two sub-datasets for LLM training. Furthermore, we design HomieBot, a sophisticated agent system consists of LLM with Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), light weighted navigation and manipulation models, and multiple error detection mechanisms. Finally, we demonstrate HomieBot's performance and the evaluation of different models and policies.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces EMMOE (Embodied Mobile Manipulation in Open Environments), a benchmarking framework aimed at advancing the development of autonomous home robots that can interpret natural language instructions and perform complex tasks in open environments. It addresses key challenges in the field, such as the absence of a unified benchmark for complicated robotic tasks, inadequate evaluation methods, and the lack of compatibility between large language models (LLMs) and mobile manipulation trajectories. EMMOE integrates high and low-level tasks within a singular framework, provides three new metrics for diverse evaluations, and presents the EMMOE-100 dataset, which includes diverse task attributes, annotated processes, failure re-plans, and supports LLM training. The framework is demonstrated using HomieBot, a sophisticated agent that consists of LLMs optimized through Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), lightweight navigation, manipulation models, and error detection mechanisms. The paper assesses the performance of HomieBot and evaluates several models and policies. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a notable contribution to the field of robotics, particularly in mobile manipulation and natural language processing. Its novelty lies in the creation of a unified benchmarking framework that ties together the high-level language understanding capabilities with the practical execution of robotic tasks—a critical gap in existing literature. The introduction of the EMMOE-100 dataset enhances the practical applicability of the framework by providing a rich set of tasks and processes that are relevant for evaluating LLM performance in real-world scenarios. One of the strengths of the work is its comprehensive approach that tackles multiple facets of the problem—from developing a new benchmark and dataset to integrating advanced agent systems. The proposed metrics for evaluation add further depth to the assessment process, enabling a more nuanced comparison of different models and strategies. However, the paper also presents some weaknesses. The implementation of the HomieBot agent, while well-conceived, may require thorough real-world testing to validate its effectiveness beyond experimental conditions. Additionally, the paper could benefit from providing a more in-depth discussion on the limitations of the EMMOE framework and potential areas for future research, such as scalability or adaptability to other environments outside of the ones tested. Overall, the impact of the research is substantial, as it not only provides tools for evaluation but also sets a foundation for future studies in embodied AI and mobile manipulation. Nonetheless, the paper is somewhat incremental in nature as it builds upon existing works in LLMs and mobile robotics without completely redefining the landscape. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### Tuning-Free Multi-Event Long Video Generation via Synchronized Coupled Sampling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08605v1)
- **Authors**: Subin Kim, Seoung Wug Oh, Jui-Hsien Wang, Joon-Young Lee, Jinwoo Shin
- **Abstract**: While recent advancements in text-to-video diffusion models enable high-quality short video generation from a single prompt, generating real-world long videos in a single pass remains challenging due to limited data and high computational costs. To address this, several works propose tuning-free approaches, i.e., extending existing models for long video generation, specifically using multiple prompts to allow for dynamic and controlled content changes. However, these methods primarily focus on ensuring smooth transitions between adjacent frames, often leading to content drift and a gradual loss of semantic coherence over longer sequences. To tackle such an issue, we propose Synchronized Coupled Sampling (SynCoS), a novel inference framework that synchronizes denoising paths across the entire video, ensuring long-range consistency across both adjacent and distant frames. Our approach combines two complementary sampling strategies: reverse and optimization-based sampling, which ensure seamless local transitions and enforce global coherence, respectively. However, directly alternating between these samplings misaligns denoising trajectories, disrupting prompt guidance and introducing unintended content changes as they operate independently. To resolve this, SynCoS synchronizes them through a grounded timestep and a fixed baseline noise, ensuring fully coupled sampling with aligned denoising paths. Extensive experiments show that SynCoS significantly improves multi-event long video generation, achieving smoother transitions and superior long-range coherence, outperforming previous approaches both quantitatively and qualitatively.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces a novel approach called Synchronized Coupled Sampling (SynCoS) to enhance the generation of long videos from multiple prompts without the need for model tuning. While existing text-to-video diffusion models are effective for generating short videos, they struggle with long video sequences due to content drift and incoherence over time. SynCoS addresses these issues by synchronizing the denoising processes of reverse and optimization-based sampling methods. This synchronization helps maintain both local and long-range coherence across video frames. The authors demonstrate through extensive experiments that SynCoS not only improves the fluidity of transitions between events but also achieves better semantic consistency than previous methods, marking a significant advancement in the multi-event long video generation landscape. **Evaluation:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in its approach to synchronizing two different sampling strategies—reverse sampling and optimization-based sampling—while generating long-form video content. The introduction of the Synchronized Coupled Sampling (SynCoS) framework is a thoughtful response to the prevalent issues of content drift and coherence loss during video generation, which are significant barriers in the current landscape of text-to-video models. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Framework**: The framework proposed is a fresh take on the existing problem of long video generation, addressing the gap in maintaining coherence when utilizing multiple prompts. 2. **Rigorous Experimentation**: The authors conducted extensive experiments, demonstrating that their method outperforms previous approaches both quantitatively and qualitatively. This empirical validation strengthens the paper. 3. **Broad Relevance**: The findings are relevant not only for researchers focusing on text-to-video generation but also for broader applications in multimedia generation and machine learning. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity of Implementation**: The synchronization of two methods could pose additional implementation challenges. Practical complexities may hinder adoption in some scenarios. 2. **Scalability**: While the method improves coherence, the computational cost and time efficiency concerns for generating long videos still warrant attention, as real-world applications may require additional scalability considerations. 3. **Limited Exploration of Variants**: The paper focuses primarily on synchronization without exploring alternative approaches or advantages offered by other sampling strategies that could also enhance the results. **Potential Influence**: The contribution could significantly influence future work in the area, pushing researchers to explore coupled approaches in similar settings. However, for immediate practical implementation, the complexity remains a concern that needs addressing. Considering these aspects, the paper represents a meaningful advancement in the field of long video generation but might face hurdles in practical application due to implementation complexity. Thus, a score of 7 reflects a strong contribution that is well-grounded but needs careful consideration for broader application. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LightGen: Efficient Image Generation through Knowledge Distillation and Direct Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08619v1)
- **Authors**: Xianfeng Wu, Yajing Bai, Haoze Zheng, Harold Haodong Chen, Yexin Liu, Zihao Wang, Xuran Ma, Wen-Jie Shu, Xianzu Wu, Harry Yang, Ser-Nam Lim
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-image generation have primarily relied on extensive datasets and parameter-heavy architectures. These requirements severely limit accessibility for researchers and practitioners who lack substantial computational resources. In this paper, we introduce \model, an efficient training paradigm for image generation models that uses knowledge distillation (KD) and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). Drawing inspiration from the success of data KD techniques widely adopted in Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs), LightGen distills knowledge from state-of-the-art (SOTA) text-to-image models into a compact Masked Autoregressive (MAR) architecture with only $0.7B$ parameters. Using a compact synthetic dataset of just $2M$ high-quality images generated from varied captions, we demonstrate that data diversity significantly outweighs data volume in determining model performance. This strategy dramatically reduces computational demands and reduces pre-training time from potentially thousands of GPU-days to merely 88 GPU-days. Furthermore, to address the inherent shortcomings of synthetic data, particularly poor high-frequency details and spatial inaccuracies, we integrate the DPO technique that refines image fidelity and positional accuracy. Comprehensive experiments confirm that LightGen achieves image generation quality comparable to SOTA models while significantly reducing computational resources and expanding accessibility for resource-constrained environments. Code is available at https://github.com/XianfengWu01/LightGen
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper presents LightGen, a new training paradigm for text-to-image generation that emphasizes efficiency and accessibility in computational resource usage. It leverages knowledge distillation (KD) from advanced text-to-image models into a compact Masked Autoregressive (MAR) architecture, comprising only 0.7 billion parameters. The authors argue that utilizing a compact synthetic dataset of 2 million high-quality images focuses more on data diversity rather than volume, leading to enhanced model performance. They address some limitations of synthetic data, such as low fidelity and spatial inaccuracies, through Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). The results indicate that LightGen achieves comparable quality to state-of-the-art (SOTA) models while significantly decreasing the training time to only 88 GPU-days. The model’s code is openly provided for further exploration. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty**:  The approach of using knowledge distillation in conjunction with Direct Preference Optimization provides a fresh perspective on reducing model complexity and training time without significant trade-offs in generative quality. While the idea of refining synthetic data is an established challenge, the integration of DPO for this specific purpose within a compact architecture presents a notable advancement. **Significance**:  This research addresses a significant barrier within the field: the extensive computing requirements traditionally associated with high-performance image generation. By making advancements that lower these requirements, the paper potentially opens the field to a broader range of researchers, promoting further innovation and experimentation. The focus on data diversity versus volume sheds light on performance metrics that could guide future dataset construction and model training strategies. **Strengths**: - The reduction in resource demands while maintaining competitive image generation quality is a strong point. This could enable practitioners with limited access to resources to engage in cutting-edge research. - The combination of knowledge distillation and DPO offers a novel methodology that could be applicable beyond image generation. **Weaknesses**: - The evaluation relies heavily on the synthetic dataset, which, despite its diversity, may not fully replicate the complexities of real-world data. It would be helpful to see comparisons against diverse real-world datasets to validate the efficacy of the approach under varied conditions. - The long-term implications and scalability of this approach to even larger datasets or more complex tasks are not addressed extensively within the paper. **Potential Influence**:  The model has the potential to significantly impact the field of generative AI by providing a compelling alternative to parameter-heavy architectures, making it relevant for deployment in resource-scarce environments. However, the true extent of its influence will depend on community adoption and further validation. **Score**: 8 This score reflects the paper’s substantial contributions to efficiency in image generation while highlighting both innovative and traditional approaches. The potential for practical application in under-resourced environments bolsters its significance, although the reliance on synthetic data without extensive real-world comparisons prevents an outright top score.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### Rethinking Diffusion Model in High Dimension
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08643v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenxin Zheng, Zhenjie Zheng
- **Abstract**: Curse of Dimensionality is an unavoidable challenge in statistical probability models, yet diffusion models seem to overcome this limitation, achieving impressive results in high-dimensional data generation. Diffusion models assume that they can learn the statistical properties of the underlying probability distribution, enabling sampling from this distribution to generate realistic samples. But is this really how they work? To address this question, this paper conducts a detailed analysis of the objective function and inference methods of diffusion models, leading to several important conclusions that help answer the above question: 1) In high-dimensional sparse scenarios, the target of the objective function fitting degrades from a weighted sum of multiple samples to a single sample. 2) The mainstream inference methods can all be represented within a simple unified framework, without requiring statistical concepts such as Markov chains and SDEs. 3) Guided by this simple framework, more efficient inference methods can be discovered.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Rethinking Diffusion Model in High Dimension" scrutinizes the effectiveness of diffusion models in high-dimensional settings, where the Curse of Dimensionality presents significant challenges. The authors analyze diffusion models' objective functions and inference methods, leading to crucial insights. They conclude that in sparse high-dimensional contexts, the fitting target deviates from a combination of multiple samples to focus on a single sample. Additionally, they propose that mainstream inference strategies can be unified under a straightforward framework that does not rely on traditional statistical methods like Markov chains and Stochastic Differential Equations (SDEs). This simplification paves the way for the development of more efficient inference techniques. **Evaluation:** The paper makes several noteworthy contributions to the understanding of diffusion models in high-dimensional data generation. The critical analysis of the objective function and inference methods provides valuable insights that could influence future research.  **Strengths:** 1. **Novel Perspectives:** The paper challenges existing assumptions about diffusion models by revealing how the fitting target changes in high-dimensional sparse scenarios. 2. **Unified Framework:** By proposing a simpler representation of inference methods, the authors create a foundation that may streamline the development of more efficient algorithms. 3. **Implications for Practice:** The findings have practical implications for researchers and practitioners working with high-dimensional data, suggesting new avenues for optimization. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Empirical Validation:** While the theoretical contributions are significant, the paper could benefit from empirical evidence demonstrating the efficacy of the proposed unified framework and the new inference methods. 2. **Broader Applicability:** The scope of the analysis appears somewhat narrow, focusing primarily on high-dimensional sparse data. Broader applicability or mention of other data contexts could enhance the relevance of the findings. 3. **Depth of Analysis:** Some sections could benefit from deeper exploration, particularly regarding the implications of transitioning from multiple to single sample fitting and its effect on model performance. In conclusion, while the paper presents important new perspectives on diffusion models, its impact is somewhat tempered by a lack of empirical validation and a narrow focus. Nonetheless, the introduction of a simplified framework and highlighted shifts in objective function fitting dig deeper into the understanding of high-dimensional statistical modeling.  **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### MF-VITON: High-Fidelity Mask-Free Virtual Try-On with Minimal Input
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08650v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenchen Wan, Yanwu xu, Dongting Hu, Weilun Cheng, Tianxi Chen, Zhaoqing Wang, Feng Liu, Tongliang Liu, Mingming Gong
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Virtual Try-On (VITON) have significantly improved image realism and garment detail preservation, driven by powerful text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. However, existing methods often rely on user-provided masks, introducing complexity and performance degradation due to imperfect inputs, as shown in Fig.1(a). To address this, we propose a Mask-Free VITON (MF-VITON) framework that achieves realistic VITON using only a single person image and a target garment, eliminating the requirement for auxiliary masks. Our approach introduces a novel two-stage pipeline: (1) We leverage existing Mask-based VITON models to synthesize a high-quality dataset. This dataset contains diverse, realistic pairs of person images and corresponding garments, augmented with varied backgrounds to mimic real-world scenarios. (2) The pre-trained Mask-based model is fine-tuned on the generated dataset, enabling garment transfer without mask dependencies. This stage simplifies the input requirements while preserving garment texture and shape fidelity. Our framework achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance regarding garment transfer accuracy and visual realism. Notably, the proposed Mask-Free model significantly outperforms existing Mask-based approaches, setting a new benchmark and demonstrating a substantial lead over previous approaches. For more details, visit our project page: https://zhenchenwan.github.io/MF-VITON/.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper presents a novel framework called MF-VITON (Mask-Free Virtual Try-On), which improves the Virtual Try-On (VITON) process by eliminating the need for user-provided masks, simplifying input requirements. This is achieved through a two-stage pipeline: the first stage synthesizes a high-quality dataset using existing Mask-based VITON models to create diverse pairs of person images and garments, while the second stage fine-tunes a pre-trained Mask-based model on this dataset to facilitate garment transfer without relying on masks. The proposed approach demonstrates superior performance in garment transfer accuracy and visual realism compared to existing methods, establishing a new benchmark within the field. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance: The novelty in MF-VITON lies in its innovative approach to circumvent the complexities and limitations associated with using masks in VITON applications. Traditionally, mask-based methods were essential for delineating garment areas, which not only required additional user input but also suffered from inaccuracies that could affect the final output quality. MF-VITON's ability to provide high-quality results using a single image and garment pair is a significant advancement. Strengths: 1. **Improved Accessibility:** By removing the need for user-provided masks, MF-VITON makes the technology more accessible to non-technical users, likely increasing adoption rates. 2. **High Fidelity:** The reported state-of-the-art performance in garment transfer accuracy and visual realism indicates a substantial improvement over previously established methods, thus pushing the boundaries of what is achievable in virtual try-on technology. 3. **Dataset Generation Methodology:** The novel dataset generation strategy by leveraging existing models is a solid methodological innovation, providing a way to create training data that captures real-world garment and person image variations effectively. Weaknesses: 1. **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** While fine-tuning on a specifically generated dataset is effective, the process's performance is still contingent on the quality and capabilities of the pre-trained Mask-based VITON model. If the pre-trained model has inherent biases or limitations, this could affect the final product. 2. **Lack of Extensive Testing Across Varied Conditions:** The paper does not sufficiently detail the evaluation of the framework across diverse demographic and style variations. The effectiveness of the model in different contexts could further validate its strength. 3. **Potential Overfitting Risk:** Generating a high-quality dataset by relying on a limited number of mask-based models might lead to overfitting, where the model performs excellently on synthetic data but poorly on real-world scenarios. Overall, the MF-VITON framework stands out as a significant contribution to the VITON literature with its streamlined approach and improved accuracy. However, the strengths mitigate concerns regarding overfitting and reliance on pre-trained models. These considerations and the practical implications of making virtual try-on technology more user-friendly contribute positively to its significance in the field. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Exploring the Word Sense Disambiguation Capabilities of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08662v1)
- **Authors**: Pierpaolo Basile, Lucia Siciliani, Elio Musacchio, Giovanni Semeraro
- **Abstract**: Word Sense Disambiguation (WSD) is a historical task in computational linguistics that has received much attention over the years. However, with the advent of Large Language Models (LLMs), interest in this task (in its classical definition) has decreased. In this study, we evaluate the performance of various LLMs on the WSD task. We extend a previous benchmark (XL-WSD) to re-design two subtasks suitable for LLM: 1) given a word in a sentence, the LLM must generate the correct definition; 2) given a word in a sentence and a set of predefined meanings, the LLM must select the correct one. The extended benchmark is built using the XL-WSD and BabelNet. The results indicate that LLMs perform well in zero-shot learning but cannot surpass current state-of-the-art methods. However, a fine-tuned model with a medium number of parameters outperforms all other models, including the state-of-the-art.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper investigates the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the task of Word Sense Disambiguation (WSD), a traditional area in computational linguistics. It critiques the waning interest in classical WSD with the rise of LLMs and introduces an expanded benchmark, XL-WSD, with two subtasks: generating a correct definition for a given word in context and selecting the correct meaning from a predefined set. The benchmark is constructed using XL-WSD and BabelNet. Findings show that while LLMs exhibit strong zero-shot learning capabilities, they do not surpass existing state-of-the-art techniques. Nevertheless, a medium-sized fine-tuned model outperforms these state-of-the-art methods, highlighting the potential for optimized models in WSD tasks. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a thoughtful exploration of LLMs in WSD, a task that has historically posed challenges in natural language processing. Its novelty hinges on the re-contextualization of WSD for modern models, using a benchmark that combines well-established resources and newly designed tasks.  **Strengths:** 1. **Addressing a Gap**: The study highlights a significant gap in existing research by revealing that while LLMs perform well, they do not achieve the highest accuracy in WSD, suggesting that the classic approaches still hold merit. 2. **Innovative Benchmark Design**: The redesign of the XL-WSD benchmark to suit LLMs indicates forward-thinking and adaptability, encouraging further research in this area. 3. **Empirical Validation**: By presenting empirical results of various models, the paper adds rigor to claims about LLM capabilities. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Discussion on Implications**: While the results are valuable, the paper lacks an extensive discussion on broader implications of the findings for the future of WSD and language model research. 2. **Context of Results**: The paper does not sufficiently contextualize the performance of the fine-tuned model compared to state-of-the-art methods in terms of real-world applications or scenarios where these distinctions matter. 3. **Scope**: The study focuses primarily on evaluation without offering significant insights into how WSD can be further improved within the framework of LLMs. Overall, this study represents a timely contribution that revitalizes interest in a classical problem with modern tools, but it would benefit from a deeper discussion of the implications of its findings and a broader perspective on potential advancements in the field. **Score: 7** Justification: The score reflects the paper's solid contributions, particularly in refining WSD evaluation for LLMs and challenging current perceptions of LLM capabilities. However, the limited depth of discussion regarding the implications of the findings and the somewhat narrow focus on the evaluation detracts from its impact. While not groundbreaking, it is an important piece that opens avenues for future work in the intersection of LLMs and WSD.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generating Robot Constitutions & Benchmarks for Semantic Safety
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08663v1)
- **Authors**: Pierre Sermanet, Anirudha Majumdar, Alex Irpan, Dmitry Kalashnikov, Vikas Sindhwani
- **Abstract**: Until recently, robotics safety research was predominantly about collision avoidance and hazard reduction in the immediate vicinity of a robot. Since the advent of large vision and language models (VLMs), robots are now also capable of higher-level semantic scene understanding and natural language interactions with humans. Despite their known vulnerabilities (e.g. hallucinations or jail-breaking), VLMs are being handed control of robots capable of physical contact with the real world. This can lead to dangerous behaviors, making semantic safety for robots a matter of immediate concern. Our contributions in this paper are two fold: first, to address these emerging risks, we release the ASIMOV Benchmark, a large-scale and comprehensive collection of datasets for evaluating and improving semantic safety of foundation models serving as robot brains. Our data generation recipe is highly scalable: by leveraging text and image generation techniques, we generate undesirable situations from real-world visual scenes and human injury reports from hospitals. Secondly, we develop a framework to automatically generate robot constitutions from real-world data to steer a robot's behavior using Constitutional AI mechanisms. We propose a novel auto-amending process that is able to introduce nuances in written rules of behavior; this can lead to increased alignment with human preferences on behavior desirability and safety. We explore trade-offs between generality and specificity across a diverse set of constitutions of different lengths, and demonstrate that a robot is able to effectively reject unconstitutional actions. We measure a top alignment rate of 84.3% on the ASIMOV Benchmark using generated constitutions, outperforming no-constitution baselines and human-written constitutions. Data is available at asimov-benchmark.github.io
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper: The paper discusses the pressing concern of semantic safety in robotics, particularly with the integration of large vision and language models (VLMs) controlling robots. It highlights the limitations of traditional safety approaches that focused on collision avoidance and introduces the ASIMOV Benchmark, a dataset for evaluating the semantic safety of robots. This benchmark includes undesirable scenarios derived from real-world data and injury reports, aiming to enhance the safety of VLMs operating in physical environments. Additionally, the paper proposes a framework for generating robot constitutions through data-driven methods to guide robot behavior in line with human preferences. A novel auto-amending process is introduced to refine the rules of conduct, achieving a top alignment rate of 84.3% in rejecting unconstitutional actions when compared to traditional approaches. The authors argue for the importance of this work in ensuring safety in robotic systems leveraging VLMs. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance: The paper presents both theoretical and practical contributions to the field of robotics safety by addressing a critical gap in the literature: the risks associated with robots utilizing VLMs. Its focus on semantic safety, rather than just collision avoidance, represents a shift in addressing robotic interactions with the environment and humans. The creation of the ASIMOV Benchmark is significant as it provides a resource for the community to evaluate and compare the safety of various robotic systems, thus promoting further research in this area. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach:** The framework for auto-generating robot constitutions is a novel contribution that combines principles from Constitutional AI with practical robotics applications, which may influence how behavior is governed in robotic systems. 2. **Scalability:** The use of scalable data generation methodologies represents a progressive approach to addressing safety concerns, making it easier to produce diverse datasets relevant to real-world contexts. 3. **High Performance Metrics:** Achieving a high alignment rate indicates that the proposed constitutions can effectively guide robot behavior, showcasing potential for practical deployment. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Validation and Generalization:** While the paper presents promising results, the generalizability of the ASIMOV Benchmark and the effectiveness of the constitutional approaches across diverse robotic applications remain uncertain, requiring further empirical validation. 2. **Ethical Considerations:** The ethical implications and potential biases in the datasets used (e.g., human injury reports) are not thoroughly addressed, which could affect the fairness of generated constitutions. 3. **Complexity of Implementation:** Adapting these frameworks in real-world robotic systems may be complex, which the paper does not fully explore. **Overall Influence:** This paper has significant implications for the field of robotics, as it charts a new course for ensuring safety through understanding and governing robot behavior at a semantic level. It encourages future research and applications of Constitutionally-aligned AI systems in practical environments. **Score: 8**   Rationale: While the contributions are significant and the paper addresses a novel aspect of robotic safety, its practical implementation and broader implications require rigorous validation to fully assess effectiveness and ethical considerations. Thus, an 8 reflects both recognition of the contributions and the need for further exploration to solidify its impact.
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### MEAT: Multiview Diffusion Model for Human Generation on Megapixels with Mesh Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08664v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhan Wang, Fangzhou Hong, Shuai Yang, Liming Jiang, Wayne Wu, Chen Change Loy
- **Abstract**: Multiview diffusion models have shown considerable success in image-to-3D generation for general objects. However, when applied to human data, existing methods have yet to deliver promising results, largely due to the challenges of scaling multiview attention to higher resolutions. In this paper, we explore human multiview diffusion models at the megapixel level and introduce a solution called mesh attention to enable training at 1024x1024 resolution. Using a clothed human mesh as a central coarse geometric representation, the proposed mesh attention leverages rasterization and projection to establish direct cross-view coordinate correspondences. This approach significantly reduces the complexity of multiview attention while maintaining cross-view consistency. Building on this foundation, we devise a mesh attention block and combine it with keypoint conditioning to create our human-specific multiview diffusion model, MEAT. In addition, we present valuable insights into applying multiview human motion videos for diffusion training, addressing the longstanding issue of data scarcity. Extensive experiments show that MEAT effectively generates dense, consistent multiview human images at the megapixel level, outperforming existing multiview diffusion methods.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "MEAT: Multiview Diffusion Model for Human Generation on Megapixels with Mesh Attention" addresses a significant challenge in the field of image-to-3D generation, specifically for human figures. Existing multiview diffusion models struggle to generate high-quality human representations due to difficulties in managing multiview attention at high resolutions. To overcome this, the authors introduce a novel approach called mesh attention, which operates at 1024x1024 resolution by using clothed human meshes as a foundational geometric representation. This method effectively reduces the complexity of multiview attention while ensuring consistent cross-view representations by utilizing rasterization and projection techniques. The paper develops a specialized multiview diffusion model, MEAT, integrating mesh attention blocks with keypoint conditioning. It also discusses leveraging multiview human motion videos to enhance training data, addressing the prevalent issue of data scarcity in the domain. Experimental results indicate that MEAT surpasses existing methods in generating detailed and consistent multiview human images at megapixel scale. ### Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance **Strengths:** 1. **Addressing a Core Challenge:** The paper tackles a well-established challenge in human generation from images, specifically in achieving high-resolution outputs while maintaining temporal and spatial consistency. 2. **Innovative Approach:** The novel mesh attention mechanism offers a theoretical and practical advancement by simplifying the interaction between different views, suggesting a promising new avenue for future research. 3. **Data Scarcity Solution:** By proposing to use motion videos for training, the authors explore new methods of augmenting datasets, which is crucial given the difficulty of obtaining human-specific image datasets at scale. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity in Implementation:** While the proposed solution is elegant, the practical implementation details and computational overhead of the mesh attention may present challenges for reproducibility and application in broader contexts. 2. **Comparison to Existing Methods:** Although the paper claims superior performance, it would be beneficial to have a more detailed analysis of specific metrics and qualitative assessments compared to not only existing multiview diffusion models but also state-of-the-art techniques in human generation. **Potential Influence:** The introduction of mesh attention in this context has the potential to shift methodologies within the field of human generation and offers a foundation for future research, particularly for tasks that require high-resolution output and spatial accuracy. If validated broadly, it could inspire new architectures in other domains where cross-view consistency is critical. ### Overall Assessment Overall, the paper presents a significant advancement in the field of multiview diffusion models specific to human generation, addressing key challenges and contributing valuable methodologies and findings. However, its practical implications and broader adaptability remain to be fully explored through extensive empirical validation in various contexts. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the promise and novelty of the research while acknowledging the need for broader applicability and detailed comparative analysis in the paper's claims. The research has important potential implications for future work in the field but falls just short of a groundbreaking contribution which can universally redefine practices.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### REGEN: Learning Compact Video Embedding with (Re-)Generative Decoder
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08665v1)
- **Authors**: Yitian Zhang, Long Mai, Aniruddha Mahapatra, David Bourgin, Yicong Hong, Jonah Casebeer, Feng Liu, Yun Fu
- **Abstract**: We present a novel perspective on learning video embedders for generative modeling: rather than requiring an exact reproduction of an input video, an effective embedder should focus on synthesizing visually plausible reconstructions. This relaxed criterion enables substantial improvements in compression ratios without compromising the quality of downstream generative models. Specifically, we propose replacing the conventional encoder-decoder video embedder with an encoder-generator framework that employs a diffusion transformer (DiT) to synthesize missing details from a compact latent space. Therein, we develop a dedicated latent conditioning module to condition the DiT decoder on the encoded video latent embedding. Our experiments demonstrate that our approach enables superior encoding-decoding performance compared to state-of-the-art methods, particularly as the compression ratio increases. To demonstrate the efficacy of our approach, we report results from our video embedders achieving a temporal compression ratio of up to 32x (8x higher than leading video embedders) and validate the robustness of this ultra-compact latent space for text-to-video generation, providing a significant efficiency boost in latent diffusion model training and inference.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "REGEN: Learning Compact Video Embedding with (Re-)Generative Decoder" introduces a novel methodology for learning video embedders aimed at generative modeling. The authors argue that video embedders should prioritize the synthesis of visually plausible reconstructions over mere exact reproduction, which allows for enhanced compression ratios while maintaining the quality of generative models. They propose an encoder-generator framework that uses a Diffusion Transformer (DiT) to recover missing details from a compact latent representation. Key contributions include the development of a latent conditioning module that effectively conditions the DiT decoder on the encoded video latent. Experimental results reveal that their approach offers substantial encoding-decoding performance improvements, achieving a temporal compression ratio of up to 32x, which is significantly better than existing methods. The study further shows the robustness of the proposed ultra-compact latent space in applications such as text-to-video generation. **Critical Evaluation:** This paper shows a noteworthy departure from traditional video embedding techniques by emphasizing plausible reconstructions rather than precise reproductions. This shift in focus is significant as it supports a notable enhancement in compression ratios, which is critical for practical applications in video processing and storage. The introduction of a diffusion transformer framework shows creativity and addresses current limitations in the field. Strengths of the paper include: 1. **Novel Approach:** The introduction of a generator-based architecture with conditioning demonstrates a forward-thinking method to learn compact video embeddings. 2. **Quantitative Results:** The reported results, such as a 32x compression ratio, are compelling and suggest that the proposed model could lead to substantial practical benefits in video generation applications. 3. **Broader Impact:** Improved efficiency in latently represented video synthesis could accelerate advancements in various applications like video editing, summarization, and generation. However, there are notable weaknesses: 1. **Specificity of Results:** While the performance metrics are impressive, more details on how these results compare across diverse datasets or conditions could strengthen the claims about robustness and applicability. 2. **Generative Quality:** Though visually plausible reconstructions are emphasized, the paper lacks a comprehensive qualitative analysis comparing generated outputs relative to existing methods, which is crucial for understanding the real-world utility. 3. **Complexity of Implementation:** The reliance on a latent conditioning module and generator architecture may pose practical implementation challenges that are not thoroughly addressed. Overall, the significance of the work is solid, particularly because it addresses integral challenges in video representation and synthesis, which is a growing area of research. However, it remains to be fully seen how these innovations hold up across real-world applications and diverse datasets. Given these considerations, I assign a score of **8** to the paper. It presents a substantial contribution to the field with its novel approach, and the results are promising; however, additional clarity on robustness and real-world applicability would further solidify its impact and significance. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Language-Depth Navigated Thermal and Visible Image Fusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08676v1)
- **Authors**: Jinchang Zhang, Zijun Li, Guoyu Lu
- **Abstract**: Depth-guided multimodal fusion combines depth information from visible and infrared images, significantly enhancing the performance of 3D reconstruction and robotics applications. Existing thermal-visible image fusion mainly focuses on detection tasks, ignoring other critical information such as depth. By addressing the limitations of single modalities in low-light and complex environments, the depth information from fused images not only generates more accurate point cloud data, improving the completeness and precision of 3D reconstruction, but also provides comprehensive scene understanding for robot navigation, localization, and environmental perception. This supports precise recognition and efficient operations in applications such as autonomous driving and rescue missions. We introduce a text-guided and depth-driven infrared and visible image fusion network. The model consists of an image fusion branch for extracting multi-channel complementary information through a diffusion model, equipped with a text-guided module, and two auxiliary depth estimation branches. The fusion branch uses CLIP to extract semantic information and parameters from depth-enriched image descriptions to guide the diffusion model in extracting multi-channel features and generating fused images. These fused images are then input into the depth estimation branches to calculate depth-driven loss, optimizing the image fusion network. This framework aims to integrate vision-language and depth to directly generate color-fused images from multimodal inputs.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Language-Depth Navigated Thermal and Visible Image Fusion" addresses the integration of depth information from thermal and visible images for enhanced 3D reconstruction and robotics applications. The authors highlight the limitations of existing thermal-visible image fusion techniques, which primarily focus on detection tasks without exploiting depth information. By utilizing depth-guided multimodal fusion, they aim to improve point cloud data accuracy and provide better scene understanding for robotic navigation and localization. The proposed network incorporates a fusion branch that employs a diffusion model and a text-guided module to extract semantic information, alongside two auxiliary depth estimation branches. This innovative framework aims to generate color-fused images directly from multimodal inputs, ultimately supporting applications in autonomous driving and rescue missions. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper contributes to the field of multimodal fusion by incorporating depth information in a novel way, which is indeed a critical component often overlooked in current thermal-visible fusion techniques. The approach leverages cutting-edge methodologies, such as diffusion models and language-guided image processing, highlighting a thoughtful integration of computer vision, natural language processing, and depth estimation. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach:** The integration of multimodal inputs (thermal and visible images) with text guidance is a notable advancement, emphasizing depth information's role in producing cohesive spatial representations. 2. **Applicability:** Focusing on practical applications such as autonomous driving and rescue missions adds significant value, making the research relevant across various real-world scenarios. 3. **Methodological Robustness:** The use of established models, such as CLIP, for semantic feature extraction demonstrates a sound methodological choice, enhancing the framework's reliability. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The abstract does not clearly indicate a comprehensive evaluation of the proposed model against existing state-of-the-art methods. Unless demonstrated, this raises questions about the extent of improvement and generalizability. 2. **Complexity Concerns:** The reliance on a multi-branch architecture may introduce significant computational overhead, posing challenges for real-time applications. This trade-off should be addressed, particularly in robotics where efficiency is paramount. 3. **Broader Context:** The integration of language in guiding image fusion, while promising, may not be fully explored or justified in terms of its necessity versus solely using depth data. More exploration of this aspect would enhance clarity on its significance. Considering these factors, the novel combination of modalities and focus on depth result in a valuable contribution; however, the concerns raised regarding evaluation and complexity temper enthusiasm. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### GarmentCrafter: Progressive Novel View Synthesis for Single-View 3D Garment Reconstruction and Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08678v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanhao Wang, Cheng Zhang, Gonçalo Frazão, Jinlong Yang, Alexandru-Eugen Ichim, Thabo Beeler, Fernando De la Torre
- **Abstract**: We introduce GarmentCrafter, a new approach that enables non-professional users to create and modify 3D garments from a single-view image. While recent advances in image generation have facilitated 2D garment design, creating and editing 3D garments remains challenging for non-professional users. Existing methods for single-view 3D reconstruction often rely on pre-trained generative models to synthesize novel views conditioning on the reference image and camera pose, yet they lack cross-view consistency, failing to capture the internal relationships across different views. In this paper, we tackle this challenge through progressive depth prediction and image warping to approximate novel views. Subsequently, we train a multi-view diffusion model to complete occluded and unknown clothing regions, informed by the evolving camera pose. By jointly inferring RGB and depth, GarmentCrafter enforces inter-view coherence and reconstructs precise geometries and fine details. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves superior visual fidelity and inter-view coherence compared to state-of-the-art single-view 3D garment reconstruction methods.
