However, existing approaches inadequately address the dual objectives of debloating: maintaining functionality by preserving essential features and enhancing security by reducing security issues. Specifically, current software debloating techniques often rely on input-based analysis, using user inputs as proxies for the specifications of desired features. However, these approaches frequently overfit provided inputs, leading to functionality loss and potential security vulnerabilities. To address these limitations, we propose LEADER, a program debloating framework enhanced by Large Language Models (LLMs), which leverages their semantic understanding, generative capabilities, and decision-making strengths. LEADER mainly consists of two modules: (1) a documentation-guided test augmentation module designed to preserve functionality, which leverages LLMs to comprehend program documentation and generates sufficient tests to cover the desired features comprehensively, and (2) a multi-advisor-aided program debloating module that employs a neuro-symbolic pipeline to ensure that the security of the software can be perceived during debloating. This module combines debloating and security advisors for analysis and employs an LLM as a decision-maker to eliminate undesired code securely. Extensive evaluations on widely used benchmarks demonstrate the efficacy of LEADER. These results demonstrate that LEADER surpasses the state-of-the-art tool CovA in functionality and security. These results underscore the potential of LEADER to set a new standard in program debloating by effectively balancing functionality and security.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents LEADER, a novel framework for program debloating which leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to effectively address the dual goals of reducing software bloat while maintaining essential functionalities and enhancing security. Traditional approaches primarily rely on input-based analysis, often leading to functionality loss and security vulnerabilities due to overfitting. LEADER incorporates two key modules: a documentation-guided test augmentation module, which generates tests based on program documentation to ensure feature coverage, and a multi-advisor program debloating module, that integrates security considerations into the debloating process. The framework utilizes a neuro-symbolic approach with LLMs acting as decision-makers to securely eliminate unwanted code. Evaluation results indicate LEADER outperforms the current leading tool, CovA, in both functionality preservation and security, suggesting its potential to redefine standards in program debloating. **Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:** This paper introduces a significant advancement in the area of software debloating by integrating state-of-the-art LLM technology into the process. The preservation of functionality while minimizing security threats presents a well-articulated response to current shortcomings in existing techniques, which tend to either compromise performance or leave security risks unaddressed. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach:** The combination of LLMs with traditional software debloating methods is a fresh perspective. The semantic understanding inherent to LLMs could indeed enhance the capability to maintain necessary functionalities better than prior input-centric methods. 2. **Robust Testing Methodology:** The documentation-guided test augmentation is particularly novel, as it shifts the focus from arbitrary user inputs to a more comprehensive understanding of the software's purpose and requirements. 3. **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The rigorous testing against established benchmarks demonstrates the practical applicability of LEADER and reinforces the credibility of the findings. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Dependency on LLM Accuracy:** The performance of LEADER is closely tied to the accuracy and capabilities of the LLM being used. Any limitations in the LLM’s understanding could lead to suboptimal debloating decisions. 2. **Potential Overhead:** While the use of LLMs offers many advantages, it may introduce additional computational overhead, which could hinder performance in environments where efficiency is critical. 3. **Generalizability Concerns:** The paper does not clearly address whether the approach can be generalized across different types of software systems beyond those used in the evaluations. The specific conditions or contexts where LEADER excels remain somewhat ambiguous. Given these considerations, while the methodology and contributions are impactful, they require careful implementation and evaluation across a broader spectrum of software types to fully validate the claims. Therefore, the paper is strong but could benefit from further exploration in diverse contexts to enhance its significance and applicability. **Score: 8**   This score reflects a high level of novelty and the potential for significant influence on the field, tempered by some concerns regarding the inherent limitations of LLMs and the need for wider applicability testing.
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### I Predict Therefore I Am: Is Next Token Prediction Enough to Learn Human-Interpretable Concepts from Data?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08980v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhang Liu, Dong Gong, Erdun Gao, Zhen Zhang, Biwei Huang, Mingming Gong, Anton van den Hengel, Javen Qinfeng Shi
- **Abstract**: The remarkable achievements of large language models (LLMs) have led many to conclude that they exhibit a form of intelligence. This is as opposed to explanations of their capabilities based on their ability to perform relatively simple manipulations of vast volumes of data. To illuminate the distinction between these explanations, we introduce a novel generative model that generates tokens on the basis of human interpretable concepts represented as latent discrete variables. Under mild conditions, even when the mapping from the latent space to the observed space is non-invertible, we establish an identifiability result: the representations learned by LLMs through next-token prediction can be approximately modeled as the logarithm of the posterior probabilities of these latent discrete concepts, up to an invertible linear transformation. This theoretical finding not only provides evidence that LLMs capture underlying generative factors, but also strongly reinforces the linear representation hypothesis, which posits that LLMs learn linear representations of human-interpretable concepts. Empirically, we validate our theoretical results through evaluations on both simulation data and the Pythia, Llama, and DeepSeek model families.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "I Predict Therefore I Am: Is Next Token Prediction Enough to Learn Human-Interpretable Concepts from Data?" explores the intricate relationship between large language models (LLMs) and the learning of human-interpretable concepts. The authors present a new generative model that posits the generation of tokens based on latent discrete variables representing these concepts. They prove that under certain conditions, the representations learned by LLMs—through next-token prediction—can be seen as logarithmic approximations of the posterior probabilities of these latent concepts, albeit linked by an invertible linear transformation. This finding reinforces the idea that LLMs may indeed capture underlying generative factors inherent in data, advocating for the linear representation hypothesis. The paper's findings are supported through empirical evaluations conducted on simulation data as well as on notable model families like Pythia, Llama, and DeepSeek. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance **Strengths:** 1. **Theoretical Contribution:** The paper offers a novel theoretical framework that connects LLMs’ token predictions to human-interpretable concepts through latent variables. This fills a significant gap in understanding how LLMs might encapsulate complex data relationships beyond simple statistical correlations. 2. **Empirical Validation:** By testing their theoretical assertions against established model families and simulations, the authors provide robust evidence for their claims, enhancing the paper’s credibility. 3. **Addressing Interpretability:** The focus on human-interpretable concepts is important for advancing AI interpretability, a critical area of research as LLMs are increasingly used in impactful applications. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Generalizability:** While the theoretical framework is compelling, its applicability across diverse datasets and tasks may need further exploration. LLMs may function in various contexts where linear transformations do not sufficiently capture complex relationships. 2. **Complexity of Real-world Data:** The implications of latent representations might oversimplify the complexities found in real-world applications, potentially leading to limitations in understanding nuanced human concepts. 3. **Impact on the Field:** Although the findings are intriguing, they may not radically change practices or lead to immediate practical applications in existing technologies, as the focus remains largely theoretical. **Conclusion:** Overall, the paper presents a thoughtful exploration of LLMs through a new lens of human-interpretable concepts and grounding in theory. The findings hold promise for future research directions, particularly around interpretability in AI. However, concerns about generalizability and practical impact limit its immediate transformative potential in the field. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper's solid contributions, both theoretically and empirically, while also acknowledging the need for further development in real-world applications and the breadth of its theoretical implications.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### JBFuzz: Jailbreaking LLMs Efficiently and Effectively Using Fuzzing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08990v1)
- **Authors**: Vasudev Gohil
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown great promise as language understanding and decision making tools, and they have permeated various aspects of our everyday life. However, their widespread availability also comes with novel risks, such as generating harmful, unethical, or offensive content, via an attack called jailbreaking. Despite extensive efforts from LLM developers to align LLMs using human feedback, they are still susceptible to jailbreak attacks. To tackle this issue, researchers often employ red-teaming to understand and investigate jailbreak prompts. However, existing red-teaming approaches lack effectiveness, scalability, or both. To address these issues, we propose JBFuzz, a novel effective, automated, and scalable red-teaming technique for jailbreaking LLMs. JBFuzz is inspired by the success of fuzzing for detecting bugs/vulnerabilities in software. We overcome three challenges related to effectiveness and scalability by devising novel seed prompts, a lightweight mutation engine, and a lightweight and accurate evaluator for guiding the fuzzer. Assimilating all three solutions results in a potent fuzzer that only requires black-box access to the target LLM. We perform extensive experimental evaluation of JBFuzz using nine popular and widely-used LLMs. We find that JBFuzz successfully jailbreaks all LLMs for various harmful/unethical questions, with an average attack success rate of 99%. We also find that JBFuzz is extremely efficient as it jailbreaks a given LLM for a given question in 60 seconds on average. Our work highlights the susceptibility of the state-of-the-art LLMs to jailbreak attacks even after safety alignment, and serves as a valuable red-teaming tool for LLM developers.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper presents "JBFuzz," a novel automated and scalable technique for evaluating the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to jailbreaking attacks, which allow them to generate harmful or unethical content. The authors argue that while LLMs have made significant strides in language understanding, they remain susceptible to these attacks, despite various alignment efforts. Current red-teaming methods for testing LLM security are found to be ineffective or unscalable. JBFuzz is inspired by software fuzzing, incorporating innovative seed prompts, a lightweight mutation engine, and a streamlined evaluator. JBFuzz has been tested on nine popular LLMs, achieving a remarkable 99% success rate in jailbreaking attempts within an average time of 60 seconds. The research underscores ongoing vulnerabilities in LLMs, even when subjected to safety alignment measures, and serves as a crucial tool for developers working on LLM safety. --- **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty:** The paper introduces an innovative approach to red-teaming LLMs by leveraging principles from software fuzzing, which is a unique angle in the context of AI safety testing. While fuzzing has been established for bug detection in software, its application within LLMs highlights a significant gap in previous methodologies. This shift in perspective contributes a fresh and much-needed tool to the arsenal against LLM vulnerabilities. **Significance:** The implications of the work are substantial, considering the increasing reliance on LLMs in various applications. By demonstrating the persistent susceptibility of state-of-the-art models to jailbreaking, the study calls attention to critical aspects of AI safety and ethics. Moreover, the creation of a tool that facilitates rapid and effective testing of LLMs could prompt developers to reconsider their models' alignment strategies and safety protocols, enhancing the overall robustness of these systems. **Strengths:** 1. **Practical Application:** The JBFuzz method is not only automated but also efficient, allowing rapid testing which is vital in fast-paced development environments. 2. **Strong Results:** The reported success rate (99%) and speed (60 seconds per attempt) indicate a well-designed and effective tool. 3. **Comprehensive Testing:** Evaluation across nine LLMs adds to the robustness of the findings and addresses the general applicability of JBFuzz. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Potential Overreliance on Black-Box Testing:** While black-box access is a significant advantage, it may limit the ability to understand deeper vulnerabilities within LLM architectures. 2. **Ethical Considerations:** The emphasis on demonstrating jailbreaking capabilities may raise ethical dilemmas regarding the promotion of such vulnerabilities, although the authors do contextualize the need for safety assessments. 3. **Assumption of Harmful Content Generation:** The focus on harmful/unethical prompts may neglect the broader range of use cases; highlighting only the negative aspects could skew further research in this area. **Influence on the Field:** JBFuzz has the potential to significantly influence future research in AI safety and security. Its automated nature may encourage more researchers and developers to incorporate systematic testing into their workflows, ultimately fostering a safer deployment of LLMs. However, ethical considerations around the misuse of such tools must also be addressed within the community. Given these considerations, I assign the paper a score of **8**. This score reflects its innovative approach and substantial findings while recognizing some limitations in ethical implications and the scope of application. The balance of strengths and weaknesses positions the paper as a noteworthy contribution to the ongoing discourse on LLM safety.  **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Unified Locomotion Transformer with Simultaneous Sim-to-Real Transfer for Quadrupeds
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08997v1)
- **Authors**: Dikai Liu, Tianwei Zhang, Jianxiong Yin, Simon See
- **Abstract**: Quadrupeds have gained rapid advancement in their capability of traversing across complex terrains. The adoption of deep Reinforcement Learning (RL), transformers and various knowledge transfer techniques can greatly reduce the sim-to-real gap. However, the classical teacher-student framework commonly used in existing locomotion policies requires a pre-trained teacher and leverages the privilege information to guide the student policy. With the implementation of large-scale models in robotics controllers, especially transformers-based ones, this knowledge distillation technique starts to show its weakness in efficiency, due to the requirement of multiple supervised stages. In this paper, we propose Unified Locomotion Transformer (ULT), a new transformer-based framework to unify the processes of knowledge transfer and policy optimization in a single network while still taking advantage of privilege information. The policies are optimized with reinforcement learning, next state-action prediction, and action imitation, all in just one training stage, to achieve zero-shot deployment. Evaluation results demonstrate that with ULT, optimal teacher and student policies can be obtained at the same time, greatly easing the difficulty in knowledge transfer, even with complex transformer-based models.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper: The paper presents the Unified Locomotion Transformer (ULT), a novel transformer-based framework designed for quadrupedal locomotion. Traditional approaches in this domain often utilize a teacher-student framework for knowledge transfer, which requires a pre-trained teacher and structured guidance for the student policy. ULT addresses inefficiencies in this method by unifying knowledge transfer and policy optimization within a single network. This framework leverages reinforcement learning, next state-action prediction, and action imitation in one training stage, allowing for zero-shot deployment of quadrupedal locomotion policies. The authors demonstrate through evaluations that ULT can concurrently optimize teacher and student policies, significantly simplifying the complexities involved in knowledge transfer, even with intricate transformer models. ### Critical Evaluation: **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Integration:** The ULT framework's unification of knowledge transfer and policy optimization represents a significant step forward in simplifying the training of locomotion policies, reducing reliance on extensive pre-trained models. 2. **Efficiency in Training:** By achieving zero-shot deployment and consolidating multiple learning paradigms into a single stage, the authors effectively address an existing inefficiency in the traditional teacher-student setup. This could lead to faster and more scalable implementations in real-world robotic applications. 3. **Robust Evaluations:** The results provided suggest substantial improvements in learning capability and deployment readiness, indicating that the method could be effectively applied in diverse terrains that quadrupedal robots may encounter. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Application:** While the approach is promising, the paper might benefit from a broader evaluation across different types of quadrupedal robots, as the robustness of the framework in varied real-world scenarios remains untested. 2. **Dependence on Privileged Information:** Despite claims of ease in knowledge transfer, the reliance on privileged information is still a potential limiting factor; it might restrict wider adoption unless methods for operating in lower information conditions are explored. 3. **Comparative Analysis:** The paper would have been more robust with a comparative analysis against other state-of-the-art models beyond the traditional teacher-student framework, to underscore the significance of improvements brought by ULT. ### Conclusion: In conclusion, the ULT framework introduces a compelling advancement in quadrupedal locomotion and addresses critical inefficiencies in training methods. The novel approach holds promise for practical applications in robotic control, indicating potential influence on both academic research and industry practice. However, without broader testing and a more thorough comparative evaluation, it's difficult to fully assess the long-term impact of the proposed method. Overall, based on the innovative approach and potential significance of the work, I would assign the paper a score of **8**.  Score: 8
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### KNighter: Transforming Static Analysis with LLM-Synthesized Checkers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09002v1)
- **Authors**: Chenyuan Yang, Zijie Zhao, Zichen Xie, Haoyu Li, Lingming Zhang
- **Abstract**: Static analysis is a powerful technique for bug detection in critical systems like operating system kernels. However, designing and implementing static analyzers is challenging, time-consuming, and typically limited to predefined bug patterns. While large language models (LLMs) have shown promise for static analysis, directly applying them to scan large codebases remains impractical due to computational constraints and contextual limitations. We present KNighter, the first approach that unlocks practical LLM-based static analysis by automatically synthesizing static analyzers from historical bug patterns. Rather than using LLMs to directly analyze massive codebases, our key insight is leveraging LLMs to generate specialized static analyzers guided by historical patch knowledge. KNighter implements this vision through a multi-stage synthesis pipeline that validates checker correctness against original patches and employs an automated refinement process to iteratively reduce false positives. Our evaluation on the Linux kernel demonstrates that KNighter generates high-precision checkers capable of detecting diverse bug patterns overlooked by existing human-written analyzers. To date, KNighter-synthesized checkers have discovered 70 new bugs/vulnerabilities in the Linux kernel, with 56 confirmed and 41 already fixed. 11 of these findings have been assigned CVE numbers. This work establishes an entirely new paradigm for scalable, reliable, and traceable LLM-based static analysis for real-world systems via checker synthesis.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper titled "KNighter: Transforming Static Analysis with LLM-Synthesized Checkers" introduces an innovative approach to enhance static analysis, particularly in critical systems such as operating system kernels. It identifies the limitations of traditional static analyzers, which are often constrained by their predefined patterns and resource-intensive requirements. To address these challenges, the authors propose KNighter, a system that utilizes large language models (LLMs) to automate the synthesis of static analyzers based on historical bug patterns. Rather than applying LLMs directly to codebases, KNighter generates tailored checkers by drawing on prior patches and incorporates a multi-stage synthesis pipeline aimed at validating checker correctness and reducing false positives systematically. Evaluation results reveal that KNighter has successfully uncovered numerous new bugs in the Linux kernel, suggesting its effectiveness in augmenting existing analysis tools. The paper delineates a new framework for scalable static analysis, affirming the significance of LLMs in synthesizing adaptable and precise checkers. --- **Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:** 1. **Novelty**: The core innovation presented in KNighter lies in its methodology of leveraging LLMs to synthesize static analyzers rather than simply employing them for direct code analysis. By focusing on synthesizing checkers from historical data, the paper addresses a significant gap in the traditional static analysis workflow that has not been deeply explored before. This novel approach is likely to inspire further research into automated synthesis techniques, not just for static analysis, but possibly for other facets of software engineering. 2. **Significance**: The impact of KNighter is evident in its practical application—the discovery of 70 new bugs and vulnerabilities in the Linux kernel. This not only demonstrates the immediate effectiveness of their approach but also emphasizes its potential real-world applicability. By validating the synthesized checkers against established patches, KNighter enhances the reliability and traceability of the analysis, which could lead to more robust software systems. 3. **Strengths**:     - The paper presents a well-structured methodology, including a clear description of the multi-stage synthesis pipeline and its advantages in refining static analyzers.    - Empirical results validate the effectiveness of the proposed system, showcasing its capability to detect bugs often missed by traditional analyzers.    - The work opens doors for further exploration in automated synthesis and the integration of LLMs into software engineering practices. 4. **Weaknesses**:     - While the paper demonstrates success in the Linux kernel context, further evaluation in diverse environments and codebases would strengthen the generalizability of their findings.    - The reliance on historical bug patterns may introduce a bias based on previously encountered issues, potentially limiting the discovery of novel bug types.    - Computational constraints were briefly mentioned, but a more detailed discussion of how these were addressed through the synthesis process would have been valuable. **Potential Influence**: Given the rapid advancements in LLM technology and the increasing need for effective static analysis tools, KNighter could potentially redefine practices within the field. It sets a precedent for future tools that prioritize synthesis over direct deployment of large models, pushing the envelope on how static analysis can evolve in line with machine learning techniques. **Score**: 8 This score reflects the paper's notable novelty in outlining a workable framework for LLM-driven static analysis, alongside robust empirical results. However, the need for broader applicability and addressing inherent biases in bug detection limit its score from reaching the highest tier of impact.
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 0/10

### Leveraging Retrieval Augmented Generative LLMs For Automated Metadata Description Generation to Enhance Data Catalogs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09003v1)
- **Authors**: Mayank Singh, Abhijeet Kumar, Sasidhar Donaparthi, Gayatri Karambelkar
- **Abstract**: Data catalogs serve as repositories for organizing and accessing diverse collection of data assets, but their effectiveness hinges on the ease with which business users can look-up relevant content. Unfortunately, many data catalogs within organizations suffer from limited searchability due to inadequate metadata like asset descriptions. Hence, there is a need of content generation solution to enrich and curate metadata in a scalable way. This paper explores the challenges associated with metadata creation and proposes a unique prompt enrichment idea of leveraging existing metadata content using retrieval based few-shot technique tied with generative large language models (LLM). The literature also considers finetuning an LLM on existing content and studies the behavior of few-shot pretrained LLM (Llama, GPT3.5) vis-\`a-vis few-shot finetuned LLM (Llama2-7b) by evaluating their performance based on accuracy, factual grounding, and toxicity. Our preliminary results exhibit more than 80% Rouge-1 F1 for the generated content. This implied 87%- 88% of instances accepted as is or curated with minor edits by data stewards. By automatically generating descriptions for tables and columns in most accurate way, the research attempts to provide an overall framework for enterprises to effectively scale metadata curation and enrich its data catalog thereby vastly improving the data catalog searchability and overall usability.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper investigates the challenges associated with the generation of metadata in data catalogs, which are crucial for ensuring that business users can easily find relevant data assets. It identifies the shortcomings of existing metadata, particularly in terms of quality and completeness, which hinder searchability. To address this, the authors propose a method that combines retrieval-augmented techniques with generative large language models (LLMs) to enhance metadata descriptions in a scalable manner. They analyze the performance of various few-shot pretrained and fine-tuned LLMs (Llama, GPT3.5, and Llama2-7b), measuring outcomes based on accuracy, factual grounding, and the presence of toxicity in generated metadata. Their preliminary findings demonstrate that the generated content achieves over 80% Rouge-1 F1 scores, with a significant percentage of generated instances being accepted or requiring only minimal edits from data stewards. The approach aims to automate the metadata generation process, thereby improving the usability and searchability of data catalogs in enterprises. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper addresses a significant issue in data management—metadata creation—that affects the usability of data catalogs, essential tools for data governance and access. The integration of retrieval-augmented techniques seems novel, and employing state-of-the-art LLMs to automate the generation of metadata is certainly a timely contribution given the current advancements in artificial intelligence. **Strengths:** 1. **Relevance:** The problem it addresses is highly relevant to organizations managing large datasets. Improving metadata quality can vastly enhance data discovery and usability. 2. **Methodological Innovation:** Combining retrieval methods with generative LLMs showcases a well-thought-out approach to scaling metadata generation, which is a unique angle in this domain. 3. **Quantitative Results:** The paper provides robust preliminary results, demonstrating that the proposed system can generate usable content, and the high Rouge-1 F1 scores indicate that it meets a necessary accuracy standard. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the results are promising, the paper would benefit from a broader evaluation involving more diverse datasets and use cases to fully assess generalizability. 2. **Lack of Comparative Analysis:** There is limited discussion on how their approach compares to existing metadata generation methods beyond the model types. This context is essential for understanding the true innovation and limitations of the proposal. 3. **Potential Bias and Toxicity Concerns:** While the paper mentions evaluating for toxicity, details on how these measures were implemented and their implications for real-world applications warrant further exploration. In conclusion, the paper contributes valuable insights into enhancing metadata creation through advanced AI techniques and presents a coherent framework. However, the limitations in empirical scope and comparative analysis indicate that further work is needed to consolidate its impact and adoption in practice. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper's strong relevance and innovative application of LLMs to a pressing issue in data cataloging, balanced by the need for further validation and comparison with existing methods. The work demonstrates potential but would benefit from deeper exploration and wider applicability to achieve a higher impact.
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Quantifying Long-Range Interactions in Graph Machine Learning: a Large Graph Dataset and a Measurement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09008v1)
- **Authors**: Huidong Liang, Haitz Sáez de Ocáriz Borde, Baskaran Sripathmanathan, Michael Bronstein, Xiaowen Dong
- **Abstract**: Long-range dependencies are critical for effective graph representation learning, yet most existing datasets focus on small graphs tailored to inductive tasks, offering limited insight into long-range interactions. Current evaluations primarily compare models employing global attention (e.g., graph transformers) with those using local neighborhood aggregation (e.g., message-passing neural networks) without a direct measurement of long-range dependency. In this work, we introduce City-Networks, a novel large-scale transductive learning dataset derived from real-world city roads. This dataset features graphs with over $10^5$ nodes and significantly larger diameters than those in existing benchmarks, naturally embodying long-range information. We annotate the graphs using an eccentricity-based approach, ensuring that the classification task inherently requires information from distant nodes. Furthermore, we propose a model-agnostic measurement based on the Jacobians of neighbors from distant hops, offering a principled quantification of long-range dependencies. Finally, we provide theoretical justifications for both our dataset design and the proposed measurement - particularly by focusing on over-smoothing and influence score dilution - which establishes a robust foundation for further exploration of long-range interactions in graph neural networks.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "Towards Quantifying Long-Range Interactions in Graph Machine Learning: a Large Graph Dataset and a Measurement" addresses the importance of long-range dependencies in graph representation learning, which are often overlooked in current datasets focused on smaller graphs. The authors introduce City-Networks, a large-scale dataset constructed from real-world city road networks, consisting of graphs with over 100,000 nodes and larger diameters, reflecting significant long-range interactions. The dataset is annotated through an eccentricity-based method to ensure that global information is essential for classification tasks. Additionally, the authors propose a model-agnostic measurement approach that leverages the Jacobians of distant neighbors, aiming to quantitate long-range dependencies in graph neural networks (GNNs). They provide theoretical justifications for their methodology, particularly addressing issues of over-smoothing and influence score dilution that may affect the performance of GNNs. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of the paper lies in its dual contributions: the introduction of a large-scale dataset specifically designed to facilitate the study of long-range interactions in GNNs, and a principled method of measuring these interactions. While there are previous works exploring long-range dependencies, the paper distinguishes itself by focusing on real-world applications and providing a robust framework for evaluating the effectiveness of different graph learning models in utilizing long-range information. Strengths: 1. **Dataset Novelty**: City-Networks fills a significant gap by providing a large-scale dataset that can support extensive research on long-range graph interactions, which testing on smaller datasets has historically hindered. 2. **Methodological Rigor**: The eccentricity-based annotation and Jacobian measurement offer an innovative approach to understanding dependencies that can influence future research directions. 3. **Theoretical Justifications**: The discussion around over-smoothing and influence score dilution adds depth to their contributions and informs the community about common pitfalls in GNN evaluation. Weaknesses: 1. **Empirical Validation**: While the theoretical framework is strong, the paper would benefit from extensive empirical validation, showcasing how different models perform on this dataset. 2. **Comparison Depth**: The evaluation primarily contrasts global attention mechanisms with local aggregation. More comprehensive comparisons with existing models and methods could enrich the findings. 3. **Potential Overfitting**: The dataset's focus on city roads may lead to overfitting issues for models designed to generalize across diverse graph structures. Overall, this work provides a significant advance in the domain of graph machine learning and adequately addresses key issues regarding long-range dependencies. The introduction of both a dataset and measurement technique is poised to generate valuable research, encouraging more thorough examinations of graph neural networks. **Score: 8** This score reflects the paper's potential impact in fostering new research initiatives while acknowledging the need for further empirical validation and model comparison that could enhance the robustness of its claims.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enhancing High-Quality Code Generation in Large Language Models with Comparative Prefix-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09020v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan Jiang, Yujian Zhang, Liang Lu, Christoph Treude, Xiaohong Su, Shan Huang, Tiantian Wang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have been widely adopted in commercial code completion engines, significantly enhancing coding efficiency and productivity. However, LLMs may generate code with quality issues that violate coding standards and best practices, such as poor code style and maintainability, even when the code is functionally correct. This necessitates additional effort from developers to improve the code, potentially negating the efficiency gains provided by LLMs. To address this problem, we propose a novel comparative prefix-tuning method for controllable high-quality code generation. Our method introduces a single, property-specific prefix that is prepended to the activations of the LLM, serving as a lightweight alternative to fine-tuning. Unlike existing methods that require training multiple prefixes, our approach trains only one prefix and leverages pairs of high-quality and low-quality code samples, introducing a sequence-level ranking loss to guide the model's training. This comparative approach enables the model to better understand the differences between high-quality and low-quality code, focusing on aspects that impact code quality. Additionally, we design a data construction pipeline to collect and annotate pairs of high-quality and low-quality code, facilitating effective training. Extensive experiments on the Code Llama 7B model demonstrate that our method improves code quality by over 100% in certain task categories, while maintaining functional correctness. We also conduct ablation studies and generalization experiments, confirming the effectiveness of our method's components and its strong generalization capability.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents a novel method called "comparative prefix-tuning" aimed at enhancing code quality generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) in code completion tasks. The authors recognize that while LLMs can provide functionally correct code, they often generate low-quality outputs that do not meet coding standards, which can negate their utility. The proposed method uses a single property-specific prefix to train the model, differentiating high-quality from low-quality code samples through a sequence-level ranking loss. This method avoids the need for extensive fine-tuning by focusing only on a single prefix and pairs of code snippets. The authors implement a data pipeline for collecting and annotating these pairs, and the results from experiments with the Code Llama 7B model indicate over 100% improvement in code quality across various tasks without sacrificing functional correctness. Ablation studies demonstrate the effectiveness of the method's components and its generalization capabilities. **Critical Evaluation:** **Strengths:** 1. **Novel Approach:** The comparative prefix-tuning represents a unique solution within the domain of code generation, addressing a pertinent issue—code quality. By emphasizing the comparison between high- and low-quality code, the method could help develop models that produce not only functional code but also maintainability.    2. **Efficiency:** The decision to utilize a single prefix rather than multiple prefixes reduces the complexity and resource requirements for training, making this approach more feasible for practical applications without extensive computational costs. 3. **Empirical Validation:** The reported experiments showing over 100% improvement in specific task categories lend credibility to the authors’ claims and signify that this method can yield significant practical benefits. 4. **Generalization:** The experiments evaluating generalization suggest that the method has the potential to be applied across different contexts, which is vital for real-world software development scenarios. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Comparison:** While the study presents improvement, it would benefit from a more robust comparison against existing state-of-the-art methods, particularly in diverse coding environments and various programming languages. Stating how this approaches compares with extreme fine-tuning methods could provide context for its benefits. 2. **Scope of Quality Definition:** The paper needs to define what constitutes "quality" more explicitly. Further exploration of various quality dimensions (e.g., performance, readability, maintainability) would enhance the understanding of how the proposed method accounts for diverse coding standards. 3. **Data Construction Pipeline:** While a data pipeline for collecting and annotating code pairs is mentioned, there could be more elaboration on the methodology and criteria used for quality assessment. This is crucial as it affects the integrity of the training data and consequently the robustness of the learned features. **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field significantly, particularly in generating high-quality code through LLMs. If adopted widely, the method could reduce the burden of post-generation code modification, streamlining the software development process. **Score: 8** The score reflects the paper's innovative contributions and practical applications while acknowledging that further elaboration and comparison against existing methodologies would strengthen its impact. Overall, it is a solid contribution to the field of AI-assisted coding.
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Prompt Inversion Attack against Collaborative Inference of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09022v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjie Qu, Yuguang Zhou, Yongji Wu, Tingsong Xiao, Binhang Yuan, Yiming Li, Jiaheng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have been widely applied for their remarkable capability of content generation. However, the practical use of open-source LLMs is hindered by high resource requirements, making deployment expensive and limiting widespread development. The collaborative inference is a promising solution for this problem, in which users collaborate by each hosting a subset of layers and transmitting intermediate activation. Many companies are building collaborative inference platforms to reduce LLM serving costs, leveraging users' underutilized GPUs. Despite widespread interest in collaborative inference within academia and industry, the privacy risks associated with LLM collaborative inference have not been well studied. This is largely because of the challenge posed by inverting LLM activation due to its strong non-linearity. In this paper, to validate the severity of privacy threats in LLM collaborative inference, we introduce the concept of prompt inversion attack (PIA), where a malicious participant intends to recover the input prompt through the activation transmitted by its previous participant. Extensive experiments show that our PIA method substantially outperforms existing baselines. For example, our method achieves an 88.4\% token accuracy on the Skytrax dataset with the Llama-65B model when inverting the maximum number of transformer layers, while the best baseline method only achieves 22.8\% accuracy. The results verify the effectiveness of our PIA attack and highlights its practical threat to LLM collaborative inference systems.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper "Prompt Inversion Attack against Collaborative Inference of Large Language Models" addresses a significant privacy concern in collaborative inference systems employing large language models (LLMs). While collaborative inference offers a way to mitigate resource constraints by distributing LLM computations across multiple users, it does not adequately address the privacy implications of sharing intermediate activations. The authors propose a novel attack, termed the prompt inversion attack (PIA), aimed at recovering the original input prompt from the shared activation data. Through empirical experiments using the Llama-65B model, the PIA achieves a token accuracy of 88.4% on the Skytrax dataset, significantly outperforming existing methods, which only reach 22.8% accuracy. The findings not only highlight the vulnerability of collaborative inference platforms but also serve as a call to action for better privacy safeguards in LLM deployment. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty**: The introduction of the prompt inversion attack (PIA) contributes a new perspective on the privacy vulnerabilities in collaborative inference systems. Previous works have primarily focused on the functional advantages of collaborative inference but largely overlooked privacy risks. The concept of inverting the activations to reconstruct input prompts is a novel approach that adds valuable insight into the security landscape of LLMs. **Significance**: As LLMs become more prominent in various applications, addressing their security and privacy concerns is becoming increasingly vital. This paper tackles an emerging threat that could have serious implications for data privacy in user interactions with LLM systems. The demonstrated effectiveness of the PIA signal a pressing need for the development of protective measures, thereby establishing the paper’s relevance in a rapidly evolving field. **Strengths**: 1. **Innovative Approach**: The introduction of the PIA provides a new direction for research on privacy in collaborative LLM usage. 2. **Robust Testing**: The extensive experimentation validates the proposed attack's effectiveness, offering concrete evidence of privacy risks. 3. **Practical Implications**: The findings could influence future designs and regulations surrounding collaborative inference systems. **Weaknesses**:  1. **Limited Scope**: The focus on only one type of model (Llama-65B) may not generalize across all LLMs. Broader testing with various architectures could strengthen the results. 2. **Assumptions**: The paper does not deeply explore the assumptions regarding user behavior and system architecture that may affect the feasibility of the PIA. 3. **Mitigation Strategies**: While it highlights the issue, the absence of proposed mitigation strategies leaves a gap for practitioners and researchers who need guidance on how to counter the presented vulnerabilities. **Overall Assessment**: The paper is pioneering in addressing a gap concerning privacy in collaborative inference using LLMs. It successfully demonstrates significant privacy risks through a well-executed attack, thereby contributing substantially to the literature. However, a thorough exploration of different models and potential solutions would enhance its impact. **Score**: 8
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 0/10

### Aligning to What? Limits to RLHF Based Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09025v1)
- **Authors**: Logan Barnhart, Reza Akbarian Bafghi, Stephen Becker, Maziar Raissi
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) is increasingly used to align large language models (LLMs) with human preferences. However, the effectiveness of RLHF in addressing underlying biases remains unclear. This study investigates the relationship between RLHF and both covert and overt biases in LLMs, particularly focusing on biases against African Americans. We applied various RLHF techniques (DPO, ORPO, and RLOO) to Llama 3 8B and evaluated the covert and overt biases of the resulting models using matched-guise probing and explicit bias testing. We performed additional tests with DPO on different base models and datasets; among several implications, we found that SFT before RLHF calcifies model biases. Additionally, we extend the tools for measuring biases to multi-modal models. Through our experiments we collect evidence that indicates that current alignment techniques are inadequate for nebulous tasks such as mitigating covert biases, highlighting the need for capable datasets, data curating techniques, or alignment tools.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "Aligning to What? Limits to RLHF Based Alignment" explores the effectiveness of Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) in aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences, particularly in mitigating biases. Specifically, the authors investigate overt and covert biases in LLMs, focusing on biases against African Americans. They implement various RLHF techniques—DPO, ORPO, and RLOO—on Llama 3 8B and assess the models' biases through matched-guise probing and explicit bias testing. Their findings suggest that applying Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) prior to RLHF solidifies existing biases and that current alignment methods are insufficient for addressing hidden biases in LLMs. Furthermore, they propose improvements in dataset curation and alignment tools, and extend bias measurement techniques to multi-modal models. ### Critical Evaluation: **Novelty and Significance:** The paper addresses critical issues of bias in LLMs, an increasingly relevant topic as such models are deployed more widely in society. The focus on the interrelation between RLHF techniques and model biases is a novel approach that adds depth to the ongoing discourse about alignment strategies. By using Llama 3 8B as a testbed and evaluating various RLHF techniques, the paper offers empirical evidence that builds on existing literature, which has often superficially addressed biases without delving deeply into the mechanics of how RLHF may perpetuate them. **Strengths:** - **Robust Methodology:** The use of multiple RLHF techniques and thorough testing across different models strengthens the validity of the findings. - **Focused Examination of Specific Biases:** Concentrating on biases against African Americans allows the authors to make targeted claims, making their results more impactful. - **Practical Implications:** The recommendations for improved datasets and alignment tools contribute constructively to potential future research and applications. **Weaknesses:** - **Limited Scope of Bias Analysis:** While the focus on specific biases is commendable, the examination could be broadened to consider other demographics or bias types for a more comprehensive perspective. - **Dependence on Llama 3 8B:** While the choice of model is justified, its singular focus may limit the generalizability of the findings across other LLMs. - **Theoretical Insights:** The paper could benefit from a more detailed theoretical discussion on why certain RLHF techniques may exacerbate biases, potentially guiding future research direction more clearly. **Impact on the Field:** The implications of this study are significant, as it raises awareness about potential shortcomings of existing RLHF methods in the practical alignment of LLMs with human values, particularly in relation to biases. These insights highlight the importance of refining the tools and strategies used to align models with human preferences. ### Score: 8 This score reflects the paper's strong contribution to understanding the limitations of RLHF in bias mitigation and its practical recommendations for future work, while acknowledging areas for broader applications and deeper theoretical insights.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### DAST: Difficulty-Aware Self-Training on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09029v1)
- **Authors**: Boyang Xue, Qi Zhu, Hongru Wang, Rui Wang, Sheng Wang, Hongling Xu, Fei Mi, Yasheng Wang, Lifeng Shang, Qun Liu, Kam-Fai Wong
- **Abstract**: Present Large Language Models (LLM) self-training methods always under-sample on challenging queries, leading to inadequate learning on difficult problems which limits LLMs' ability. Therefore, this work proposes a difficulty-aware self-training (DAST) framework that focuses on improving both the quantity and quality of self-generated responses on challenging queries during self-training. DAST is specified in three components: 1) sampling-based difficulty level estimation, 2) difficulty-aware data augmentation, and 3) the self-training algorithm using SFT and DPO respectively. Experiments on mathematical tasks demonstrate the effectiveness and generalization of DAST, highlighting the critical role of difficulty-aware strategies in advancing LLM self-training.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents a novel framework called Difficulty-Aware Self-Training (DAST) aimed at enhancing the self-training process for Large Language Models (LLMs) by addressing the under-sampling of difficult queries. DAST incorporates three key components: 1) a methodology for estimating difficulty levels based on sampling, 2) strategies for augmenting data considering the identified difficulty, and 3) a self-training algorithm that combines Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). Experiments centered on mathematical tasks illustrate the framework's strength in improving both the quantity and quality of self-generated responses to challenging queries, underscoring the importance of difficulty-aware approaches for advancing LLM capabilities. **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty**: The idea of utilizing difficulty-aware strategies in self-training for LLMs is relatively novel and contributes to the ongoing dialogues surrounding model training enhancements. While there have been discussions around the selection and quality of training data, the specific focus on difficulty levels is less common and fills a crucial gap in the self-training literature. **Significance**: The identified problem of under-sampling difficult queries represents a legitimate limitation in current self-training approaches. By addressing this, DAST could lead to substantial improvements in LLM performance, especially in application areas that require higher reasoning abilities (e.g., mathematics). The empirical demonstration of DAST's effectiveness also lends weight to its proposed methodology, indicating its potential impact on the field. **Strengths**: 1. **Innovative Approach**: The focus on difficulty estimation and targeted data augmentation is a well-considered method that could enhance training effectiveness dramatically. 2. **Empirical Validation**: The experiments conducted provide a strong basis for claims regarding DAST's efficacy, which is essential for establishing credibility in research. **Weaknesses**: 1. **Scope of Evaluation**: The experiments are primarily based on mathematical tasks, which may limit the generalizability of the findings. Additional evaluations across diverse datasets and tasks would strengthen the paper's claims. 2. **Potential Complexity**: The proposed framework may introduce additional complexity in the training pipeline, which could be a hurdle for practitioners who may need to balance performance improvements against implementation difficulty. **Conclusion**: Overall, the DAST framework offers valuable insights into improving LLM self-training through difficulty-aware strategies. However, its true impact will depend on further validation across a wider range of tasks and real-world applications. The innovative approach and initial empirical success support a positive assessment, though broader applicability remains to be seen. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper's strong potential contributions and methodological rigor while acknowledging the need for broader validation and possible challenges in practical implementation.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Teaching LLMs How to Learn with Contextual Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09032v1)
- **Authors**: Younwoo Choi, Muhammad Adil Asif, Ziwen Han, John Willes, Rahul G. Krishnan
- **Abstract**: Prompting Large Language Models (LLMs), or providing context on the expected model of operation, is an effective way to steer the outputs of such models to satisfy human desiderata after they have been trained. But in rapidly evolving domains, there is often need to fine-tune LLMs to improve either the kind of knowledge in their memory or their abilities to perform open ended reasoning in new domains. When human's learn new concepts, we often do so by linking the new material that we are studying to concepts we have already learned before. To that end, we ask, "can prompting help us teach LLMs how to learn". In this work, we study a novel generalization of instruction tuning, called contextual fine-tuning, to fine-tune LLMs. Our method leverages instructional prompts designed to mimic human cognitive strategies in learning and problem-solving to guide the learning process during training, aiming to improve the model's interpretation and understanding of domain-specific knowledge. We empirically demonstrate that this simple yet effective modification improves the ability of LLMs to be fine-tuned rapidly on new datasets both within the medical and financial domains.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Teaching LLMs How to Learn with Contextual Fine-Tuning" explores a method called contextual fine-tuning that aims to enhance the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in rapidly evolving domains. The authors propose that traditional prompting techniques can be improved by incorporating human cognitive strategies—specifically, linking new knowledge with existing concepts. This is framed as a form of instructional learning that allows LLMs to adapt their understanding of domain-specific knowledge more effectively. The empirical results presented demonstrate a significant improvement in the models' ability to fine-tune quickly on new datasets, particularly in medical and financial sectors. **Critical Evaluation:** 1. **Novelty:** The paper introduces the concept of contextual fine-tuning, which is rooted in cognitive strategies. While the idea of adapting LLMs using prompts is not new, the specific approach of mimicking human learning patterns adds a distinct twist to existing techniques. This contributes an innovative perspective to the field of instruction tuning. However, the core idea relies on well-established learning principles, which may limit perceived novelty. 2. **Significance:** Given the rapid evolution of knowledge in various fields, the capability of LLMs to efficiently update their knowledge and reasoning abilities is crucial. The empirical evidence provided supports the notion that incorporating cognitive strategies can significantly enhance the fine-tuning process, making this work relevant and timely. This could encourage further research in applying cognitive principles to AI training methods, thereby influencing future practices within the domain. 3. **Strengths:**     - The paper is clear and well-structured, making complex ideas accessible.    - Empirical validation across two critical domains (medical and financial) strengthens the argument for practical applicability and relevance.    - The concept of contextual fine-tuning is a refreshing approach that has potential for broader implications in LLM adaptation. 4. **Weaknesses:**    - The paper lacks extensive theoretical grounding that explains why the proposed method is more effective than existing approaches.    - A broader examination of limitations or challenges in applying contextual fine-tuning outside the tested domains could provide a more comprehensive perspective.    - It would benefit from comparative benchmarks against other recent state-of-the-art methods to demonstrate clear advantages. Overall, the paper outlines a valuable and innovative approach in fine-tuning LLMs, coupled with empirical support. However, while noteworthy, it does not fully break radical ground as some aspects remain within existing paradigms. Given these considerations, I rate the paper as follows: **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Discovering Influential Neuron Path in Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09046v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Wang, Yifei Liu, Yingdong Shi, Changming Li, Anqi Pang, Sibei Yang, Jingyi Yu, Kan Ren
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformer models exhibit immense power yet remain opaque to human understanding, posing challenges and risks for practical applications. While prior research has attempted to demystify these models through input attribution and neuron role analysis, there's been a notable gap in considering layer-level information and the holistic path of information flow across layers. In this paper, we investigate the significance of influential neuron paths within vision Transformers, which is a path of neurons from the model input to output that impacts the model inference most significantly. We first propose a joint influence measure to assess the contribution of a set of neurons to the model outcome. And we further provide a layer-progressive neuron locating approach that efficiently selects the most influential neuron at each layer trying to discover the crucial neuron path from input to output within the target model. Our experiments demonstrate the superiority of our method finding the most influential neuron path along which the information flows, over the existing baseline solutions. Additionally, the neuron paths have illustrated that vision Transformers exhibit some specific inner working mechanism for processing the visual information within the same image category. We further analyze the key effects of these neurons on the image classification task, showcasing that the found neuron paths have already preserved the model capability on downstream tasks, which may also shed some lights on real-world applications like model pruning. The project website including implementation code is available at https://foundation-model-research.github.io/NeuronPath/.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "Discovering Influential Neuron Path in Vision Transformers" addresses the challenge of understanding the decision-making processes within Vision Transformer models, which are powerful yet difficult to interpret. The authors identify a gap in existing research regarding the assessment of neuron influence at the layer level and propose a novel method for pinpointing the critical paths of neuron influence from model input to output. They introduce a joint influence measure to quantify the overall contribution of groups of neurons and a layer-progressive approach to identify the most impactful neurons at each layer. Experimental results indicate that their methodology outperforms existing baseline solutions in mapping the influential neuron paths. The findings suggest that these paths reveal distinct mechanisms in processing visual information, benefiting tasks like image classification and model pruning. The research encourages further exploration of model interpretability and practical functionalities. --- **Critical Evaluation:** 1. **Novelty**: The paper presents a meaningful advancement in the interpretability of Vision Transformers by focusing on neuron paths, which is a less explored aspect compared to existing techniques primarily centered around input attribution. While previous efforts have concentrated on individual neuron roles or input features, this paper's approach to assess collective neuron influence across layers offers a fresh perspective. However, the concept of examining paths in neural networks itself isn't entirely new, as similar analyses have been conducted in other architectures, which slightly diminishes its novelty. 2. **Methodology**: The proposed joint influence measure and layer-progressive neuron locating method are well-articulated, and the experimental results validating these methods demonstrate substantial improvements over baseline approaches. Still, one potential weakness is the lack of a comparative analysis across a broader set of architectures beyond Vision Transformers, which could provide context for the generalizability of the findings. 3. **Significance**: The implications of discovering influential neuron paths extend beyond understanding the workings of Vision Transformers. They may have significant ramifications for model optimization practices such as pruning, which can enhance performance in real-world applications. However, the exploration of practical implementations and how they can be integrated into existing frameworks and workflows remains under-addressed. 4. **Clarity and Presentation**: The paper is clearly written, and the logic flows well from motivation through methodology to results. However, the complexity of visualizing neuron paths and their actual impact on model output could benefit from additional illustrative examples or case studies, potentially enhancing reader comprehension. 5. **Impact on Field**: While the findings are insightful and pave the way for further research into model transparency, the overall impact may be moderated by how widely this approach can be adopted across various architectures. Broader applicability and accessibility of the methods presented in practical settings would strengthen the contribution. Given these considerations, the paper demonstrates solid research with a thoughtful approach to a relevant problem. It innovates in the area of interpretability in deep learning models but is limited in scope regarding applied contexts and breadth of architecture comparisons.  **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Probing Latent Subspaces in LLM for AI Security: Identifying and Manipulating Adversarial States
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09066v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Wei Chia, Jonathan Pan
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities across various tasks, yet they remain vulnerable to adversarial manipulations such as jailbreaking via prompt injection attacks. These attacks bypass safety mechanisms to generate restricted or harmful content. In this study, we investigated the underlying latent subspaces of safe and jailbroken states by extracting hidden activations from a LLM. Inspired by attractor dynamics in neuroscience, we hypothesized that LLM activations settle into semi stable states that can be identified and perturbed to induce state transitions. Using dimensionality reduction techniques, we projected activations from safe and jailbroken responses to reveal latent subspaces in lower dimensional spaces. We then derived a perturbation vector that when applied to safe representations, shifted the model towards a jailbreak state. Our results demonstrate that this causal intervention results in statistically significant jailbreak responses in a subset of prompts. Next, we probed how these perturbations propagate through the model's layers, testing whether the induced state change remains localized or cascades throughout the network. Our findings indicate that targeted perturbations induced distinct shifts in activations and model responses. Our approach paves the way for potential proactive defenses, shifting from traditional guardrail based methods to preemptive, model agnostic techniques that neutralize adversarial states at the representation level.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper explores the vulnerabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to adversarial manipulations, particularly through prompt injection attacks, which can result in harmful outputs by bypassing safety mechanisms. The authors hypothesize that LLM activations can be classified into semi-stable states identified as "safe" and "jailbroken". By using dimensionality reduction techniques, they reveal the latent subspaces corresponding to these states and derive a perturbation vector that can manipulate safe representations towards a jailbroken state. Their results show that these perturbations can induce statistically significant changes in model responses, raising questions about the propagation of these perturbations through the model’s architecture. The study proposes a shift from traditional defensive measures to proactive interventions at the representation level to counteract adversarial threats. ### Evaluation: **Novelty and Significance:** 1. **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach by linking LLM vulnerabilities to concepts of attractor dynamics from neuroscience, which is relatively unexplored in the context of AI security. The idea of probing latent subspaces and manipulating them is an innovative conceptual framework that can potentially open new pathways for research into model vulnerabilities. This fresh perspective contributes to the understanding of how adversarial states can be induced and offers a more nuanced approach to developing defenses. 2. **Methodology:** The application of dimensionality reduction and perturbation vector techniques to identify and shift between model states is an effective methodological choice. However, while the methods are scientifically sound, the broader implications of these perturbations could be better articulated. The study would benefit from deeper exploration of how the perturbations affect model behavior across more diverse and complex prompts, as well as long-term impacts of such manipulations. 3. **Significance:** The implications of this research are significant in the field of AI security. By demonstrating that adversarial states can be induced through representation-level alterations, this work shifts the framework of AI safety from reactive defenses to potentially more proactive and preemptive strategies. **Strengths:** - **Innovative Conceptual Integration:** Incorporating neuroscience attractor dynamics into LLM behavior offers a unique angle that may encourage cross-disciplinary approaches to AI safety. - **Empirical Validation:** The study provides empirical evidence of the effectiveness of their perturbation strategy, backed by statistical analysis, which enhances the credibility of their findings. **Weaknesses:** - **Generalizability:** The results need to be assessed on a wider array of LLM architectures and diverse datasets to ensure that the findings are not specific to the model being tested. - **Conflict Mitigation:** There’s a lack of discussion on the ethical implications of manipulating LLM states and potential unintended consequences, such as reinforcing biases or generating unforeseen harmful behavior in other contexts. **Overall Assessment:** While the paper introduces new concepts and methods, there are areas where further exploration is needed. Its novelty and proposed contributions to AI security make it a valuable addition to the literature, though the lack of broader applicability and the need for ethical considerations detract somewhat from its impact. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Theoretical Guarantees for High Order Trajectory Refinement in Generative Flows
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09069v1)
- **Authors**: Chengyue Gong, Xiaoyu Li, Yingyu Liang, Jiangxuan Long, Zhenmei Shi, Zhao Song, Yu Tian
- **Abstract**: Flow matching has emerged as a powerful framework for generative modeling, offering computational advantages over diffusion models by leveraging deterministic Ordinary Differential Equations (ODEs) instead of stochastic dynamics. While prior work established the worst case optimality of standard flow matching under Wasserstein distances, the theoretical guarantees for higher-order flow matching - which incorporates acceleration terms to refine sample trajectories - remain unexplored. In this paper, we bridge this gap by proving that higher-order flow matching preserves worst case optimality as a distribution estimator. We derive upper bounds on the estimation error for second-order flow matching, demonstrating that the convergence rates depend polynomially on the smoothness of the target distribution (quantified via Besov spaces) and key parameters of the ODE dynamics. Our analysis employs neural network approximations with carefully controlled depth, width, and sparsity to bound acceleration errors across both small and large time intervals, ultimately unifying these results into a general worst case optimal bound for all time steps.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "Theoretical Guarantees for High Order Trajectory Refinement in Generative Flows" addresses the theoretical underpinnings of higher-order flow matching techniques in the context of generative modeling. Flow matching is established as a computationally efficient method for generating samples through deterministic Ordinary Differential Equations (ODEs) as opposed to stochastic approaches like diffusion models. The authors provide theoretical guarantees showing that higher-order flow matching retains worst-case optimality as a distribution estimator. They derive upper error bounds for estimation in second-order flow matching, highlighting that the convergence rates are influenced by the smoothness of the target distribution (measured in Besov spaces) and the ODE parameters. By utilizing neural network approximations with controlled characteristics, they unify these results and present a general worst-case optimal bound applicable across various time steps. **Evaluation:** The paper introduces significant advancements in understanding the theoretical foundations of higher-order flow matching. The novelty lies in extending the existing framework of flow matching under Wasserstein distances to include higher-order methods that improve trajectory refinement. This represents an important step forward in optimizing generative modeling techniques, particularly as the field increasingly values computational efficiency without sacrificing performance. **Strengths:** 1. **Theoretical Contributions:** The paper fills a notable gap in the literature by providing rigorous theoretical guarantees for higher-order flow matching. 2. **Comprehensive Error Analysis:** The methodology involving upper bounds on estimation error and the focus on smoothness of the target distribution adds depth to the analysis. 3. **Unified Framework:** The authors integrate results across time steps, which adds considerable value and clarity to their findings. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Specificity to Second-Order Matching:** While the results are promising for second-order flow matching, the paper could benefit from discussing potential extensions or implications for higher-order methods beyond second-order. 2. **Applicability and Practical Implications:** The theoretical focus might make it less accessible for practitioners who are primarily concerned with the empirical performance of generative models. Overall, the paper represents a meaningful contribution to the theoretical landscape of generative flow models, pushing the boundaries of what is known about flow matching techniques. Its implications for future research and applications in generative modeling are substantial, albeit with some limitations regarding broader applicability. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Sequential Multi-Object Grasping with One Dexterous Hand
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09078v1)
- **Authors**: Sicheng He, Zeyu Shangguan, Kuanning Wang, Yongchong Gu, Yuqian Fu, Yanwei Fu, Daniel Seita
- **Abstract**: Sequentially grasping multiple objects with multi-fingered hands is common in daily life, where humans can fully leverage the dexterity of their hands to enclose multiple objects. However, the diversity of object geometries and the complex contact interactions required for high-DOF hands to grasp one object while enclosing another make sequential multi-object grasping challenging for robots. In this paper, we propose SeqMultiGrasp, a system for sequentially grasping objects with a four-fingered Allegro Hand. We focus on sequentially grasping two objects, ensuring that the hand fully encloses one object before lifting it and then grasps the second object without dropping the first. Our system first synthesizes single-object grasp candidates, where each grasp is constrained to use only a subset of the hand's links. These grasps are then validated in a physics simulator to ensure stability and feasibility. Next, we merge the validated single-object grasp poses to construct multi-object grasp configurations. For real-world deployment, we train a diffusion model conditioned on point clouds to propose grasp poses, followed by a heuristic-based execution strategy. We test our system using $8 \times 8$ object combinations in simulation and $6 \times 3$ object combinations in real. Our diffusion-based grasp model obtains an average success rate of 65.8% over 1600 simulation trials and 56.7% over 90 real-world trials, suggesting that it is a promising approach for sequential multi-object grasping with multi-fingered hands. Supplementary material is available on our project website: https://hesic73.github.io/SeqMultiGrasp.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper introduces SeqMultiGrasp, a system that enables a four-fingered Allegro Hand to sequentially grasp multiple objects. The authors note that while humans often grasp two or more objects simultaneously using their dexterity, doing this with robots presents challenges due to the varied geometries of objects and complex contact interactions. The SeqMultiGrasp system addresses these challenges by first identifying potential single-object grasp candidates, which are validated through a physics simulator for stability and feasibility. The next step involves merging these individual grasp configurations to enable the robot to sequentially grasp two objects while maintaining grip on the first during the process. The authors employ a diffusion model conditioned on point clouds to suggest feasible grasp poses, coupled with a heuristic for execution. The system is tested in both simulation (with $8 \times 8$ object combinations) and real-world scenarios (with $6 \times 3$ combinations), achieving success rates of 65.8% and 56.7%, respectively. This suggests the proposed approach could effectively aid in the robotic manipulation of multiple objects. --- ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance #### Strengths: 1. **Focus on Real-World Application**: The study tackles a real-world problem that is relevant to robotics, specifically the manipulation of multiple objects, which is critical for applications such as warehouse automation, manufacturing, and assistive technologies.    2. **Innovative Use of Diffusion Model**: The introduction of a diffusion model for proposing grasp poses is a relatively novel approach in the field of robot grasping tasks.
