This aspect highlights an intersection between advanced machine learning techniques and robotics. 3. **Methodological Rigor**: The paper demonstrates a well-structured methodology, from grasp candidate synthesis to validation and execution, providing clear steps for future researchers to replicate or build upon. 4. **Empirical Validation**: The authors validate their approach both in simulations and real-world trials, which is essential for assessing practical viability. #### Weaknesses: 1. **Limited Scope of Testing**: The paper primarily focuses on two-object grasping, which, while an important step, may be seen as a limitation. Scalability to grasping three or more objects, which is more typical in many applications, isn’t addressed. 2. **Success Rates on the Lower Side**: The reported success rates (65.8% in simulation and 56.7% in real-world testing) suggest that there may be considerable room for improvement. This could impact its applicability in real-world scenarios where higher reliability is crucial. 3. **Evaluation Metrics**: The metrics used to evaluate the grasping success could be considered somewhat simplistic. A more nuanced evaluation that considers different scenarios (e.g., varying object weights, sizes, and configurations) might provide a fuller picture of the system's capabilities. 4. **Potential Lack of Generalization**: The reliance on point cloud data and the specific robot hand may limit the generalization of the approach across different types of robots or grasping systems. ### Conclusion Overall, while SeqMultiGrasp presents an innovative approach to addressing sequential object grasping with a dexterous hand, the paper could benefit from extending the breadth of its applications beyond two-object scenarios and improving upon success rates. These factors affect its overall impact in the broader field of robotics, but it still contributes meaningfully to the ongoing research on multi-object grasping. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Self-Consistent Equation-guided Neural Networks for Censored Time-to-Event Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09097v1)
- **Authors**: Sehwan Kim, Rui Wang, Wenbin Lu
- **Abstract**: In survival analysis, estimating the conditional survival function given predictors is often of interest. There is a growing trend in the development of deep learning methods for analyzing censored time-to-event data, especially when dealing with high-dimensional predictors that are complexly interrelated. Many existing deep learning approaches for estimating the conditional survival functions extend the Cox regression models by replacing the linear function of predictor effects by a shallow feed-forward neural network while maintaining the proportional hazards assumption. Their implementation can be computationally intensive due to the use of the full dataset at each iteration because the use of batch data may distort the at-risk set of the partial likelihood function. To overcome these limitations, we propose a novel deep learning approach to non-parametric estimation of the conditional survival functions using the generative adversarial networks leveraging self-consistent equations. The proposed method is model-free and does not require any parametric assumptions on the structure of the conditional survival function. We establish the convergence rate of our proposed estimator of the conditional survival function. In addition, we evaluate the performance of the proposed method through simulation studies and demonstrate its application on a real-world dataset.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper presents an innovative approach for estimating conditional survival functions in survival analysis, particularly for censored time-to-event data. The authors critique existing deep learning methods that adapt traditional Cox regression models by integrating shallow neural networks, highlighting their computational inefficiencies when processing full datasets at each iteration. To address these issues, the authors propose a new framework utilizing generative adversarial networks (GANs) guided by self-consistent equations, which allows for non-parametric estimation without requiring specific parametric assumptions regarding the conditional survival function's structure. The paper includes theoretical developments, establishing the convergence rate of the proposed estimator, and demonstrates the effectiveness of the method through comprehensive simulation studies and applications to a real-world dataset. ### Critical Evaluation #### Novelty The paper showcases a significant departure from existing approaches by introducing a GAN-based method tailored for survival analysis, moving away from the Cox model's reliance on parametric structures. The utilization of self-consistent equations within a neural network framework enriches the potential for non-parametric estimation, which is a valuable advancement in the field given the limits of traditional methods. #### Significance The significance lies in its ability to handle high-dimensional predictors without strict parametric assumptions, addressing a prominent need in survival analysis. Given the complex nature of real-world data and the challenges posed by censorship, this approach could enhance predictive performance and interpretability in survival studies. #### Strengths 1. **Innovative Approach**: By leveraging GANs and self-consistent equations, the paper introduces a novel method that expands the toolkit available for survival analysis. 2. **Non-parametric Nature**: The model-free characteristic provides flexibility and applicability to diverse datasets. 3. **Theoretical Rigor**: The paper includes a mathematical framework that supports the validity of the proposed estimator's convergence. 4. **Comprehensive Evaluation**: The performance is tested through simulation studies and real-world data applications, providing empirical support for the method. #### Weaknesses 1. **Computational Complexity**: While the method aims to improve efficiency, the inherent nature of GANs can still lead to substantial computational demands, which may limit practical applicability, particularly in very large datasets. 2. **Comparative Analysis**: The paper could benefit from deeper comparisons with other state-of-the-art methods for survival analysis, facilitating a clearer understanding of its relative strengths and weaknesses. 3. **Implementation Challenges**: Depending on the familiarity of the target audience with GANs, there may be hurdles in understanding or implementing the proposed method effectively. #### Conclusion Overall, this paper represents a meaningful contribution to the field of survival analysis, particularly in its ability to provide a more flexible and potentially robust framework for handling complex, high-dimensional censored data. While there are areas for improvement, particularly in computational efficiency and comparative analysis, the innovation and rigor presented in this work stand out positively. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper's strong novelty and methodological development while indicating that practical concerns and a necessity for comparative analysis warrant further investigation.
- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 8/10

### VaxGuard: A Multi-Generator, Multi-Type, and Multi-Role Dataset for Detecting LLM-Generated Vaccine Misinformation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09103v1)
- **Authors**: Syed Talal Ahmad, Haohui Lu, Sidong Liu, Annie Lau, Amin Beheshti, Mark Dras, Usman Naseem
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have significantly improved text generation capabilities. However, they also present challenges, particularly in generating vaccine-related misinformation, which poses risks to public health. Despite research on human-authored misinformation, a notable gap remains in understanding how LLMs contribute to vaccine misinformation and how best to detect it. Existing benchmarks often overlook vaccine-specific misinformation and the diverse roles of misinformation spreaders. This paper introduces VaxGuard, a novel dataset designed to address these challenges. VaxGuard includes vaccine-related misinformation generated by multiple LLMs and provides a comprehensive framework for detecting misinformation across various roles. Our findings show that GPT-3.5 and GPT-4o consistently outperform other LLMs in detecting misinformation, especially when dealing with subtle or emotionally charged narratives. On the other hand, PHI3 and Mistral show lower performance, struggling with precision and recall in fear-driven contexts. Additionally, detection performance tends to decline as input text length increases, indicating the need for improved methods to handle larger content. These results highlight the importance of role-specific detection strategies and suggest that VaxGuard can serve as a key resource for improving the detection of LLM-generated vaccine misinformation.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "VaxGuard: A Multi-Generator, Multi-Type, and Multi-Role Dataset for Detecting LLM-Generated Vaccine Misinformation" addresses the growing concern surrounding vaccine-related misinformation generated by Large Language Models (LLMs). Recognizing the inadequacies in existing resources that overlook vaccine-specific misinformation, the authors develop VaxGuard, a novel dataset that encompasses misinformation created by various LLMs. This dataset aims to enhance the understanding and detection of misinformation across diverse roles. The findings indicate that GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 outperform other models in identifying misinformation, particularly in complex narratives, while models like PHI3 and Mistral perform sub-optimally in emotionally charged contexts. Additionally, the paper notes that detection efficacy decreases with longer text inputs, stressing the necessity for better strategies when dealing with larger content. Overall, VaxGuard is posited as a key resource for advancing the detection of LLM-generated vaccine misinformation. --- **Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty:** The introduction of the VaxGuard dataset is a notable contribution to the field of misinformation detection, specifically targeting the timely and critical area of vaccine-related content. By focusing on LLM-generated misinformation, the paper fills a significant gap in the literature that previously centered predominantly on human-authored data. The multi-generator and multi-role approach are particularly innovative, considering the complexity of misinformation dissemination. **Significance:** The significance of the study lies in its potential applications in public health and misinformation detection. Given the ongoing discourse surrounding vaccines and the role of social media in spreading misinformation, VaxGuard can provide valuable insights and tools for researchers and practitioners aiming to combat vaccine misinformation. Furthermore, by evaluating the performance of various LLMs, the study contributes to our understanding of their strengths and weaknesses, which is essential for researchers developing mitigation strategies. **Strengths:** 1. **Targeted Dataset Creation:** The dataset is specifically tailored for vaccine misinformation, which enhances its relevance and usability. 2. **Empirical Analysis:** The paper provides a solid empirical evaluation of LLMs, offering practical findings about their detection capabilities. 3. **Addressing a Critical Public Health Issue:** By focusing on vaccines, the paper tackles a matter of considerable social consequence, enhancing its importance in the field. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Evaluation Scope:** While the paper identifies performance discrepancies among LLMs, it could further explore the underlying reasons for these differences, particularly in emotional contexts. 2. **Generalizability Concerns:** The focus on vaccine misinformation may limit the applicability of the findings to other types of misinformation, which could be explored in future work. 3. **Lack of Real-World Testing:** The results are primarily based on synthetic data generated by LLMs; real-world validation would bolster claims about the practical applications of VaxGuard. **Score Justification:** Overall, VaxGuard presents a well-conceived initiative that addresses a critical issue in the intersection of technology and public health. The strengths of the paper, particularly in creating a targeted resource and providing empirical insights, outweigh its limitations. However, the lack of broader applications and real-world validation slightly detract from its overall impact. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### GRU: Mitigating the Trade-off between Unlearning and Retention for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09117v1)
- **Authors**: Yue Wang, Qizhou Wang, Feng Liu, Wei Huang, Yali Du, Xiaojiang Du, Bo Han
- **Abstract**: Large language model (LLM) unlearning has demonstrated its essential role in removing privacy and copyright-related responses, crucial for their legal and safe applications. However, the pursuit of complete unlearning often comes with substantial costs due to its compromises in their general functionality, leading to a notorious trade-off between unlearning and retention. In examining the update process for unlearning dynamically, we find gradients hold essential information for revealing this trade-off. In particular, we look at the varying relationship between retention performance and directional disparities between gradients during unlearning. It motivates the sculpting of an update mechanism derived from gradients from two sources, i.e., harmful for retention and useful for unlearning. Accordingly, we propose Gradient Rectified Unlearning (GRU), an enhanced unlearning framework controlling the updating gradients in a geometry-focused and optimization-driven manner such that their side impacts on other, unrelated responses can be minimized. Specifically, GRU derives a closed-form solution to project the unlearning gradient onto the orthogonal space of that gradient harmful for retention, ensuring minimal deviation from its original direction under the condition that overall performance is retained. Comprehensive experiments are conducted to demonstrate that GRU, as a general framework, is straightforward to implement and efficiently enhances a range of baseline methods through its adaptable and compatible characteristics. Additionally, experimental results show its broad effectiveness across a diverse set of benchmarks for LLM unlearning.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "GRU: Mitigating the Trade-off between Unlearning and Retention for Large Language Models" addresses the critical issue of unlearning in large language models (LLMs)—removing specific responses related to privacy or copyright while maintaining overall model functionality. The authors identify a significant compromise between effective unlearning and retention of knowledge, often leading to a detrimental impact on LLM performance. They propose a novel framework, Gradient Rectified Unlearning (GRU), which focuses on managing gradients during the unlearning process. GRU uniquely differentiates between harmful gradients and beneficial gradients for unlearning, projecting the unlearning gradient to minimize adverse effects on retention. Experiments demonstrate GRU's efficiency and compatibility across various baseline methods and benchmarks, marking it as a generalizable approach in LLM unlearning. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a noteworthy advancement in addressing a prominent challenge in the ethically responsible deployment of LLMs. The introduction of GRU offers a mathematically grounded solution that not only mitigates the unlearning-retention trade-off but also has broad applicability across existing LLM methodologies. The technical innovation of projecting gradients to orthogonal spaces is a clever approach and represents a significant step in improving LLM operations concerning privacy. However, certain limitations and areas for improvement are evident. The experimental results, while promising, may benefit from further exploration of edge cases where unlearning is particularly challenging, especially in nuanced scenarios involving complex datasets. Additionally, the paper could expand on the implications of this approach in practical applications, including details on computational efficiency and scalability in large-scale environments. Furthermore, while the proposed method is shown to enhance a range of baseline methods, the paper does not deeply engage with comparison metrics or provide a comprehensive assessment of these baselines, which could enrich the understanding of GRU's relative effectiveness. The authors should also discuss potential limitations of GRU in specific contexts and how it might handle situations where retention is critical, but the need for unlearning is acute. In summary, the work is an exciting contribution to the field of machine learning and privacy, presenting a fresh perspective on balancing unlearning and retention in LLMs. However, for it to fully resonate within the community, additional validation and practical implications would enhance its credibility and real-world applicability. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper's innovative approach and significant contribution to the field while also acknowledging the necessity for further elaboration and exploration of practical implications.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### AdvAD: Exploring Non-Parametric Diffusion for Imperceptible Adversarial Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09124v1)
- **Authors**: Jin Li, Ziqiang He, Anwei Luo, Jian-Fang Hu, Z. Jane Wang, Xiangui Kang
- **Abstract**: Imperceptible adversarial attacks aim to fool DNNs by adding imperceptible perturbation to the input data. Previous methods typically improve the imperceptibility of attacks by integrating common attack paradigms with specifically designed perception-based losses or the capabilities of generative models. In this paper, we propose Adversarial Attacks in Diffusion (AdvAD), a novel modeling framework distinct from existing attack paradigms. AdvAD innovatively conceptualizes attacking as a non-parametric diffusion process by theoretically exploring basic modeling approach rather than using the denoising or generation abilities of regular diffusion models requiring neural networks. At each step, much subtler yet effective adversarial guidance is crafted using only the attacked model without any additional network, which gradually leads the end of diffusion process from the original image to a desired imperceptible adversarial example. Grounded in a solid theoretical foundation of the proposed non-parametric diffusion process, AdvAD achieves high attack efficacy and imperceptibility with intrinsically lower overall perturbation strength. Additionally, an enhanced version AdvAD-X is proposed to evaluate the extreme of our novel framework under an ideal scenario. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed AdvAD and AdvAD-X. Compared with state-of-the-art imperceptible attacks, AdvAD achieves an average of 99.9$\%$ (+17.3$\%$) ASR with 1.34 (-0.97) $l_2$ distance, 49.74 (+4.76) PSNR and 0.9971 (+0.0043) SSIM against four prevalent DNNs with three different architectures on the ImageNet-compatible dataset. Code is available at https://github.com/XianguiKang/AdvAD.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces AdvAD, a novel framework for imperceptible adversarial attacks on deep neural networks (DNNs), conceptualizing the attacking process as a non-parametric diffusion model rather than relying on traditional attack methods. This approach allows for subtly guiding the adversarial perturbation without necessitating additional neural networks. The authors theoretically validate their model and demonstrate that AdvAD achieves superior attack success rates, imperceptibility, and lower perturbation strength compared to existing methods. Additionally, they propose an enhanced version, AdvAD-X, to explore the framework's potential under optimal conditions. Experiments show AdvAD outperforms state-of-the-art techniques on various metrics across multiple neural architectures, with code made publicly available. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of adversarial machine learning by proposing a new framework that departs from the heavily parametric models that have dominated previous research. The exploration of non-parametric diffusion for generating imperceptible adversarial examples is innovative and has the potential to influence future work by offering alternative methodologies for adversarial attack generation. The strong results reported, including impressive averages in attack success rate (ASR), $l_2$ distance, PSNR, and SSIM metrics, reinforce the practicality and effectiveness of the proposed method. One strength of this work lies in its theoretical foundation, which strengthens its claims to novelty and effectiveness. However, the reliance on diffusion processes could raise questions regarding the scalability of this technique and its applicability to varied contexts beyond the tested platforms. While the results are compelling, the focus on a specific set of architectures (DNNs from the ImageNet-compatible dataset) may limit its generalizability and impact across broader applications or different models. Additionally, the paper lacks a thorough comparative analysis of how existing perception-based losses and generative models specifically measure up against AdvAD, which would be vital for contextualizing its contributions within the existing literature. This comparative aspect is crucial since the strengths of AdvAD need to not only be claimed but also clearly demonstrated against direct competitors. Overall, the novelty of utilizing a non-parametric diffusion process for adversarial attacks is significant and can inspire subsequent research into alternative frameworks. The strong empirical results bolster its contribution, although some aspects of scalability and generalizability warrant further investigation. **Score: 8**  This score reflects a strong novelty and significant practical impact while acknowledging validity concerns surrounding scalability and comparative analysis with existing methods. The work is noteworthy, but full quantification of its implications across diverse contexts remains to be thoroughly explored in future studies.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Spiritus: An AI-Assisted Tool for Creating 2D Characters and Animations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09127v1)
- **Authors**: Qirui Sun, Yunyi Ni, Teli Yuan, Jingjing Zhang, Fan Yang, Zhihao Yao, Haipeng Mi
- **Abstract**: This research presents Spiritus, an AI-assisted creation tool designed to streamline 2D character animation creation while enhancing creative flexibility. By integrating natural language processing and diffusion models, users can efficiently transform natural language descriptions into personalized 2D characters and animations. The system employs automated segmentation, layered costume techniques, and dynamic mesh-skeleton binding solutions to support flexible adaptation of complex costumes and additional components. Spiritus further achieves real-time animation generation and efficient animation resource reuse between characters through the integration of BVH data and motion diffusion models. Experimental results demonstrate Spiritus's effectiveness in reducing technical barriers, enhancing creative freedom, and supporting resource universality. Future work will focus on optimizing user experience and further exploring the system's human-computer collaboration potential.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents Spiritus, an innovative AI-assisted tool aimed at simplifying the creation of 2D character animations. Leveraging advancements in natural language processing and diffusion models, Spiritus enables users to convert natural language descriptions into unique 2D characters and animations efficiently. Key features of the tool include automated segmentation, layered costume design, and dynamic mesh-skeleton binding, which facilitate flexible costume adaptation. Additionally, the system allows for real-time animation generation and the effective reuse of animation resources via BVH data and motion diffusion models. Experimental findings indicate that Spiritus successfully lowers technical barriers while promoting creative possibilities and resource adaptability. Future developments will concentrate on improving user experience and investigating the potential for enhanced collaboration between humans and the AI system. **Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:** Evaluating the novelty and significance of the work presented in this paper entails examining its methodological innovations, potential impact on the field of character design and animation, and how it compares to existing solutions. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Integration of Technologies**: Spiritus combines natural language processing with diffusion models in a novel way, enabling users to create characters by simply describing them. This is a significant step toward democratizing 2D animation creation for individuals without technical expertise. 2. **Automation and Flexibility**: The tool's automated segmentation and layered costume techniques demonstrate a clear advancement in workflow efficiency in animation creation, highlighting the combination of creativity with technical execution. 3. **Real-time Capabilities**: The incorporation of real-time animation generation and animation resource reuse sets Spiritus apart from many existing tools, which often provide less flexibility and necessitate longer production times. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Dependency on Natural Language Quality**: The effectiveness of the tool greatly relies on the clarity and precision of the natural language descriptions provided by the user. This dependence may alienate users who might struggle with articulating their ideas verbally, which could limit the tool's user base. 2. **Limited Scope of Application**: While the paper presents promising results in handling character animations, it does not extensively cover scenarios involving complex narratives or environments, which are intrinsic to animation development. 3. **Lack of Empirical Validation**: The experimental results, while promising, do not provide expansive metrics or comparisons with existing tools. A more comprehensive evaluation against established benchmarks could strengthen claims of effectiveness. **Potential Influence on the Field**: If successfully developed and refined, Spiritus could transform the landscape of 2D animation, making it accessible to non-experts and encouraging greater creative expression within the field. Its capabilities could empower indie developers and artists to produce high-quality animations without extensive technical knowledge. **Score**: 7 **Justification**: The score is reflective of Spiritus's innovative approach and its potential to significantly impact the animation field by lowering entry barriers and enhancing creative expression. However, limitations in user dependency on language quality and the scope of application somewhat temper its novelty and current utility. Future improvements addressing these weaknesses are critical to realizing the tool's full potential.

### Exo2Ego: Exocentric Knowledge Guided MLLM for Egocentric Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09143v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyu Zhang, Qiaohui Chu, Meng Liu, Yunxiao Wang, Bin Wen, Fan Yang, Tingting Gao, Di Zhang, Yaowei Wang, Liqiang Nie
- **Abstract**: AI personal assistants, deployed through robots or wearables, require embodied understanding to collaborate effectively with humans. Current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) primarily focus on third-person (exocentric) vision, overlooking the unique aspects of first-person (egocentric) videos. Additionally, high acquisition costs limit data size, impairing MLLM performance. To address these challenges, we propose learning the mapping between exocentric and egocentric domains, leveraging the extensive exocentric knowledge within existing MLLMs to enhance egocentric video understanding. To this end, we introduce Ego-ExoClip, a pre-training dataset comprising 1.1M synchronized ego-exo clip-text pairs derived from Ego-Exo4D. Our approach features a progressive training pipeline with three stages: Teacher Self-Preparation, Teacher-Student Guidance, and Student Self-Practice. Additionally, we propose an instruction-tuning data EgoIT from multiple sources to strengthen the model's instruction-following capabilities, along with the EgoBench benchmark comprising eight different tasks for thorough evaluation. Extensive experiments across diverse egocentric tasks reveal that existing MLLMs perform inadequately in egocentric video understanding, while our model significantly outperforms these leading models.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper, titled "Exo2Ego: Exocentric Knowledge Guided MLLM for Egocentric Video Understanding," addresses the limitations of current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) that heavily rely on exocentric perspectives for understanding video content, neglecting the distinct nuances of egocentric (first-person) videos. Recognizing the high costs associated with collecting egocentric data, the authors propose a novel framework that maps exocentric knowledge, derived from existing MLLMs, to enhance the comprehension of egocentric video data. Central to their approach is Ego-ExoClip, a dataset containing 1.1 million synchronized ego-exo clip-text pairs constructed from the Ego-Exo4D dataset. The training pipeline is structured into three progressive stages to optimize the learning process: Teacher Self-Preparation, Teacher-Student Guidance, and Student Self-Practice. The authors also introduce a novel instruction-tuning dataset, EgoIT, to improve the model's capability to follow instructions, alongside an evaluation benchmark, EgoBench, featuring eight tasks. Experimental results indicate that the proposed model significantly outperforms existing MLLMs across multiple egocentric tasks, highlighting the inadequacies of traditional MLLMs in this specific domain. **Critical Evaluation:** **Strengths:** 1. **Novel Approach**: The paper presents a novel way to leverage exocentric knowledge for improving egocentric video understanding, which is a relatively underexplored area within the field of AI and computer vision. 2. **Comprehensive Dataset**: The introduction of the Ego-ExoClip dataset and EgoIT instruction-tuning dataset is significant, as it could serve as a valuable resource for researchers working on similar problems. 3. **Structured Training Framework**: The three-stage progressive training pipeline is logically structured and demonstrates a clear methodology for enhancing model learning through an interaction between different modalities. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Generalization Concerns**: While the experiments show improved performance, it remains to be seen how well the model generalizes across diverse real-world scenarios, which could limit its practical applicability. 2. **Benchmarking Clarity**: The paper indicates the use of the EgoBench for evaluation, but further details on the nature of the tasks and the benchmarks' rigor would enhance the trustworthiness of their comparisons against existing models. 3. **High Dependency on Dataset Quality**: The performance gains may heavily rely on the quality and diversity of the Ego-ExoClip dataset, and if not diverse enough, the model may be susceptible to overfitting. **Potential Influence**: The novelty of bridging exocentric and egocentric video understanding can influence future research directions and methodologies in the field of AI-driven multimedia analysis. If the proposed methods can be verified to yield consistent improvements in practical applications, this could lead to advancements in human-robot interaction and AI personal assistants. **Score: 8**   The paper presents significant contributions in defining a framework that enhances egocentric video understanding by creatively capitalizing on existing exocentric knowledge. However, there are some concerns about the generalizability and the robustness of the proposed evaluation benchmarks that prevent it from being rated higher. Overall, it provides a meaningful advancement in the field, balanced by some caveats that warrant careful consideration in future research.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Generative Frame Sampler for Long Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09146v1)
- **Authors**: Linli Yao, Haoning Wu, Kun Ouyang, Yuanxing Zhang, Caiming Xiong, Bei Chen, Xu Sun, Junnan Li
- **Abstract**: Despite recent advances in Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs), effectively understanding long-form videos remains a significant challenge. Perceiving lengthy videos containing thousands of frames poses substantial computational burden. To mitigate this issue, this paper introduces Generative Frame Sampler (GenS), a plug-and-play module integrated with VideoLLMs to facilitate efficient lengthy video perception. Built upon a lightweight VideoLLM, GenS leverages its inherent vision-language capabilities to identify question-relevant frames. To facilitate effective retrieval, we construct GenS-Video-150K, a large-scale video instruction dataset with dense frame relevance annotations. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GenS consistently boosts the performance of various VideoLLMs, including open-source models (Qwen2-VL-7B, Aria-25B, VILA-40B, LLaVA-Video-7B/72B) and proprietary assistants (GPT-4o, Gemini). When equipped with GenS, open-source VideoLLMs achieve impressive state-of-the-art results on long-form video benchmarks: LLaVA-Video-72B reaches 66.8 (+4.3) on LongVideoBench and 77.0 (+2.7) on MLVU, while Aria obtains 39.2 on HourVideo surpassing the Gemini-1.5-pro by 1.9 points. We will release all datasets and models at https://generative-sampler.github.io.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "Generative Frame Sampler for Long Video Understanding" addresses the challenge of long-form video understanding within the framework of Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs). To enhance efficiency and effectiveness in processing lengthy videos, the authors propose a module called the Generative Frame Sampler (GenS). This module is designed to streamline the video perception process by identifying frames relevant to given queries, thereby reducing computational burdens. The authors also present the GenS-Video-150K dataset, enriched with dense frame relevance annotations, to facilitate training and evaluation. Experimental results indicate that GenS significantly improves the performance of multiple VideoLLMs, achieving state-of-the-art results on several benchmarks. The authors plan to publicly release their datasets and models. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation: **Novelty:** The introduction of the Generative Frame Sampler (GenS) represents a novel approach to addressing one of the notable challenges in the field of video understanding—namely, efficiently identifying relevant frames from long videos to optimize processing time and output accuracy. While prior works have tackled video comprehension, few have thoroughly focused on enhancing efficiency with plug-and-play modules within existing VideoLLMs. This innovative structure may inspire future research into similar frameworks for other forms of data, giving GenS a distinctive position in the academic dialogue. **Strengths:** 1. **Practical Impacts:** The integration of GenS within VideoLLMs demonstrates tangible performance improvements, marking a significant contribution that enhances usability in real-world applications. 2. **Dataset Contribution:** GenS-Video-150K offers a substantial resource for researchers, promoting further exploration and validation of video understanding techniques. 3. **Broad Applicability:** By applying GenS to both open-source and proprietary VideoLLMs, the authors validate the versatility of their solution across different models. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Exploration of Trade-Offs:** While the paper highlights improvements, it could elaborate more on the trade-offs involved with the integration of GenS, such as potential loss of detail or nuances in understanding video content. 2. **Dependence on Benchmarks:** The results heavily rely on specific benchmarks, which may not fully capture the generalizability of the approach across all types of long-form video. **Influence on the Field:** This paper is likely to drive further research into efficient methodologies for video processing, particularly concerning the integration of frame sampling techniques with language models. By addressing how to handle lengthy videos, the paper may open pathways to novel applications in fields such as video content summarization, retrieval, and even interactive applications. **Score: 8** This score reflects the promising advancements made within the paper, alongside its potential implications and practical contributions to the field. While it is a strong contribution, further investigation into trade-offs and applicability beyond existing benchmarks would elevate its impact.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### AdaptAI: A Personalized Solution to Sense Your Stress, Fix Your Mess, and Boost Productivity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09150v1)
- **Authors**: Rushiraj Gadhvi, Soham Petkar, Priyansh Desai, Shreyas Ramachandran, Siddharth Siddharth
- **Abstract**: Personalization is a critical yet often overlooked factor in boosting productivity and wellbeing in knowledge-intensive workplaces to better address individual preferences. Existing tools typically offer uniform guidance whether auto-generating email responses or prompting break reminders without accounting for individual behavioral patterns or stress triggers. We introduce AdaptAI, a multimodal AI solution combining egocentric vision and audio, heart and motion activities, and the agentic workflow of Large Language Models LLMs to deliver highly personalized productivity support and context-aware well-being interventions. AdaptAI not only automates peripheral tasks (e.g. drafting succinct document summaries, replying to emails etc.) but also continuously monitors the users unique physiological and situational indicators to dynamically tailor interventions such as micro-break suggestions or exercise prompts, at the exact point of need. In a preliminary study with 15 participants, AdaptAI demonstrated significant improvements in task throughput and user satisfaction by anticipating user stressors and streamlining daily workflows.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper describes AdaptAI, a multimodal AI system designed to enhance productivity and well-being by providing personalized support in knowledge-intensive workplaces. Unlike existing tools that offer generic guidance, AdaptAI takes into account individual user behaviors and stress triggers through the integration of various data sources, such as egocentric vision, audio inputs, and physical activity metrics. The system automates routine tasks—like drafting emails and summarizing documents—while also monitoring users' physiological signals to deliver context-specific interventions, such as suggesting breaks or exercises when needed. A preliminary study with 15 participants indicated that AdaptAI improved task throughput and user satisfaction by proactively addressing users' stressors and optimizing workflow. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance **Novelty:** The concept behind AdaptAI showcases notable innovation by blending multiple technological facets—egocentric vision, audio data, and physiological metrics—alongside the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to create a comprehensive productivity tool. This holistic approach to personalization, which adapts not only to user preferences but also to real-time physiological cues, distinguishes it from typical productivity software that lacks this level of customization.  However, the idea of using physiological indicators to inform productivity interventions is not entirely new, and there exist other platforms exploring similar concepts. Therefore, while AdaptAI does present a unique synthesis of methodologies, its groundbreaking nature is somewhat diluted by existing literature. **Significance:** AdaptAI's implications for workplace productivity and mental health are significant. By providing individualized support that dynamically adjusts based on user context, it has the potential to enhance not just achievement but also user well-being. This aligns with recent movements in workplace design towards enhancing employee mental health through technology. Nevertheless, the preliminary nature of the study with only 15 participants raises concerns about the generalizability and scalability of the findings. Without a more significant, diverse sample, the evidence supporting the effectiveness of AdaptAI remains limited. **Strengths:** - Strong integration of multimodal data sources for personalized feedback. - Clear potential for improving productivity and mental health in workplace settings. - Initial positive findings on user satisfaction. **Weaknesses:** - Small sample size in preliminary studies questions the robustness of results. - Limited novelty compared to other personalized productivity tools. - Lack of long-term evaluation on the effectiveness and user dependence on the system. In summary, while AdaptAI represents a promising advancement in designing personalized productivity tools, its impact could be improved through expanded trials and diverse participant engagement. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper's respectable contributions in merging existing technologies for enhanced workplace productivity but also highlights the need for more substantial evidence to validate its claims of effectiveness on a broader scale.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reangle-A-Video: 4D Video Generation as Video-to-Video Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09151v1)
- **Authors**: Hyeonho Jeong, Suhyeon Lee, Jong Chul Ye
- **Abstract**: We introduce Reangle-A-Video, a unified framework for generating synchronized multi-view videos from a single input video. Unlike mainstream approaches that train multi-view video diffusion models on large-scale 4D datasets, our method reframes the multi-view video generation task as video-to-videos translation, leveraging publicly available image and video diffusion priors. In essence, Reangle-A-Video operates in two stages. (1) Multi-View Motion Learning: An image-to-video diffusion transformer is synchronously fine-tuned in a self-supervised manner to distill view-invariant motion from a set of warped videos. (2) Multi-View Consistent Image-to-Images Translation: The first frame of the input video is warped and inpainted into various camera perspectives under an inference-time cross-view consistency guidance using DUSt3R, generating multi-view consistent starting images. Extensive experiments on static view transport and dynamic camera control show that Reangle-A-Video surpasses existing methods, establishing a new solution for multi-view video generation. We will publicly release our code and data. Project page: https://hyeonho99.github.io/reangle-a-video/
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Reangle-A-Video" presents a groundbreaking framework for generating synchronized multi-view videos from a single input video. This approach diverges from traditional multimodal models that rely on extensive 4D datasets, instead proposing a novel video-to-video translation paradigm that utilizes publicly accessible diffusion priors from images and videos. The proposed method is executed in two central stages:  1. **Multi-View Motion Learning** - A self-supervised, image-to-video diffusion transformer is fine-tuned to extract view-invariant motion from a collection of transformed videos. 2. **Multi-View Consistent Image-to-Images Translation** - The transformation process generates multi-view consistent images by warping and inpainting the initial frame into different camera perspectives guided by cross-view consistency.  The experiments validate that Reangle-A-Video outperforms existing methods, particularly in static view transport and dynamic camera control, marking a significant advancement in multi-view video generation. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty**: The paper introduces a significant conceptual shift by framing multi-view video generation as a translation task, rather than a mere generation task reliant on large datasets. It highlights the utilization of publicly available diffusion models as a foundational basis, suggesting a more resource-efficient approach. This reframing, coupled with the dual-stage processing, adds a layer of innovation that distinguishes it from existing methodologies in the field. **Significance**: The potential impacts of this research are profound. By establishing a framework that can operate with limited data, it democratizes access to high-quality multi-view video generation, which could have numerous applications in fields such as virtual reality, film production, and telepresence. Furthermore, the focus on cross-view consistency addresses common artifacts that appear in earlier methods, thus enhancing the visual quality of generated content. **Strengths**: - The authors provide a clear theoretical framework that is well-supported by experimental results. - The two-stage methodology is systematic and allows for performance improvements over prior art by refining existing techniques. - The plan to release code and data supports reproducibility and future research. **Weaknesses**: - While the approach shows promise, the effectiveness largely depends on the quality of the publicly available diffusion models. Their reliance could be a limiting factor in terms of applicability across diverse contexts or lower-quality inputs. - The experiments could benefit from a more extensive discussion or comparative benchmarks with a wider array of existing methods to strengthen claims of superiority. **Overall Impact**: The work is positioned to influence ongoing advancements in video generation technology significantly, encouraging further research into efficient models and data-poor environments.  **Score**: 8  This score reflects a solid contribution to the field while recognizing areas for further exploration and validation. The study effectively tackles important challenges in video generation but would benefit from expanded comparative analyses to fully establish its standing among contemporary approaches.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### FaVChat: Unlocking Fine-Grained Facail Video Understanding with Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09158v1)
- **Authors**: Fufangchen Zhao, Ming Li, Linrui Xu, Wenhao Jiang, Jian Gao, Danfeng Yan
- **Abstract**: Video-based multimodal large language models (VMLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable potential in cross-modal video understanding. However, their abilities in fine-grained face comprehension remain largely underexplored. Given its pivotal role in human-centric intelligence, developing VMLLMs for facial understanding holds a fundamental problem. To address this gap, we propose FaVChat, the first VMLLM specifically designed for fine-grained facial video understanding. To facilitate its training, we construct a large-scale facial video dataset comprising over 60k videos, with the majority annotated with 83 fine-grained facial attributes. These attributes are incorporated to enrich GPT-4o-generated captions, yielding 60k high-quality video-summary pairs and an additional 170k fine-grained question-answering (QA) pairs. To effectively capture rich facial clues, we propose a hybrid model architecture composed of a general visual encoder, a dedicated facial encoder, and a mixture-of-experts-enhanced adapter for adaptive fusion of multi-source visual features. To mitigate information loss during feature transformation, we extract multi-granularity representations from the facial encoder and integrate them into the subsequent LLM. This design enhances the model's ability to comprehend and respond to questions involving diverse levels of visual details. We employ a progressive training paradigm, transitioning from video summarization to a high-quality subset of video QA, gradually increasing task complexity to enhance the model's fine-grained visual perception. We conduct extensive zero-shot evaluation on a couple of public benchmarks, demonstrating that FaVChat consistently surpasses existing VMLLMs across multiple tasks.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces FaVChat, the first multimodal large language model (VMLLM) specifically designed for fine-grained facial video understanding. Recognizing the need for improved facial comprehension within video analyses, the authors construct a large-scale dataset of over 60,000 facial videos, annotated with 83 fine-grained facial attributes. This facilitates the model's training alongside generated captions, resulting in 60,000 high-quality video-summary pairs and 170,000 fine-grained question-answering pairs. A hybrid model architecture is proposed, integrating a general visual encoder, a dedicated facial encoder, and an improved mechanism for adaptive feature fusion. Through a progressive training paradigm, FaVChat transitions from summarizing videos to complex question-and-answer tasks, enhancing its capability to understand fine-grained visual details. Evaluations indicate that FaVChat outperforms existing VMLLMs across various benchmarks. **Evaluation:** **Novelty and Significance:** FaVChat presents a notable advancement in the intersection of multimodal understanding and fine-grained facial recognition within video content. The systematic construction of a specialized dataset and the introduction of tailored architectural components (like the dedicated facial encoder) demonstrate a strategic focus on enhancing model performance for facial analysis. Moreover, the progressive training approach appears innovative, allowing the model to incrementally develop its capabilities, which can be beneficial for similar future research. However, while the paper provides a compelling framework and claims substantial performance improvements over existing models, the true breadth of its impact may be limited by a few factors. First, the existing body of research around VMLLMs is relatively nascent, meaning that while FaVChat is a step forward, its long-term impact may depend on how it inspires future models and applications. Additionally, the specifics of the evaluation metrics and baseline comparisons used are not detailed in the abstract, leaving some ambiguity about the extent of the claimed advantages over previous approaches. Overall, FaVChat certainly contributes to the field, especially in a niche area that has previously been underexplored. Its focus on fine-grained understanding enhances the body of knowledge in multimodal processing of facial cues. **Score: 8** **Justification:** The score of 8 reflects the significant contributions made by the paper in addressing a vital gap in fine-grained facial video understanding. The innovative dataset creation, the architectural advancements, and the training paradigm strengthen its standing. However, due to a lack of detailed discussion on the evaluation process and the potential limitations inherent in the experimental setups, it stops short of a perfect score. The study's influence will be determined by its reception and replication in future research, but it undoubtedly marks an important advancement in the field of VMLLMs.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Token Weighting for Long-Range Language Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09202v1)
- **Authors**: Falko Helm, Nico Daheim, Iryna Gurevych
- **Abstract**: Many applications of large language models (LLMs) require long-context understanding, but models continue to struggle with such tasks. We hypothesize that conventional next-token prediction training could contribute to this, because each token is assigned equal weight. Yet, intuitively, the amount of context needed to predict the next token accurately varies greatly across different data. To reflect this, we propose various novel token-weighting schemes that assign different weights to each training token in the loss, thereby generalizing existing works. For this, we categorize token-weighting methods using a two-step framework which compares the confidences of a long-context and short-context model to score tokens. We evaluate all methods on multiple long-context understanding tasks and show that non-uniform loss weights are helpful to improve the long-context abilities of LLMs. Different short-context models can be used effectively for token scoring, including models that are much smaller than the long-context model that is trained. All in all, this work contributes to a better understanding of the trade-offs long-context language modeling faces and provides guidelines for model steering via loss-weighting based on empirical evidence. The code can be found on Github.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "Token Weighting for Long-Range Language Modeling" addresses the challenge that large language models (LLMs) face in understanding long contexts. The authors argue that traditional next-token prediction training treats each token with equal importance, which might not be optimal since different tokens require varying amounts of contextual information for accurate predictions. To improve this, they propose several novel token-weighting schemes that assign non-uniform weights to training tokens based on the contextual demands of predicting the next token. The authors present a two-step framework that utilizes the confidence scores from both long-context and short-context models to evaluate and weight tokens effectively. Their empirical evaluations on multiple long-context understanding tasks indicate that adapting loss weights through token-scoring improves the ability of LLMs to handle long-context inputs. This work not only enhances the understanding of long-context modeling but also provides actionable guidelines for loss-weighting strategies. --- **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty and Contribution:** The paper contributes a fresh perspective on long-context language modeling by suggesting that not all tokens should be treated equally during training. By introducing token-weighting schemes and a new framework for evaluating token significance based on short-context models' output, the authors provide a novel approach that diverges from conventional methodologies in LLM training. This shift towards non-uniform loss weighting represents a significant advancement in optimizing model performance for long contextual understanding. However, while the idea of weighting is not entirely new in machine learning, its specific application to LLMs, particularly in the context of long-range dependencies, holds potential. The authors successfully demonstrate empirical advantages, but the broader implications of these findings on a variety of architectures and datasets need further exploration. A more comprehensive analysis of the potential limitations and trade-offs inherent in varying weights for different models would strengthen the contributions. **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its practical applicability to a burgeoning area of research—utilizing LLMs for tasks demanding long memory contexts, such as document summarization or dialogue systems. Learning from smaller models to enhance larger models through token weighting could theoretically lower training costs and enhance scalability.  Nevertheless, the paper could benefit from deeper theoretical grounding. For instance, clarifying why certain short-context models are particularly effective for token scoring would bolster claims of robustness and may encourage further research into model selection for this task. Furthermore, real-world application scenarios or user studies that illustrate the practical improvements in LLMs when using these methods would enhance the paper's impact and relevance. **Strengths:** - Offers a novel approach to address long-context modeling issues. - Empirically demonstrates improvements in performance across various tasks. - Provides actionable insights for future research. **Weaknesses:** - Lacks broader theoretical context or broader applicability discussions regarding token weighting. - Could further investigate the implications of the findings across different model architectures and tasks. **Final Score: 7** **Rationale for the Score:** The score reflects a notable contribution to the field with substantial experimental validation and introduces a framework that could positively influence further research on long-context LLM training. However, it also acknowledges certain limitations in theoretical exploration and the scope of practical applicability, which could prevent it from being classified as a transformative work within the landscape of language modeling research.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Why LLMs Cannot Think and How to Fix It
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09211v1)
- **Authors**: Marius Jahrens, Thomas Martinetz
- **Abstract**: This paper elucidates that current state-of-the-art Large Language Models (LLMs) are fundamentally incapable of making decisions or developing "thoughts" within the feature space due to their architectural constraints. We establish a definition of "thought" that encompasses traditional understandings of that term and adapt it for application to LLMs. We demonstrate that the architectural design and language modeling training methodology of contemporary LLMs inherently preclude them from engaging in genuine thought processes. Our primary focus is on this theoretical realization rather than practical insights derived from experimental data. Finally, we propose solutions to enable thought processes within the feature space and discuss the broader implications of these architectural modifications.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Why LLMs Cannot Think and How to Fix It" argues that current Large Language Models (LLMs) lack the capacity for genuine thought due to inherent architectural limitations. By defining "thought" in the context of LLMs and comparing it to traditional understandings, the authors highlight that the design and training methodologies of state-of-the-art LLMs do not support complex decision-making or thoughtful process engagement. The focus of the paper is theoretical rather than experimental, aiming to articulate the conceptual barriers that prevent LLMs from thinking. Moreover, the authors propose potential architectural modifications to allow for thought processes in LLMs and explore the implications of these changes. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty:**   The paper introduces a novel perspective by linking the concept of "thought" to the architectural design of LLMs, which is often overlooked in discussions around AI capabilities. This theoretical framing provides a fresh understanding of the limitations of current models, distinguishing this work from typical technical papers that focus solely on performance metrics and applications. **Significance:**   The implications of this paper are significant for the field of AI and language modeling, as it challenges the assumptions that guide current research directions. By highlighting the architectural constraints of LLMs, the authors prompt a re-evaluation of both the development and the philosophical understanding of machine intelligence. The proposed solutions could inspire future research, making this paper relevant for ongoing discussions about improving AI systems and their cognitive capabilities. **Strengths:**   - The paper provides a theoretical critique of LLMs that stimulates deeper thinking regarding machine intelligence.   - It defines a working concept of "thought" tailored to LLMs, facilitating a better discussion on what constitutes cognitive processes in AI.   - The proposed modifications to allow thought processes could serve as a framework for subsequent research and development. **Weaknesses:**   - The paper lacks empirical validation; it is primarily theoretical, which may limit its immediate applicability.   - Without substantial experimental data to support the proposed solutions, the ideas presented may be seen as speculative or difficult to implement practically.   - The absence of comparisons with other frameworks or models limits the depth of discussion regarding potential design alternatives. **Influence on the Field:**   While this paper puts forth a compelling theoretical foundation, the practical impact will depend on how well the proposed architecture modifications can be implemented and validated in future research. The conceptual insights into the nature of "thought" in AI may influence subsequent explorations of LLM design and capabilities but require empirical substantiation to solidify their relevance. **Conclusion:**   Overall, the paper is a valuable contribution that pushes the boundaries of our understanding of LLMs. Its theoretical insights could lead to paradigm shifts in AI development, despite the current lack of empirical support.  **Score:** 7
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking Prompt-based Debiasing in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09219v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyi Yang, Runzhe Zhan, Derek F. Wong, Shu Yang, Junchao Wu, Lidia S. Chao
- **Abstract**: Investigating bias in large language models (LLMs) is crucial for developing trustworthy AI. While prompt-based through prompt engineering is common, its effectiveness relies on the assumption that models inherently understand biases. Our study systematically analyzed this assumption using the BBQ and StereoSet benchmarks on both open-source models as well as commercial GPT model. Experimental results indicate that prompt-based is often superficial; for instance, the Llama2-7B-Chat model misclassified over 90% of unbiased content as biased, despite achieving high accuracy in identifying bias issues on the BBQ dataset. Additionally, specific evaluation and question settings in bias benchmarks often lead LLMs to choose "evasive answers", disregarding the core of the question and the relevance of the response to the context. Moreover, the apparent success of previous methods may stem from flawed evaluation metrics. Our research highlights a potential "false prosperity" in prompt-base efforts and emphasizes the need to rethink bias metrics to ensure truly trustworthy AI.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper "Rethinking Prompt-based Debiasing in Large Language Models" addresses the critical issue of bias in large language models (LLMs) and examines the efficacy of prompt-based debiasing strategies. The authors challenge the prevailing assumption that LLMs inherently understand biases, based on experimental results using the BBQ and StereoSet benchmarks across various models, including open-source and commercial GPT variants. Their findings reveal significant limitations: the Llama2-7B-Chat model, for instance, misclassified over 90% of unbiased content as biased. The authors also highlight a tendency for models to provide "evasive answers" that sidestep the essence of questions posed in bias evaluations, potentially revealing the inadequacy of existing metrics. Ultimately, the study calls for a reevaluation of bias metrics to foster genuinely trustworthy AI, cautioning against what they term "false prosperity" in prompt-based debiasing. ### Critical Evaluation: **Novelty and Significance**: This paper presents a compelling critique of the current methodologies employed in prompt-based debiasing for LLMs, exposing underlying assumptions that have so far remained unchallenged. One of the main strengths lies in its systematic analysis that contrasts performance on different benchmarks, effectively demonstrating the inadequacies of current approaches. However, while the identification of biases within LLMs is crucial, the argument hinges on previously recognized issues—the discrepancy between model performance on curated benchmarks and real-world applications of these models. The concept of "false prosperity" is both thought-provoking and necessary in discussions about AI accountability and ethics but is not entirely novel, as warnings about over-reliance on flawed evaluation metrics have appeared in prior literature.  Moreover, the choice of benchmarks (BBQ and StereoSet) may not fully represent the spectrum of biases encountered in real-world deployment of AI, potentially limiting the broader applicability of the findings. The paper could benefit from a more detailed exploration of alternative debiasing methods that may yield different results, as well as recommendations for future research directions. **Overall Impact**: The paper does significantly contribute to the ongoing discourse around bias in LLMs, especially regarding the implications for ethical AI. By challenging established prompt-engineering methods, it invites researchers and practitioners to rethink their approaches, which may encourage a more rigorous standard in evaluations of bias. **Score**: 7. This score reflects the paper's valuable contributions to the discussion of bias in AI models and prompts necessary re-evaluation of current methodologies while acknowledging that some of its key ideas are not wholly novel and could be further developed.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### Active Learning Inspired ControlNet Guidance for Augmenting Semantic Segmentation Datasets
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09221v1)
- **Authors**: Hannah Kniesel, Pedro Hermosilla, Timo Ropinski
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in conditional image generation from diffusion models have shown great potential in achieving impressive image quality while preserving the constraints introduced by the user. In particular, ControlNet enables precise alignment between ground truth segmentation masks and the generated image content, allowing the enhancement of training datasets in segmentation tasks. This raises a key question: Can ControlNet additionally be guided to generate the most informative synthetic samples for a specific task? Inspired by active learning, where the most informative real-world samples are selected based on sample difficulty or model uncertainty, we propose the first approach to integrate active learning-based selection metrics into the backward diffusion process for sample generation. Specifically, we explore uncertainty, query by committee, and expected model change, which are commonly used in active learning, and demonstrate their application for guiding the sample generation process through gradient approximation. Our method is training-free, modifying only the backward diffusion process, allowing it to be used on any pretrained ControlNet. Using this process, we show that segmentation models trained with guided synthetic data outperform those trained on non-guided synthetic data. Our work underscores the need for advanced control mechanisms for diffusion-based models, which are not only aligned with image content but additionally downstream task performance, highlighting the true potential of synthetic data generation.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents a novel approach that integrates active learning principles into the process of synthetic sample generation using ControlNet within the context of enhancing semantic segmentation datasets. ControlNet is recognized for its ability to generate high-quality images that correspond closely to provided segmentation masks, thereby assisting in the training of segmentation models. The authors propose to further improve this process by employing active learning metrics—including uncertainty, query by committee, and expected model change—to inform the backward diffusion process used in generating samples. This integration aims to produce the most informative synthetic samples that can drive better model performance. Notably, the proposed method is training-free, allowing it to be applied to any pretrained ControlNet model. The results demonstrate that segmentation models trained on actively guided synthetic data significantly outperform those trained on non-guided synthetic data, indicating that the method effectively enhances the utility of synthetic data in machine learning tasks. --- **Evaluation:** The paper's novelty lies in its unique amalgamation of active learning techniques with a state-of-the-art image generation model, ControlNet. It addresses a significant gap in the literature by proposing a mechanism to enhance synthetic dataset generation guided by task-related metrics, rather than merely relying on generative capabilities. The integration of active learning principles into diffusion models to improve the quality of generated training data is an innovative step that could pave the way for more adaptive and effective machine learning methodologies. **Strengths:** - The paper establishes a clear connection between active learning and generative modeling, elucidating the potential benefits of leveraging both fields. - The proposed method is straightforward to implement, as it does not require retraining ControlNet but instead modifies the existing backward diffusion process. - Empirical results show a practical improvement in model performance, strengthening the paper's claims regarding the efficacy of the approach. **Weaknesses:** - While the experiments demonstrate performance improvement, the extent of practical applicability in real-world scenarios remains to be fully explored, as the dependency on synthetic data can vary widely across datasets and tasks. - The paper does not delve deeply into the limitations of the proposed approach or the potential scenarios in which it may perform suboptimally. - The active learning metrics chosen, though standard, could benefit from a broader exploration to validate the robustness of the method under varying conditions. Overall, the paper represents a commendable contribution to the field, arguing for a significant advancement in how synthetic data can be utilized. Its potential to influence future research and applications in both active learning and generative models cannot be understated. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper's considerable novelty and practical implications while acknowledging its limitations in scope and depth of exploration. It shows promise for future developments in enhancing data augmentation techniques through active learning, but unanswered questions about broader applicability and methodology should be addressed in future research.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### LREF: A Novel LLM-based Relevance Framework for E-commerce
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09223v1)
- **Authors**: Tian Tang, Zhixing Tian, Zhenyu Zhu, Chenyang Wang, Haiqing Hu, Guoyu Tang, Lin Liu, Sulong Xu
- **Abstract**: Query and product relevance prediction is a critical component for ensuring a smooth user experience in e-commerce search. Traditional studies mainly focus on BERT-based models to assess the semantic relevance between queries and products. However, the discriminative paradigm and limited knowledge capacity of these approaches restrict their ability to comprehend the relevance between queries and products fully. With the rapid advancement of Large Language Models (LLMs), recent research has begun to explore their application to industrial search systems, as LLMs provide extensive world knowledge and flexible optimization for reasoning processes. Nonetheless, directly leveraging LLMs for relevance prediction tasks introduces new challenges, including a high demand for data quality, the necessity for meticulous optimization of reasoning processes, and an optimistic bias that can result in over-recall. To overcome the above problems, this paper proposes a novel framework called the LLM-based RElevance Framework (LREF) aimed at enhancing e-commerce search relevance. The framework comprises three main stages: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with Data Selection, Multiple Chain of Thought (Multi-CoT) tuning, and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) for de-biasing. We evaluate the performance of the framework through a series of offline experiments on large-scale real-world datasets, as well as online A/B testing. The results indicate significant improvements in both offline and online metrics. Ultimately, the model was deployed in a well-known e-commerce application, yielding substantial commercial benefits.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents the LLM-based RElevance Framework (LREF), designed to enhance query and product relevance prediction in e-commerce search engines. Traditional approaches primarily utilize BERT-based models, which face limitations in their scope and understanding of the relevance between queries and products. The authors argue that advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) make them suitable for this task, due to their extensive knowledge and enhanced reasoning capabilities. However, the authors also highlight challenges when directly applying LLMs, including data quality requirements, the need for careful optimization processes, and a tendency for over-recall.  The proposed LREF framework features three main components: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with data selection, Multiple Chain of Thought (Multi-CoT) tuning, and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) for de-biasing. The framework's performance is validated through thorough offline experiments and online A/B testing, demonstrating significant improvements in both settings. Ultimately, the framework was successfully implemented in an e-commerce application, leading to noteworthy commercial advantages. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation **Novelty and Contribution**: The paper's novelty lies in its innovative approach to utilizing LLMs for e-commerce search relevance prediction. While previous studies primarily focused on BERT models, the transition to LLMs represents a significant shift in the understanding and application of natural language processing in this domain. Moreover, LREF addresses inherent challenges associated with LLMs, making it a practical contribution rather than just theoretical exploration. **Strengths**: 1. **Comprehensive Framework**: The LREF framework integrates multiple innovative techniques (SFT, Multi-CoT, DPO) that collectively enhance performance and address bias, showcasing depth in research methodology. 2. **Real-World Applications**: The successful deployment of the framework in a recognized e-commerce application indicates its practical relevance and potential impact on industry practices. 3. **Robust Evaluation**: The combination of offline experiments and online A/B testing provides a thorough validation of the framework's effectiveness across various metrics. **Weaknesses**:  1. **Potential Overfitting**: While the focus on data quality and fine-tuning is critical, there's a risk that careful optimization may lead to overfitting, particularly in specialized contexts where the model may not generalize well. 2. **Limited Exploration of Challenges**: The paper acknowledges the challenges in utilizing LLMs but could delve deeper into potential limitations and future areas for improvement, such as the trade-offs between computational resource demands and performance gains. 3. **Generality of Findings**: The results achieved may be context-specific to the e-commerce platform used for testing, which could temper the broader applicability of the findings across different industries or types of products. **Impact on the Field**: The introduction of LREF may catalyze more research into the adaptation of LLMs for real-world applications, particularly in areas of search relevance beyond e-commerce. This can lead to a paradigm shift in how search engines assess the relevance of queries and products, potentially influencing how models are developed and deployed in the future. ### Score: 8 **Rationale**: The paper presents a significant advancement in the use of LLMs for relevance prediction in e-commerce, marked by an innovative approach and thorough validation. The contribution is substantial, marking a departure from traditional BERT-only methodologies and extending the application of LLMs in industrial contexts. While there are areas for improvement, particularly concerning the methodological robustness and broader applicability of the findings, the overall impact of the work is strong. The paper can be seen as a catalyst for future research in applying advanced language models within practical settings, warranting a score of 8.
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### In-Context Defense in Computer Agents: An Empirical Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09241v1)
- **Authors**: Pei Yang, Hai Ci, Mike Zheng Shou
- **Abstract**: Computer agents powered by vision-language models (VLMs) have significantly advanced human-computer interaction, enabling users to perform complex tasks through natural language instructions. However, these agents are vulnerable to context deception attacks, an emerging threat where adversaries embed misleading content into the agent's operational environment, such as a pop-up window containing deceptive instructions. Existing defenses, such as instructing agents to ignore deceptive elements, have proven largely ineffective. As the first systematic study on protecting computer agents, we introduce textbf{in-context defense}, leveraging in-context learning and chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning to counter such attacks. Our approach involves augmenting the agent's context with a small set of carefully curated exemplars containing both malicious environments and corresponding defensive responses. These exemplars guide the agent to first perform explicit defensive reasoning before action planning, reducing susceptibility to deceptive attacks. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our method, reducing attack success rates by 91.2% on pop-up window attacks, 74.6% on average on environment injection attacks, while achieving 100% successful defenses against distracting advertisements. Our findings highlight that (1) defensive reasoning must precede action planning for optimal performance, and (2) a minimal number of exemplars (fewer than three) is sufficient to induce an agent's defensive behavior.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "In-Context Defense in Computer Agents: An Empirical Study" investigates the vulnerability of vision-language model (VLM)-powered computer agents to context deception attacks. These attacks involve adversaries inserting misleading instructions within an agent’s operational context.
