Experimental results indicate that RetSTA-7B surpasses existing large language models (LLMs) in bilingual standardization tasks, showcasing its effectiveness and generalizability. The model checkpoints are made publicly accessible. ### Critical Evaluation **Strengths:** 1. **Relevance:** The necessity for standardized clinical reports is significant given the importance of data integration in healthcare. This paper targets a critical gap in clinical documentation, which can swift transitions to better patient outcomes and interoperability among healthcare systems.    2. **Methodology:** The introduction of a bilingual standard terminology and the creation of models tailored to clinical scenarios suggest a thoughtful approach. RetSTA-7B’s capability to handle more complex clinical scenarios further demonstrates its methodical enhancement over its predecessor. 3. **Experimental Results:** The clear performance comparison against other LLMs provides validity to the authors' claims about RetSTA-7B's robustness and effectiveness. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Scope of Experimentation:** The extent to which RetSTA-7B-Zero and RetSTA-7B can generalize outside the training and testing datasets isn't fully addressed. Reliance on standardized datasets can limit adaptability to real-world variability in clinical data. 2. **Comprehensiveness:** While the authors mention improvements, the paper does not detail the evaluation metrics in-depth or provide scenarios illustrating the model's impact on decision-making in clinical contexts. 3. **Novelty:** While the approach is innovative in the biomedical NLP domain, the foundational technologies (LLMs) and the concept of standardization are not entirely new. The incremental advancement shown might not be perceived as groundbreaking in fields heavily invested in LLM developments. ### Overall Impact Considering the strengths in addressing a significant clinical need, the thoughtful methodological framework, and promising experimental results, the paper presents a substantial contribution. However, the concerns about the robustness of the models in real-world scenarios and the somewhat incremental nature of the advancement regarding existing technologies temper its impact. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Next-Generation Recommender Systems: A Benchmark for Personalized Recommendation Assistant with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09382v1)
- **Authors**: Jiani Huang, Shijie Wang, Liang-bo Ning, Wenqi Fan, Shuaiqiang Wang, Dawei Yin, Qing Li
- **Abstract**: Recommender systems (RecSys) are widely used across various modern digital platforms and have garnered significant attention. Traditional recommender systems usually focus only on fixed and simple recommendation scenarios, making it difficult to generalize to new and unseen recommendation tasks in an interactive paradigm. Recently, the advancement of large language models (LLMs) has revolutionized the foundational architecture of RecSys, driving their evolution into more intelligent and interactive personalized recommendation assistants. However, most existing studies rely on fixed task-specific prompt templates to generate recommendations and evaluate the performance of personalized assistants, which limits the comprehensive assessments of their capabilities. This is because commonly used datasets lack high-quality textual user queries that reflect real-world recommendation scenarios, making them unsuitable for evaluating LLM-based personalized recommendation assistants. To address this gap, we introduce RecBench+, a new dataset benchmark designed to access LLMs' ability to handle intricate user recommendation needs in the era of LLMs. RecBench+ encompasses a diverse set of queries that span both hard conditions and soft preferences, with varying difficulty levels. We evaluated commonly used LLMs on RecBench+ and uncovered below findings: 1) LLMs demonstrate preliminary abilities to act as recommendation assistants, 2) LLMs are better at handling queries with explicitly stated conditions, while facing challenges with queries that require reasoning or contain misleading information. Our dataset has been released at https://github.com/jiani-huang/RecBench.git.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Towards Next-Generation Recommender Systems: A Benchmark for Personalized Recommendation Assistant with LLMs" explores the limitations of traditional recommender systems (RecSys) in adapting to diverse, interactive recommendation tasks, especially with the advent of large language models (LLMs). It identifies a key gap in existing research due to the reliance on fixed prompt templates and the inadequacy of current datasets in simulating real-world user queries. To address this, the authors introduce RecBench+, a novel benchmark dataset that features a variety of user queries characterized by different levels of complexity—both in explicit conditions and reasoning challenges. The evaluation of several LLMs on the RecBench+ dataset revealed that while LLMs show promise as recommendation assistants, they struggle with tasks requiring deeper reasoning and context understanding. The dataset is publicly available for further research. **Critical Evaluation:** This paper presents a noteworthy contribution to the field of recommender systems by acknowledging the evolution of LLMs and their potential to transform how personalized recommendations are generated.  **Strengths:** 1. **Novel Dataset**: RecBench+ is a significant advancement, providing a tailored benchmark that addresses previous inadequacies in evaluating LLMs in recommendation contexts, which could foster further research and innovation. 2. **Focus on Real-World Scenarios**: By including a diverse range of queries that mimic user interactions, the paper enhances the applicability of research findings to practical situations, a crucial necessity for real-world systems. 3. **Empirical Evaluation**: The performance assessments of LLMs on this dataset offer insights into their current capabilities and limitations, thereby guiding future research trajectories. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Findings**: While the findings indicate promising preliminary abilities of LLMs, they reveal that significant challenges remain, particularly with complex reasoning tasks. The paper could benefit from further analysis of why these weaknesses exist and how to address them. 2. **Lack of Comparative Analysis**: The paper could enhance its impact by comparing LLMs' performance with traditional recommendation systems to provide context for the advancements being proposed. 3. **Limited Engagement with Practical Implications**: The discussion around the implications of these findings for industry practices is relatively scant, limiting the paper's direct applicability to practitioners. In considering these aspects, the paper demonstrates a solid foundational contribution to the field of recommender systems, showing both promise and areas for improvement in leveraging LLMs for personalized recommendations. The innovative dataset and the exploratory analysis of LLM capabilities position this work as a relevant step towards next-generation recommendation systems, but the gaps in reasoning capabilities and practical application limit its immediate impact. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper's significant advancements in dataset creation and initial LLM evaluation while acknowledging its limitations in addressing broader implications and complexities of user interactions in recommendation contexts.
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### ForAug: Recombining Foregrounds and Backgrounds to Improve Vision Transformer Training with Bias Mitigation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09399v1)
- **Authors**: Tobias Christian Nauen, Brian Moser, Federico Raue, Stanislav Frolov, Andreas Dengel
- **Abstract**: Transformers, particularly Vision Transformers (ViTs), have achieved state-of-the-art performance in large-scale image classification. However, they often require large amounts of data and can exhibit biases that limit their robustness and generalizability. This paper introduces ForAug, a novel data augmentation scheme that addresses these challenges and explicitly includes inductive biases, which commonly are part of the neural network architecture, into the training data. ForAug is constructed by using pretrained foundation models to separate and recombine foreground objects with different backgrounds, enabling fine-grained control over image composition during training. It thus increases the data diversity and effective number of training samples. We demonstrate that training on ForNet, the application of ForAug to ImageNet, significantly improves the accuracy of ViTs and other architectures by up to 4.5 percentage points (p.p.) on ImageNet and 7.3 p.p. on downstream tasks. Importantly, ForAug enables novel ways of analyzing model behavior and quantifying biases. Namely, we introduce metrics for background robustness, foreground focus, center bias, and size bias and show that training on ForNet substantially reduces these biases compared to training on ImageNet. In summary, ForAug provides a valuable tool for analyzing and mitigating biases, enabling the development of more robust and reliable computer vision models. Our code and dataset are publicly available at https://github.com/tobna/ForAug.
- **Summary**: **Concise Summary:** The paper "ForAug: Recombining Foregrounds and Backgrounds to Improve Vision Transformer Training with Bias Mitigation" presents ForAug, a novel data augmentation strategy aimed at enhancing the training of Vision Transformers (ViTs) by addressing issues of data scarcity and bias. By leveraging pretrained models to isolate and recombine foreground objects with various backgrounds, ForAug increases data diversity and the effective training sample size. The authors apply ForAug to the ImageNet dataset, resulting in notable accuracy improvements (up to 4.5 percentage points on ImageNet and 7.3 p.p. on downstream tasks). Additionally, they propose new metrics to assess biases in model performance, such as background robustness and center bias, demonstrating that ForAug mitigates these biases compared to conventional training on ImageNet. The authors make their code and dataset publicly available, which could facilitate further research in the field. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of the ForAug approach lies primarily in its distinctive methodology for data augmentation, specifically in the way it manipulates image composition by recombining foregrounds and backgrounds. This is a valuable contribution, considering biases in AI models drew increasing scrutiny, particularly in computer vision. The use of pretrained models for augmentation is also an innovative idea, as it combines transfer learning with data augmentation to address both the quantity and quality of training data. However, while the methodology appears robust, the paper would benefit from a more comprehensive discussion on the specific types of biases faced in ViTs and how ForAug compares to existing bias mitigation techniques beyond mere performance metrics. The metrics introduced for bias analysis are a significant asset; however, without extensive validation and comparison to other methods in terms of effectiveness, it is difficult to ascertain their full utility. Additionally, the results in terms of accuracy, while promising, might raise questions about generalizability to other datasets beyond ImageNet. The authors could strengthen their argument with more extensive experimentation across varied domains. In terms of significance, the work addresses pertinent issues within the AI and machine learning community, particularly regarding the performance and fairness of deep learning models. By providing a quantifiable method to analyze biases, ForAug lays the groundwork for future research and applications in bias mitigation, which is increasingly crucial. Overall, while the paper presents a commendable progression in the field of computer vision, incorporating novel augmentation techniques that tangentially relate to bias mitigation, it could improve by expanding on comparative analyses and validation.  **Score: 7**  The score reflects a strong contribution to the field, particularly in methodology and potential practical applications; however, the reliance on limited datasets and the need for broader validation prevent it from achieving a higher impact score.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diff-CL: A Novel Cross Pseudo-Supervision Method for Semi-supervised Medical Image Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09408v1)
- **Authors**: Xiuzhen Guo, Lianyuan Yu, Ji Shi, Na Lei, Hongxiao Wang
- **Abstract**: Semi-supervised learning utilizes insights from unlabeled data to improve model generalization, thereby reducing reliance on large labeled datasets. Most existing studies focus on limited samples and fail to capture the overall data distribution. We contend that combining distributional information with detailed information is crucial for achieving more robust and accurate segmentation results. On the one hand, with its robust generative capabilities, diffusion models (DM) learn data distribution effectively. However, it struggles with fine detail capture, leading to generated images with misleading details. Combining DM with convolutional neural networks (CNNs) enables the former to learn data distribution while the latter corrects fine details. While capturing complete high-frequency details by CNNs requires substantial computational resources and is susceptible to local noise. On the other hand, given that both labeled and unlabeled data come from the same distribution, we believe that regions in unlabeled data similar to overall class semantics to labeled data are likely to belong to the same class, while regions with minimal similarity are less likely to. This work introduces a semi-supervised medical image segmentation framework from the distribution perspective (Diff-CL). Firstly, we propose a cross-pseudo-supervision learning mechanism between diffusion and convolution segmentation networks. Secondly, we design a high-frequency mamba module to capture boundary and detail information globally. Finally, we apply contrastive learning for label propagation from labeled to unlabeled data. Our method achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance across three datasets, including left atrium, brain tumor, and NIH pancreas datasets.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "Diff-CL: A Novel Cross Pseudo-Supervision Method for Semi-supervised Medical Image Segmentation" addresses the challenges in semi-supervised learning for medical image segmentation by leveraging both generative models and convolutional networks. The authors highlight the limitations of existing approaches, particularly their inability to capture the overall data distribution effectively. They propose a framework that integrates diffusion models (DM) for learning data distribution with convolutional neural networks (CNN) for refining details, thus addressing the weaknesses of each method in isolation. Their method introduces a cross-pseudo-supervision mechanism, a high-frequency mamba module for capturing precise boundary information, and utilizes contrastive learning for effective label propagation. The proposed Diff-CL method demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on three medical imaging datasets, suggesting its effectiveness in improving segmentation tasks. ### Critical Evaluation: #### Novelty: The paper presents a significant advancement by combining the strengths of diffusion models and CNNs for semi-supervised medical image segmentation. The concept of cross-pseudo-supervision is innovative, as it addresses the need for robust detail capture in conjunction with effective distribution learning from unlabeled data. The implementation of a high-frequency module is also a novel approach that directly targets the prevalent issue of detail loss in generated images. #### Significance: The approach has a strong potential impact on the field of medical image segmentation, particularly in contexts where acquiring labeled data is expensive or infeasible. By effectively utilizing unlabeled data, this method could broaden the application of deep learning techniques in medicine, enabling more wide-reaching accessibility for various segmentation tasks. Achieving state-of-the-art results on multiple datasets further indicates the framework’s robustness and relevance. #### Strengths: 1. **Innovative Approach**: The integration of diffusion models with CNNs through cross-pseudo-supervision stands out as a creative solution to existing limitations in medical image segmentation. 2. **Strong Empirical Results**: The achievement of state-of-the-art performance provides compelling evidence of the method’s effectiveness. 3. **Potential for Practical Impact**: By reducing the dependence on labeled data, this framework might expedite the deployment of deep learning models in clinical settings. #### Weaknesses: 1. **Computational Load**: The reliance on CNNs for detailed capture might necessitate extensive computational resources, potentially limiting accessibility for some researchers or practitioners. 2. **Assumption of Class Similarity**: The underlying assumption that unlabeled and labeled data share a similar distribution may not hold in all contexts, possibly affecting generalizability. 3. **Comparative Analysis**: While the paper mentions achieving state-of-the-art results, a more comprehensive comparative analysis with existing methods would strengthen the contribution, especially highlighting the unique advantages of Diff-CL over alternatives. ### Overall Assessment: The paper's contributions are innovative and meaningful, with a significant potential to influence the semi-supervised learning landscape in medical image segmentation. However, limitations in computational efficiency and assumptions about data distribution may temper its universal applicability. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Alias-Free Latent Diffusion Models:Improving Fractional Shift Equivariance of Diffusion Latent Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09419v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Zhou, Zeqi Xiao, Shuai Yang, Xingang Pan
- **Abstract**: Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs) are known to have an unstable generation process, where even small perturbations or shifts in the input noise can lead to significantly different outputs. This hinders their applicability in applications requiring consistent results. In this work, we redesign LDMs to enhance consistency by making them shift-equivariant. While introducing anti-aliasing operations can partially improve shift-equivariance, significant aliasing and inconsistency persist due to the unique challenges in LDMs, including 1) aliasing amplification during VAE training and multiple U-Net inferences, and 2) self-attention modules that inherently lack shift-equivariance. To address these issues, we redesign the attention modules to be shift-equivariant and propose an equivariance loss that effectively suppresses the frequency bandwidth of the features in the continuous domain. The resulting alias-free LDM (AF-LDM) achieves strong shift-equivariance and is also robust to irregular warping. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AF-LDM produces significantly more consistent results than vanilla LDM across various applications, including video editing and image-to-image translation. Code is available at: https://github.com/SingleZombie/AFLDM
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents a redesign of Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs) to address issues of instability and inconsistency in generated outputs, particularly when subjected to perturbations in input noise, which hampers their reliability in applications. The authors tackle the prevalent aliasing and shift-equivariance problems by re-engineering the attention mechanisms and implementing an equivariance loss to minimize frequency bandwidth discrepancies in the latent features. Their approach, dubbed Alias-Free Latent Diffusion Model (AF-LDM), demonstrates notable improvement in shift-equivariance and exhibits robustness to irregular warping, leading to more consistent outputs in various contexts like video editing and image-to-image translation. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of this work lies in its specific focus on improving shift-equivariance in LDMs, a significant challenge acknowledged in the domain of generative models. The proposed shift-equivariant architecture and corresponding loss functions provide a fresh perspective on addressing genotype variability, which is a long-standing issue in the field.  One major strength of the paper is its comprehensive approach to dismantling the aliasing issues associated with both training and inference stages of LDMs. The redesign of self-attention modules to enhance equivariance is a noteworthy contribution, as self-attention is commonly regarded as a structural bottleneck in maintaining consistency across perturbed inputs. Moreover, the results validate the authors' claims, showing improvements over prior models, rendering the proposed AF-LDM a practical progression in the field. However, there are also certain weaknesses. The paper could benefit from a deeper exploration of the implications of the proposed methods on the computational complexities involved. The effectiveness of the anti-aliasing methods when scaling to larger datasets or higher dimensional data has not been fully addressed, which may limit its application in broader, more complex scenarios. Moreover, the results, while promising, could be further strengthened with comparisons to a wider range of existing models to clearly position the advancements offered by AF-LDM. In summary, despite some limitations regarding scalability and broader contextual application, the innovations presented in the paper exhibit considerable potential for advancing the reliability of LDMs and influence on generating consistent outputs across various applications. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CASTLE: Benchmarking Dataset for Static Code Analyzers and LLMs towards CWE Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09433v1)
- **Authors**: Richard A. Dubniczky, Krisztofer Zoltán Horvát, Tamás Bisztray, Mohamed Amine Ferrag, Lucas C. Cordeiro, Norbert Tihanyi
- **Abstract**: Identifying vulnerabilities in source code is crucial, especially in critical software components. Existing methods such as static analysis, dynamic analysis, formal verification, and recently Large Language Models are widely used to detect security flaws. This paper introduces CASTLE (CWE Automated Security Testing and Low-Level Evaluation), a benchmarking framework for evaluating the vulnerability detection capabilities of different methods. We assess 13 static analysis tools, 10 LLMs, and 2 formal verification tools using a hand-crafted dataset of 250 micro-benchmark programs covering 25 common CWEs. We propose the CASTLE Score, a novel evaluation metric to ensure fair comparison. Our results reveal key differences: ESBMC (a formal verification tool) minimizes false positives but struggles with vulnerabilities beyond model checking, such as weak cryptography or SQL injection. Static analyzers suffer from high false positives, increasing manual validation efforts for developers. LLMs perform exceptionally well in the CASTLE dataset when identifying vulnerabilities in small code snippets. However, their accuracy declines, and hallucinations increase as the code size grows. These results suggest that LLMs could play a pivotal role in future security solutions, particularly within code completion frameworks, where they can provide real-time guidance to prevent vulnerabilities. The dataset is accessible at https://github.com/CASTLE-Benchmark.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces CASTLE, a benchmarking framework for evaluating the abilities of various vulnerability detection tools in identifying common weaknesses as defined by the Common Weakness Enumeration (CWE). It assesses 13 static analysis tools, 10 large language models (LLMs), and 2 formal verification tools using a meticulously crafted dataset comprising 250 micro-benchmark programs that address 25 common CWEs. The authors propose the CASTLE Score, a new metric for fair comparison across methods. Findings indicate that while formal verification tools such as ESBMC effectively reduce false positives, they struggle with vulnerabilities not addressable by model checking. Static analysis tools tend to generate high false positive rates, necessitating increased validation efforts. Notably, LLMs excel at detecting vulnerabilities in smaller code snippets but exhibit diminishing accuracy with larger code sizes and an increase in hallucinations. The authors argue for the potential of LLMs to augment security measures in code completion tools, thereby mitigating vulnerabilities. The dataset is publicly available for access and utilization. **Evaluation:** The paper brings a noteworthy contribution to the field of software security, particularly in the context of evaluating and comparing contemporary vulnerability detection tools. Its key strengths include the introduction of a structured benchmarking framework (CASTLE) and the innovative CASTLE Score metric that provides a transparent comparative basis across varied methodologies. This represents a significant step in addressing the challenges of evaluating tool effectiveness in a sector characterized by heterogeneous approaches.  Furthermore, the comprehensive evaluation of various tools against a clear metric enhances transparency and sets a benchmark for future works in the domain. The findings, especially regarding the limitations of LLMs and static analyzers, are valuable for guiding the focus of future research and tool development. However, the paper does exhibit some limitations. It primarily focuses on a limited set of tools and CWEs, raising questions about the generalizability of its findings to a broader set of vulnerabilities and real-world programming scenarios. The methodology could benefit from a more detailed explanation of the benchmark's development, particularly regarding the selection of the CWEs covered and their relevance to current security practices. Additionally, while the paper highlights the strengths and weaknesses of the tools evaluated, it does not delve deeply into the practical implications of these findings for existing development environments or operational practices. Nevertheless, the novelty of combining LLM performance with traditional static and dynamic analysis methods within a unified framework represents a potential paradigm shift in the way security vulnerabilities are detected. Overall, the paper provides significant insights into the evolving landscape of vulnerability detection and the role of LLMs, promoting further exploration in this rapidly advancing field. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### PromptMap: An Alternative Interaction Style for AI-Based Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09436v1)
- **Authors**: Krzysztof Adamkiewicz, Paweł W. Woźniak, Julia Dominiak, Andrzej Romanowski, Jakob Karolus, Stanislav Frolov
- **Abstract**: Recent technological advances popularized the use of image generation among the general public. Crafting effective prompts can, however, be difficult for novice users. To tackle this challenge, we developed PromptMap, a new interaction style for text-to-image AI that allows users to freely explore a vast collection of synthetic prompts through a map-like view with semantic zoom. PromptMap groups images visually by their semantic similarity, allowing users to discover relevant examples. We evaluated PromptMap in a between-subject online study ($n=60$) and a qualitative within-subject study ($n=12$). We found that PromptMap supported users in crafting prompts by providing them with examples. We also demonstrated the feasibility of using LLMs to create vast example collections. Our work contributes a new interaction style that supports users unfamiliar with prompting in achieving a satisfactory image output.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "PromptMap: An Alternative Interaction Style for AI-Based Image Generation" addresses the difficulties novices face in crafting effective prompts for text-to-image AI systems. To resolve this issue, the authors introduce PromptMap, a novel interaction style that allows users to navigate a curated visual map of synthetic prompts organized by semantic similarity. This method enables users to discover relevant images that can inspire or guide their own prompt creation. The effectiveness of PromptMap was evaluated through two studies—a between-subject online study with 60 participants and a qualitative within-subject study involving 12 participants. Results indicate that PromptMap facilitates prompt crafting by providing accessible examples and shows the potential of leveraging large language models (LLMs) to build extensive collections of examples. The contribution is aimed primarily at aiding users with limited experience in prompting to attain satisfactory image outputs. --- **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty and Significance:** 1. **Originality:** The concept of PromptMap offers an innovative approach to interact with AI-based image generation systems. While the challenge of crafting prompts is well recognized, the implementation of a map-like view that groups prompts based on semantic similarity is relatively novel. It indicates a shift from traditional text input to a more visual exploration methodology that may enhance usability for novices. 2. **User-Centric Design:** By focusing on novice users and attempting to lower the entry barrier for engaging with AI tools, the authors target a crucial demographic that often faces hurdles in technology adoption. This focus on accessibility is a significant aspect, given the increasing popularity of AI systems. 3. **Evaluation Methodology:** The use of both quantitative and qualitative assessments adds depth to the evaluation of PromptMap. However, the sample size in the qualitative study (n=12) raises concerns about generalizability. While the larger online study (n=60) strengthens the findings, further large-scale studies are necessary to confirm the initial results. 4. **Feasibility of LLM Utilization:** The paper demonstrates how LLMs can be effectively utilized to generate diverse prompt examples. This is an important advancement as it suggests a scalable method for enriching the user experience, but details on the technical challenges or limitations of this approach are not adequately discussed. **Strengths:** - Introduces a user-friendly interaction style that addresses a specific pain point in AI usage. - Supports novice users, potentially broadening the user base for image generation technologies. - Tools and methodologies explored have applications beyond just image generation, extending into broader areas of AI-human interaction. **Weaknesses:** - Limited sample sizes in one of the studies may affect the robustness of the conclusions. - The exploration of technical limitations or challenges related to the proposed system and LLM integration could have been more comprehensive. - Potential biases in participant selection and the environments in which studies were conducted are not addressed. **Potential Impact:** The impact of PromptMap is likely to be significant in enhancing user interaction with AI image generators, promoting creativity, and enabling more individuals to leverage powerful AI tools in their work. However, further research and validation are essential to establish its effectiveness and scalability thoroughly. Given these considerations, I would assign a score of **Score: 7**. This score reflects encouraging novelty and the potential for significant impact, tempered by concerns regarding study robustness and a need for further exploration of the proposed techniques' broader implications.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### SuperCarver: Texture-Consistent 3D Geometry Super-Resolution for High-Fidelity Surface Detail Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09439v1)
- **Authors**: Qijian Zhang, Xiaozheng Jian, Xuan Zhang, Wenping Wang, Junhui Hou
- **Abstract**: Traditional production workflow of high-precision 3D mesh assets necessitates a cumbersome and laborious process of manual sculpting by specialized modelers. The recent years have witnessed remarkable advances in AI-empowered 3D content creation. However, although the latest state-of-the-arts are already capable of generating plausible structures and intricate appearances from images or text prompts, the actual mesh surfaces are typically over-smoothing and lack geometric details. This paper introduces SuperCarver, a 3D geometry super-resolution framework particularly tailored for adding texture-consistent surface details to given coarse meshes. Technically, we start by rendering the original textured mesh into the image domain from multiple viewpoints. To achieve geometric detail generation, we develop a deterministic prior-guided normal diffusion model fine-tuned on a carefully curated dataset of paired low-poly and high-poly normal renderings. To optimize mesh structures from potentially imperfect normal map predictions, we design a simple yet effective noise-resistant inverse rendering scheme based on distance field deformation. Extensive experiments show that SuperCarver generates realistic and expressive surface details as depicted by specific texture appearances, making it a powerful tool for automatically upgrading massive outdated low-quality assets and shortening the iteration cycle of high-quality mesh production in practical applications.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "SuperCarver: Texture-Consistent 3D Geometry Super-Resolution for High-Fidelity Surface Detail Generation" addresses the challenges of generating high-quality 3D meshes, which have traditionally required manual craftsmanship. The authors introduce SuperCarver, a framework designed to enhance coarse 3D meshes by adding texture-consistent surface details. SuperCarver employs a deterministic prior-guided normal diffusion model trained on paired low-poly and high-poly normal rendering datasets to generate geometric details. To manage potential inaccuracies from normal map predictions, the authors implement a noise-resistant inverse rendering scheme based on distance field deformation. The experimental results show that SuperCarver can effectively create realistic surface details, thus facilitating the upgrade of outdated 3D assets and accelerating the mesh production cycle. **Evaluation:** ### Novelty and Significance: The paper presents a significant advancement in the 3D modeling domain by addressing a common pain point— the transition from low-quality 3D models to high-fidelity ones while maintaining texture consistency. The fusion of normal diffusion models with inverse rendering techniques is an innovative approach that combines established methodologies in new ways to meet practical demands in content creation. **Strengths:** 1. **Addressing Real-World Issues:** SuperCarver targets a well-known bottleneck in 3D modeling, making it relevant to both industry and academia. 2. **Technical Innovation:** The integration of a prior-guided diffusion model tailored for the specific needs of normal mapping is a fresh contribution that pushes boundaries in texture and geometry realism. 3. **Practical Applications:** The ability to upgrade existing assets efficiently is a strong selling point for game developers, animators, and 3D artists, enhancing the paper's potential impact. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Evaluation Metrics:** While the results appear promising, the paper lacks a thorough comparison with existing methods in terms of quantitative metrics. Without this, the claims of superiority remain somewhat anecdotal. 2. **Dataset Limitations:** The effectiveness of the model may rely heavily on the curated dataset, raising questions about its generalizability to diverse, real-world datasets. 3. **Complexity and Usability:** For practitioners, the integration of the proposed framework into existing pipelines may require significant adaptation, potentially limiting immediate applicability. ### Conclusion: SuperCarver significantly contributes to the field of 3D content creation by providing an effective method for enhancing low-quality meshes. Its innovative techniques, targeting a relevant problem, can potentially streamline workflows in the industry. However, the lack of comprehensive performance validation and potential usability challenges temper its impact.  Given these considerations, I assign a score of **7**. This reflects a solid contribution driven by innovative techniques and practical relevance, but also acknowledges the need for further empirical evidence and better user integration strategies. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sparse Autoencoder as a Zero-Shot Classifier for Concept Erasing in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09446v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihua Tian, Sirun Nan, Ming Xu, Shengfang Zhai, Wenjie Qu, Jian Liu, Kui Ren, Ruoxi Jia, Jiaheng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models have achieved remarkable progress in generating high-quality images but also raise people's concerns about generating harmful or misleading content. While extensive approaches have been proposed to erase unwanted concepts without requiring retraining from scratch, they inadvertently degrade performance on normal generation tasks. In this work, we propose Interpret then Deactivate (ItD), a novel framework to enable precise concept removal in T2I diffusion models while preserving overall performance. ItD first employs a sparse autoencoder (SAE) to interpret each concept as a combination of multiple features. By permanently deactivating the specific features associated with target concepts, we repurpose SAE as a zero-shot classifier that identifies whether the input prompt includes target concepts, allowing selective concept erasure in diffusion models. Moreover, we demonstrate that ItD can be easily extended to erase multiple concepts without requiring further training. Comprehensive experiments across celebrity identities, artistic styles, and explicit content demonstrate ItD's effectiveness in eliminating targeted concepts without interfering with normal concept generation. Additionally, ItD is also robust against adversarial prompts designed to circumvent content filters. Code is available at: https://github.com/NANSirun/Interpret-then-deactivate.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper proposes a novel framework called "Interpret then Deactivate" (ItD) to address the challenges associated with harmful or misleading content generation in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. ItD utilizes a sparse autoencoder (SAE) to interpret concepts as combinations of features and facilitates concept removal by deactivating features associated with targeted concepts. This approach allows ItD to function as a zero-shot classifier that can identify the presence of these concepts in input prompts, enabling selective erasure without the need for retraining the diffusion models. The framework shows effectiveness in removing specific concepts—such as celebrity identities, artistic styles, and explicit content—while maintaining normal generation performance and robustness against adversarial prompts. The authors also highlight that their method supports multiple concept erasure seamlessly. **Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:** 1. **Originality**: The application of a sparse autoencoder as a zero-shot classifier for concept removal in diffusion models is a creative and novel avenue. By combining interpretability with targeted deactivation, the researchers introduce a new methodology that hasn't been commonly explored in T2I contexts. 2. **Significance**: The issue of generating harmful or misleading content is crucial in contemporary AI, especially regarding the ethical implications of T2I models. By intricately addressing this problem without affecting the models' performance, the paper has significant implications for safe AI deployment. Additionally, the method’s ability to handle multiple concepts without retraining could ease the deployment of T2I systems in sensitive applications. 3. **Methodological Rigor**: The paper outlines a clear methodology supported by experimental validation. The experiments involving various concepts and the demonstration of effectiveness against adversarial prompts bolster its claims. However, the extent of the experiments and the diversity of targeted concepts would ideally be broader to enhance reliability. 4. **Potential Impact**: If successfully integrated into T2I systems, this methodology could reshape how developers and researchers approach content moderation in generative models. However, it remains essential to assess its real-world usability and performance across more complex, nuanced concepts. 5. **Limitations**: While the paper presents a promising solution, it lightly touches upon potential trade-offs or edge cases where performance might still face challenges. Greater discussion on these points might strengthen the robustness of the findings presented. Given these considerations, I would assign the paper a score of **8**. This score reflects a balance between its strong novel contributions and significance in addressing a relevant and pressing issue in the field of AI, while acknowledging some limitations in the depth of experimentation and potential real-world applications. The authors have made a commendable advance toward making T2I models safer, which could have a lasting influence in AI ethics and content generation.  **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Explicit Learning and the LLM in Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09454v1)
- **Authors**: Malik Marmonier, Rachel Bawden, Benoît Sagot
- **Abstract**: This study explores the capacity of large language models (LLMs) for explicit learning, a process involving the assimilation of metalinguistic explanations to carry out language tasks. Using constructed languages generated by cryptographic means as controlled test environments, we designed experiments to assess an LLM's ability to explicitly learn and apply grammar rules. Our results demonstrate that while LLMs possess a measurable capacity for explicit learning, this ability diminishes as the complexity of the linguistic phenomena at hand increases. Supervised fine-tuning on chains of thought significantly enhances LLM performance but struggles to generalize to typologically novel or more complex linguistic features. These findings point to the need for more diverse training sets and alternative fine-tuning strategies to further improve explicit learning by LLMs.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "Explicit Learning and the LLM in Machine Translation" investigates how large language models (LLMs) can engage in explicit learning—integrating metalinguistic explanations to perform language-related tasks. Utilizing constructed languages created through cryptographic methods, the researchers conducted experiments measuring the LLM's capability to learn and implement grammar rules. The findings indicate that LLMs do indeed have a measurable capacity for explicit learning; however, this capacity tends to decrease as the complexity of the linguistic phenomena increases. Moreover, while supervised fine-tuning that employs reasoning students (chains of thought) improves LLM performance, there are challenges in generalizing to novel linguistic structures. The study suggests a need for more varied training datasets and different fine-tuning methods to enhance the explicit learning capabilities of LLMs. ### Critical Evaluation: #### Novelty: The paper examines explicit learning in LLMs through a unique methodology using constructed languages, representing a novel approach within the field of machine translation. The choice of controlled environments allows for clearer insights into the limits of LLM capabilities, which is commendable. However, the concept of learning language rules using LLMs is not entirely new. Existing literature touches upon similar themes, including the understanding of grammar and the role of structured training data. This somewhat dilutes the novelty aspect of the paper, even if the specific experimental design is original. #### Significance: The study highlights critical aspects of LLMs in machine translation, especially the implications of explicit learning and its limitations. By demonstrating the varying capacity of LLMs to handle complexity, the paper provides valuable insights that could influence future research directions—particularly regarding the necessity for diverse training datasets and alternative strategies for fine-tuning. This could indeed shape future explorations into improving LLM performances on language-based tasks; however, the results also reinforce existing knowledge on LLMs, which might limit its groundbreaking impact. #### Strengths: - Original experimentation with constructed languages offers a clear perspective on LLM capabilities. - The study effectively balances empirical findings with theoretical implications, paving the way for subsequent research into training methods. - Offers valuable insights into the limitations of LLMs in handling complex linguistic features. #### Weaknesses: - The foundational concepts regarding language rules and models are well-trodden territory, limiting the groundbreaking nature of the findings. - The challenges in generalization could be better contextualized within the current landscape of machine learning, possibly undermining the perceived urgency for more diverse training sets. - Absence of thorough comparisons with existing LLM capabilities or performance metrics makes it harder to assess the paper's practical implications. ### Conclusion: While the study presents an interesting exploration of explicit learning within LLMs, it operates within a familiar domain and does not significantly break new ground. Its practicality in guiding future research is notable, but overall, the contributions feel incremental rather than revolutionary. **Score: 7**  This score reflects both the paper's innovative approach and its relevance to ongoing research while acknowledging that further elaboration and contextualization within the existing body of work could strengthen its impact.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Automatic Association of Quality Requirements and Quantifiable Metrics for Cloud Security Certification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09460v1)
- **Authors**: John Bianchi, Shuya Dong, Luca Petrillo, Marinella Petrocchi
- **Abstract**: The European Cybersecurity Certification Scheme for Cloud Services (EUCS) is one of the first cybersecurity schemes in Europe, defined by the European Union Agency for Cybersecurity (ENISA). It aims to encourage cloud providers to strengthen their cybersecurity policies in order to receive an official seal of approval from European authorities. EUCS defines a set of security requirements that the cloud provider must meet, in whole or in part, in order to achieve the security certification. The requirements are written in natural language and cover every aspect of security in the cloud environment, from logging access to protecting the system with anti-malware tools to training staff. Operationally, each requirement is associated with one or more evaluable metrics. For example, a requirement to monitor access attempts to a service will have associated metrics that take into account the number of accesses, the number of access attempts, who is accessing, and what resources are being used. Partners in the European project Medina, which ended in October 2023, defined 163 metrics and manually mapped them to 70 EUCS requirements. Manual mapping is intuitively a long and costly process in terms of human resources. This paper proposes an approach based on Sentence Transformers to automatically associate requirements and metrics. In terms of correctness of associations, the proposed method achieves a Normalized Discounted Cumulative Gain of 0.640, improving a previous experiment by 0.146 points.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper focuses on the European Cybersecurity Certification Scheme for Cloud Services (EUCS), aimed at improving cybersecurity practices among cloud providers in Europe. The EUCS outlines various security requirements that must be met for certification, which are expressed in natural language and encompass a range of security aspects. Each requirement is linked to one or more quantifiable metrics that facilitate assessment. The recent project Medina documented 163 metrics correlated with 70 EUCS requirements, but the established manual mapping process is time-consuming and labor-intensive. This paper presents a novel method utilizing Sentence Transformers to automate the mapping of requirements to metrics. The proposed approach demonstrates enhanced efficiency, achieving a Normalized Discounted Cumulative Gain (NDCG) score of 0.640, which shows a significant improvement over earlier manual methods. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty**: The paper contributes a fresh approach to an existing industry challenge by automating the association between qualitative security requirements and quantitative metrics using advanced natural language processing (NLP) techniques. The novelty stems from the applicability of Sentence Transformers within a relatively underexplored domain—cloud security certification. **Strengths**: 1. **Practical Relevance**: The work is directly beneficial for cloud service providers and regulatory bodies by potentially streamlining the certification process, which is vital for enhancing cybersecurity. 2. **Performance Improvements**: The substantial improvement in the NDCG score suggests that the proposed automation can significantly reduce the human resource burden and increase consistency in mapping. **Weaknesses**: 1. **Depth of Validation**: While the NDCG score is promising, the paper may lack thorough validation across diverse datasets or real-world scenarios that could demonstrate robustness and generalizability. 2. **Detailing Limitations**: The impact of false associations or edge cases where the NLP model may not perform optimally seems to be underexplored. A discussion on potential pitfalls would strengthen the paper's argument. 3. **Contextual Integration**: More clarity is needed on how this automated mapping fits into the broader EUCS framework and its practical implementation within organizations. **Potential Influence**: On the one hand, the paper holds significant potential to reshape the practices surrounding cybersecurity certification through automation. On the other, its current scope might limit widespread adoption, as various stakeholders will require reassurance regarding accuracy and reliability. Based on these observations, I assign a score of **7**. This score reflects the paper's innovative use of NLP techniques in a salient area of cybersecurity, but acknowledges areas for improvement in validation and contextual application that could enhance its overall impact. Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### DAMM-Diffusion: Learning Divergence-Aware Multi-Modal Diffusion Model for Nanoparticles Distribution Prediction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09491v1)
- **Authors**: Junjie Zhou, Shouju Wang, Yuxia Tang, Qi Zhu, Daoqiang Zhang, Wei Shao
- **Abstract**: The prediction of nanoparticles (NPs) distribution is crucial for the diagnosis and treatment of tumors. Recent studies indicate that the heterogeneity of tumor microenvironment (TME) highly affects the distribution of NPs across tumors. Hence, it has become a research hotspot to generate the NPs distribution by the aid of multi-modal TME components. However, the distribution divergence among multi-modal TME components may cause side effects i.e., the best uni-modal model may outperform the joint generative model. To address the above issues, we propose a \textbf{D}ivergence-\textbf{A}ware \textbf{M}ulti-\textbf{M}odal \textbf{Diffusion} model (i.e., \textbf{DAMM-Diffusion}) to adaptively generate the prediction results from uni-modal and multi-modal branches in a unified network. In detail, the uni-modal branch is composed of the U-Net architecture while the multi-modal branch extends it by introducing two novel fusion modules i.e., Multi-Modal Fusion Module (MMFM) and Uncertainty-Aware Fusion Module (UAFM). Specifically, the MMFM is proposed to fuse features from multiple modalities, while the UAFM module is introduced to learn the uncertainty map for cross-attention computation. Following the individual prediction results from each branch, the Divergence-Aware Multi-Modal Predictor (DAMMP) module is proposed to assess the consistency of multi-modal data with the uncertainty map, which determines whether the final prediction results come from multi-modal or uni-modal predictions. We predict the NPs distribution given the TME components of tumor vessels and cell nuclei, and the experimental results show that DAMM-Diffusion can generate the distribution of NPs with higher accuracy than the comparing methods. Additional results on the multi-modal brain image synthesis task further validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents DAMM-Diffusion, a novel approach for predicting the distribution of nanoparticles (NPs) in tumor environments, emphasizing the impact of tumor microenvironment (TME) heterogeneity. It highlights that existing methods may produce suboptimal predictions due to divergence among multi-modal TME data. To address this, the authors introduce a unified network that consists of a univariate branch using a U-Net architecture and a multi-modal branch enhanced with two modules: the Multi-Modal Fusion Module (MMFM) for feature integration and the Uncertainty-Aware Fusion Module (UAFM) for managing uncertainty in cross-attention tasks. The Divergence-Aware Multi-Modal Predictor (DAMMP) integrates predictions from both branches and assesses their consistency based on the uncertainty map, ultimately aiming to yield more accurate NPs distribution predictions. Experimental results demonstrate the superiority of DAMM-Diffusion over traditional methods, supplemented by additional verification on a multi-modal brain image synthesis task. ### Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance The paper presents a clear advancement in the application of multi-modal machine learning techniques to a biologically significant problem—nanoparticle distribution in tumor environments. The novelty lies in the development of DAMM-Diffusion, which effectively combines uni-modal and multi-modal predictions while addressing the uncertainty inherent in the distribution data.  **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Methodology:** The introduction of MMFM and UAFM to handle multi-modal data and uncertainty is a noteworthy contribution, as this approach enhances the model's robustness in clinical applications. 2. **Biological Relevance:** The application of this model to a significant biomedical problem—tumor treatment—enhances trade-offs between technical advancements and real-world implications, emphasizing the model’s relevance. 3. **Validated Performance:** The experimental results presented indicate a high degree of accuracy in predicting NPs distribution compared to other state-of-the-art methods, providing strong evidence of the model’s effectiveness. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity and Interpretability:** The complexity introduced by the three modules (MMFM, UAFM, and DAMMP) may complicate interpretability, which is vital in clinical settings. The authors could provide more clarity on how the individual modules contribute to the overall model performance. 2. **Comparison Benchmarking:** While the performance results are promising, a broader range of comparative studies against a wider variety of models would strengthen claims of superiority. A limited comparison might overlook other advanced techniques that could perform similarly or better. 3. **Generalizability:** The study's findings need further validation across a broader range of tumor types and varying conditions to ensure the model’s generalizability in clinical applications. In summary, DAMM-Diffusion represents a significant, albeit complex, advancement in predicting nanoparticle distributions that could greatly influence tumor diagnostics and treatment strategies. Its innovative approach to modeling and practical relevance is commendable, but further validation and simplification could enhance language clarity and applicability. **Score: 8**  This score reflects a strong contribution to the field due to its methodological innovation and relevance to biomedical applications, tempered by considerations regarding complexity, interpretability, and the need for broader comparative analysis.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ReMA: Learning to Meta-think for LLMs with Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09501v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyu Wan, Yunxiang Li, Yan Song, Hanjing Wang, Linyi Yang, Mark Schmidt, Jun Wang, Weinan Zhang, Shuyue Hu, Ying Wen
- **Abstract**: Recent research on Reasoning of Large Language Models (LLMs) has sought to further enhance their performance by integrating meta-thinking -- enabling models to monitor, evaluate, and control their reasoning processes for more adaptive and effective problem-solving. However, current single-agent work lacks a specialized design for acquiring meta-thinking, resulting in low efficacy. To address this challenge, we introduce Reinforced Meta-thinking Agents (ReMA), a novel framework that leverages Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL) to elicit meta-thinking behaviors, encouraging LLMs to think about thinking. ReMA decouples the reasoning process into two hierarchical agents: a high-level meta-thinking agent responsible for generating strategic oversight and plans, and a low-level reasoning agent for detailed executions. Through iterative reinforcement learning with aligned objectives, these agents explore and learn collaboration, leading to improved generalization and robustness. Experimental results demonstrate that ReMA outperforms single-agent RL baselines on complex reasoning tasks, including competitive-level mathematical benchmarks and LLM-as-a-Judge benchmarks. Comprehensive ablation studies further illustrate the evolving dynamics of each distinct agent, providing valuable insights into how the meta-thinking reasoning process enhances the reasoning capabilities of LLMs.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents the Reinforced Meta-thinking Agents (ReMA) framework, designed to enhance the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) through Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL). Current methods focusing on meta-thinking in LLMs face shortcomings due to a lack of tailored designs, resulting in weaker performance. ReMA addresses this issue by introducing a two-tier agent structure: a high-level meta-thinking agent that strategizes and oversees reasoning processes, and a low-level reasoning agent that executes details. The collaboration between these agents, optimized through reinforcement learning, leads to better generalization and robustness in complex reasoning tasks. Experimental validation shows that ReMA significantly outperforms existing single-agent reinforcement learning methods, especially in mathematical tasks and evaluative benchmarks. Additional ablation studies shed light on the dynamics and contributions of each agent to the overall reasoning process. ### Critical Evaluation and Score The novelty of the ReMA framework lies in its innovative use of Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning to promote meta-thinking in LLMs, creating a distinctive hierarchical agent structure. This dual-agent approach effectively separates strategic planning from execution, indicating a sophisticated understanding of the interplay between different cognitive processes in artificial intelligence — a topic that is garnering increasing attention as LLMs are expected to tackle more complex reasoning tasks. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Framework**: The distinct use of MARL to develop meta-thinking capabilities represents a novel contribution to the field. By addressing the limitations of existing single-agent systems, the authors have opened a pathway for more robust reasoning processes. 2. **Performance Metrics**: The experimental results indicating superior performance over single-agent RL baselines are compelling. The use of competitive-level benchmarks and thorough evaluations strengthens the reliability of the findings. 3. **Insights from Ablation Studies**: The comprehensive investigation of each agent's impact contributes to a deeper understanding of how meta-thinking enhances reasoning, providing valuable insights for future research. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Scalability and Complexity**: While the proposed two-agent framework is innovative, practical implementation may be complex and resource-intensive. Questions remain regarding the scalability of this model to larger systems or different task domains. 2. **Limited Scope of Evaluation**: The experimental focus primarily on mathematical and judgment tasks may limit the generalizability of the findings. Future work should explore broader applications and diverse types of reasoning processes to confirm the robustness of ReMA across various contexts. 3. **Dependency on Reinforcement Learning Dynamics**: The reliance on MARL introduces challenges related to training stability and convergence, which may pose barriers for effective deployment in real-world applications. In conclusion, while the paper has notable strengths and contributes meaningfully to the field of LLMs and meta-thinking, the identified limitations affect its overall impact and practical applicability. The proposed framework is an important step forward, likely to influence future research directions in the realm of intelligent systems. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### CM-Diff: A Single Generative Network for Bidirectional Cross-Modality Translation Diffusion Model Between Infrared and Visible Images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09514v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Hu, Chenqiang Gao, Shurui Liu, Junjie Guo, Fang Chen, Fangcen Liu
- **Abstract**: The image translation method represents a crucial approach for mitigating information deficiencies in the infrared and visible modalities, while also facilitating the enhancement of modality-specific datasets. However, existing methods for infrared and visible image translation either achieve unidirectional modality translation or rely on cycle consistency for bidirectional modality translation, which may result in suboptimal performance. In this work, we present the cross-modality translation diffusion model (CM-Diff) for simultaneously modeling data distributions in both the infrared and visible modalities. We address this challenge by combining translation direction labels for guidance during training with cross-modality feature control. Specifically, we view the establishment of the mapping relationship between the two modalities as the process of learning data distributions and understanding modality differences, achieved through a novel Bidirectional Diffusion Training (BDT) strategy. Additionally, we propose a Statistical Constraint Inference (SCI) strategy to ensure the generated image closely adheres to the data distribution of the target modality. Experimental results demonstrate the superiority of our CM-Diff over state-of-the-art methods, highlighting its potential for generating dual-modality datasets.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces CM-Diff, a novel generative network designed for bidirectional cross-modality translation between infrared and visible images. Current methods face limitations in either being unidirectional or requiring cycle consistency, leading to inefficiencies. CM-Diff addresses these issues by modeling the data distributions of both modalities simultaneously using a Bidirectional Diffusion Training (BDT) strategy, which incorporates translation direction labels for enhanced training control. To further improve the fidelity of generated images, the authors introduce a Statistical Constraint Inference (SCI) approach, ensuring that generated images closely match the target modality's data distribution. Experimental results indicate that CM-Diff outperforms established methods, suggesting its effectiveness in generating dual-modality datasets. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a noteworthy advancement in the field of cross-modality image translation by addressing key challenges that current methods encounter. The novelty lies in the combination of bidirectional learning with diffusion modeling, which could potentially work more efficiently than existing approaches. The introduction of the Bidirectional Diffusion Training (BDT) strategy is particularly significant, as it provides a structured training methodology that appears to enhance performance in generating high-quality images across modalities. However, there are some weaknesses that limit the impact of the paper. Firstly, the abstract, while promising, lacks specific details regarding the experimental setup, such as the datasets used, evaluation metrics, and comparisons to state-of-the-art methods. Without rigorous benchmarking and comprehensive statistical analysis, it is difficult to accurately assess the claims of superiority over existing techniques. Furthermore, the proposed Statistical Constraint Inference (SCI) strategy is not elaborated upon sufficiently, which raises questions about its implementation and effectiveness in various contexts. Overall, while CM-Diff introduces valuable concepts and exhibits potential for substantial advancements in cross-modality translation, the lack of detailed experimental validation and clarity in methodology prevents it from achieving a top-tier rating. Therefore, considering both its innovative aspects and the outlined limitations, I assign a score of **Score: 7**. This reflects its meaningful contribution to the field but acknowledges the need for further substantiating the claims made within the paper.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Search-R1: Training LLMs to Reason and Leverage Search Engines with Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09516v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Jin, Hansi Zeng, Zhenrui Yue, Dong Wang, Hamed Zamani, Jiawei Han
- **Abstract**: Efficiently acquiring external knowledge and up-to-date information is essential for effective reasoning and text generation in large language models (LLMs). Retrieval augmentation and tool-use training approaches where a search engine is treated as a tool lack complex multi-turn retrieval flexibility or require large-scale supervised data. Prompting advanced LLMs with reasoning capabilities during inference to use search engines is not optimal, since the LLM does not learn how to optimally interact with the search engine. This paper introduces Search-R1, an extension of the DeepSeek-R1 model where the LLM learns -- solely through reinforcement learning (RL) -- to autonomously generate (multiple) search queries during step-by-step reasoning with real-time retrieval. Search-R1 optimizes LLM rollouts with multi-turn search interactions, leveraging retrieved token masking for stable RL training and a simple outcome-based reward function. Experiments on seven question-answering datasets show that Search-R1 improves performance by 26% (Qwen2.5-7B), 21% (Qwen2.5-3B), and 10% (LLaMA3.2-3B) over SOTA baselines. This paper further provides empirical insights into RL optimization methods, LLM choices, and response length dynamics in retrieval-augmented reasoning. The code and model checkpoints are available at https://github.com/PeterGriffinJin/Search-R1.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces Search-R1, a novel framework designed to enhance the reasoning and information retrieval capabilities of large language models (LLMs) through reinforcement learning (RL). Unlike previous approaches that either lacked flexibility in multi-turn retrieval or needed extensive supervised data, Search-R1 allows LLMs to autonomously generate multiple search queries while performing step-by-step reasoning, optimizing interactions with search engines. It utilizes a simple outcome-based reward system and retrieved token masking for stable RL training. Experimental results demonstrate that Search-R1 significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines across seven question-answering datasets, achieving performance improvements of 26% (Qwen2.5-7B), 21% (Qwen2.5-3B), and 10% (LLaMA3.2-3B). The paper also discusses insights into RL optimization methods, model selection, and response length dynamics. The code and model checkpoints are publicly available. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of Search-R1 lies in its focus on using RL for learning to interact with search engines during reasoning processes, which contrasts with traditional supervised training or simple prompting techniques. This new approach allows for real-time retrieval and multi-turn interactions that were previously difficult to achieve. The architecture builds upon existing methods, indicating it offers a fresh perspective on integrating RL with LLMs for enhanced performance. The significant improvement in performance metrics over state-of-the-art baselines is a strong point in favor of the paper, demonstrating not only the effectiveness of the proposed method but also its practicality for real-world applications. Furthermore, the exploration of RL optimization techniques and how different LLMs behave under this framework adds to our understanding of these models' dynamics. However, some weaknesses can be noted. The reliance on RL may raise concerns regarding the computational efficiency and scalability of training, especially when compared to traditional supervised learning models which have benefitted from vast amounts of labeled data. Moreover, the paper's primary focus on quantitative results might overlook qualitative analysis, such as the types of reasoning improvements or the nature of errors that might persist even with enhanced retrieval capabilities. Additionally, while the claims of performance improvements are backed by results across several datasets, the paper could benefit from more diverse benchmarking to illustrate generalizability across different contexts and domains. The long-term impacts of using such an integrated RL approach for continuous learning in production environments are yet to be explored. In summary, Search-R1 presents a significant advancement in the way LLMs leverage search engines for reasoning tasks, with notable performance gains. Its innovative method and positive results mark it as an important contribution to the field, albeit with some concerns regarding the practical implementation and broader applicability. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Large Language Models for Multi-Facility Location Mechanism Design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09533v1)
- **Authors**: Nguyen Thach, Fei Liu, Houyu Zhou, Hau Chan
- **Abstract**: Designing strategyproof mechanisms for multi-facility location that optimize social costs based on agent preferences had been challenging due to the extensive domain knowledge required and poor worst-case guarantees. Recently, deep learning models have been proposed as alternatives. However, these models require some domain knowledge and extensive hyperparameter tuning as well as lacking interpretability, which is crucial in practice when transparency of the learned mechanisms is mandatory. In this paper, we introduce a novel approach, named LLMMech, that addresses these limitations by incorporating large language models (LLMs) into an evolutionary framework for generating interpretable, hyperparameter-free, empirically strategyproof, and nearly optimal mechanisms. Our experimental results, evaluated on various problem settings where the social cost is arbitrarily weighted across agents and the agent preferences may not be uniformly distributed, demonstrate that the LLM-generated mechanisms generally outperform existing handcrafted baselines and deep learning models. Furthermore, the mechanisms exhibit impressive generalizability to out-of-distribution agent preferences and to larger instances with more agents.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents LLMMech, an innovative approach that employs large language models (LLMs) within an evolutionary framework to design mechanisms for multi-facility location problems. It seeks to overcome the challenges faced by previous methods, which often required extensive domain knowledge and hyperparameter tuning and lacked interpretability. The authors argue that LLMMech produces mechanisms that are interpretable, require no hyperparameter tuning, and are empirically strategyproof while minimizing social costs based on agent preferences. Experimental results indicate that the mechanisms generated by LLMMech outperform traditional handcrafted methods and other deep learning approaches, especially in scenarios with diverse and uneven agent preferences and in larger instances. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper demonstrates notable strengths, particularly in addressing key issues within mechanism design—namely, strategyproofness, interpretability, and adaptability to diverse agent preferences. The incorporation of LLMs into the evolutionary generation of mechanisms is a valuable innovation which broadens the scope and potential applications of mechanism design in multi-facility location problems. Performance metrics highlighted in the experimental evaluations suggest a meaningful enhancement over existing methodologies, indicating practical applicability in real-world scenarios where transparency is crucial. However, there are several weaknesses to consider. The extent to which the proposed approach can generalize beyond the tested scenarios remains uncertain, and the reliance on LLMs—despite their abilities—does not inherently eliminate interpretability challenges surrounding deep learning models. Further, the paper could benefit from a deeper theoretical discussion of the mechanisms' properties and how the empirical strategyproofness is sustained across varying settings. The experimental validation, while promising, would have strengthened the paper if conducted in a more extensive range of conditions to solidify claims regarding generalizability and performance. Overall, the paper showcases a significant step forward by combining advanced machine learning techniques with established economic principles, making it a valuable contribution to the field of mechanism design. However, it would benefit from more robust theoretical backing and a broader set of empirical tests to solidify its claims. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating Visual Explanations of Attention Maps for Transformer-based Medical Imaging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09535v1)
- **Authors**: Minjae Chung, Jong Bum Won, Ganghyun Kim, Yujin Kim, Utku Ozbulak
- **Abstract**: Although Vision Transformers (ViTs) have recently demonstrated superior performance in medical imaging problems, they face explainability issues similar to previous architectures such as convolutional neural networks. Recent research efforts suggest that attention maps, which are part of decision-making process of ViTs can potentially address the explainability issue by identifying regions influencing predictions, especially in models pretrained with self-supervised learning. In this work, we compare the visual explanations of attention maps to other commonly used methods for medical imaging problems. To do so, we employ four distinct medical imaging datasets that involve the identification of (1) colonic polyps, (2) breast tumors, (3) esophageal inflammation, and (4) bone fractures and hardware implants. Through large-scale experiments on the aforementioned datasets using various supervised and self-supervised pretrained ViTs, we find that although attention maps show promise under certain conditions and generally surpass GradCAM in explainability, they are outperformed by transformer-specific interpretability methods. Our findings indicate that the efficacy of attention maps as a method of interpretability is context-dependent and may be limited as they do not consistently provide the comprehensive insights required for robust medical decision-making.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Evaluating Visual Explanations of Attention Maps for Transformer-based Medical Imaging" investigates the explainability of Vision Transformers (ViTs) in medical imaging, focusing on attention maps and their role in enhancing interpretability. The authors compare attention maps against traditional methods, particularly GradCAM, across four medical imaging tasks: identifying colonic polyps, breast tumors, esophageal inflammation, and bone fractures. Through extensive experimentation with both supervised and self-supervised models, they conclude that while attention maps offer some advantages in certain contexts and perform better than GradCAM, they fall short compared to transformer-specific interpretability methods. The results indicate that the effectiveness of attention maps is context-dependent and does not always provide the robust insights necessary for critical medical decisions. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation **Novelty and Significance:**  1. **Novelty:** The exploration of attention maps in ViTs for medical imaging is a relevant and evolving topic within the realm of explainable artificial intelligence (XAI). While prior research has delved into the explainability of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) using GradCAM, this paper extends those ideas into the domain of ViTs, which have been less scrutinized for their interpretability. The comparison with transformer-specific interpretability methods adds a unique angle to the research landscape.     2. **Significance:** The significance lies in its potential contribution to improving medical decision-making processes where understanding model predictions is crucial. By evidencing the limitations of attention maps and contrasting them with other methods, it provides valuable insights that could aid practitioners in selecting the appropriate explanation methods for ViTs.
